# CIPRMS System Audit — 2026-07-16

Full read-only audit of the codebase (`cirl.js`, all `views/`, `routes/`, `middleware/`, `services/`). No code was changed while producing this report. Methodology: mechanical inventory of every backend route, every view file, and every frontend `fetch()` call, cross-referenced for mismatches; four parallel deep-dive investigations (calendar/notifications/activity-log, reports/OCR/avatar-upload, the three request subsystems, and a full frontend↔backend cross-reference); plus a direct RBAC, security, and database-schema sweep. Every finding below cites exact file:line and was confirmed by reading the actual code on both sides — nothing here is speculative.

**This document is the project's living audit and implementation tracker, effective 2026-07-18.** Every implementation task starts by locating its finding(s) here and understanding the documented root cause before writing code; every completed fix is verified live (real accounts, real data, real regression run — never assumed) before being marked Resolved below. Historical findings are never deleted, only status-annotated, so this file remains a complete implementation history, not just a current-state snapshot.

## Status Tracker

Authoritative, current-as-of-last-update status for every finding in this document. Anything not listed in the Resolved or Partially Resolved tables below is **Open** — full original detail lives in its numbered finding further down (§ Security Issues, § Broken Features, § Database Issues, § Role Permission Issues, § UI/UX Problems). This tracker is updated in place after every verified fix; historical finding text elsewhere in the document is never edited or removed.

### Resolved

| S1 | Notifications had no ownership/role check (GET, PATCH, DELETE, markallread) | 2026-07-17 | `cirl.js` | Unscoped `GET /api/notifications` and `PATCH /api/notifications/markallread` restricted to `requirePersonnel`; per-id `PATCH`/`DELETE /api/notifications/:id` gained a `canActOnNotification()` ownership check (staff act on anything, everyone else only on notifications addressed to them) | Live-tested with real Admin/Personnel/Partner/View-Only accounts: partner correctly blocked from unscoped routes, able to act on own notification, 403 on another's; staff unaffected. `npm test` 26/26 | None known |

| B1 / S9 / U8 | Partnership Requests Approve/Reject buttons completely non-functional | 2026-07-18 | `views/administrator/partnership_requests.ejs` | **Root cause confirmed unchanged before fixing** (per this task's Phase 1 requirement): `renderPR()` builds each row's `uid` from the app's numeric id alone (`String(r.id \|\| i)`, line 803), and `openPRDetail()` correctly looks up `String(x.id) === uid` (line 839) — but `resolvePR()` (969) and `quickPR()` (994) both looked up `String(x._id \|\| x.id) === uid`. Since every document from `GET /api/requests` carries MongoDB's own `_id` (an ObjectId string, always truthy), `x._id \|\| x.id` always evaluated to `_id`, which never equals a `uid` built from `x.id` — the `.find()` never matched, both functions hit `if (!r) return;`, and the `fetch()` call was never reached. Line 974 had the same bug a second time, independently, building the request URL from `(r._id \|\| r.id)` — confirmed against the backend (`cirl.js:830-843`, `app.patch('/api/requests/:id', requireAdmin, ...)`) which does `parseInt(req.params.id)`, so even a partial fix of only the `.find()` would still have sent the wrong (garbled) id. **Fix:** changed both lookups to `String(x.id) === uid` (matching `openPRDetail`'s already-correct pattern) and the fetch URL to plain `r.id`. Three lines changed total, no other code touched. | Static: `node -c cirl.js` clean; inline `<script>` block extracted and syntax-checked via `new Function()`, clean; confirmed zero remaining `_id \|\| ` lookup patterns anywhere in the file. Live (real MongoDB, real HTTP, no mocks of the actual fix logic): created two real Pending partnership requests via a disposable `potential_partner` test account through the real `POST /api/requests` endpoint, then executed the **actual, unmodified `resolvePR`/`quickPR`/`openPRDetail` functions** extracted verbatim from the fixed file inside a Node harness (minimal DOM/`bootstrap.Modal`/`confirm()` mocks standing in for the browser, real `fetch` against the running server with a real Administrator session cookie) — this runs the identical code a browser would run, not a re-implementation of it. Approve: `resolvePR('8','Approved')` completed with `NO_EXCEPTIONS_THROWN`, modal-hide fired, `renderPR()`/`updateStats()` re-ran and updated the mocked DOM (`pr-count`, `cnt-approved-all`) correctly. Reject: `quickPR('9','Rejected')` completed the same way, `confirm()` fired as expected. Server-side, confirmed via direct MongoDB read: both requests' `status` persisted correctly (`Approved`/`Rejected`); a targeted notification was created in `notifications` for the submitter for each decision (correct `title`/`desc`/`icon`/`color` per decision); a matching `APPROVE`/`REJECT` entry was written to `activitylogs`. `GET /api/requests`, `/api/access-requests`, `/api/document-requests`, `/api/notifications`, `/api/activitylogs`, and the `/partnership-requests` page itself all re-confirmed `200` post-fix. `npm test` 26/26. All test data (2 requests, 3 notifications, 5 activity-log entries, 1 disposable account) deleted after verification — no residue. | Access Requests (`loadAR`/`renderAR`/`resolveAR`) and Document Requests (`loadDR`/`renderDR`) were not touched by this fix and were confirmed still functional (`200` on both list APIs, tab/section switching code untouched); the Partnership Request details modal (`openPRDetail`) was already correct before this fix and was re-confirmed working via the same live harness; dashboard counters (`updateStats()`) recompute correctly after Approve/Reject, confirmed via the mocked DOM element values. | None known. The three request-review endpoints (partnership, access, document) still have no status-transition guard (B4, separately tracked, still Open) — re-approving/re-rejecting an already-decided request is not blocked by the backend; this was outside the scope of this fix and was not touched. |

Personnel/potential_partner should each render their own sidebar/top nav and never leak into another role's chrome | 2026-07-18 | `cirl.js`, `views/partials/header.ejs`, `views/partials/sidebar_personnel.ejs`, `views/administrator/registry.ejs` | **Phase 1 inspection finding, stated before any code was touched:** contrary to the task's framing, Auth. Personnel and potential_partner already had fully dedicated routes *and* their own sidebar (`sidebarPartial: 'sidebar_personnel'` / `'sidebar_partner'`) for all four requested pages (Monitoring/Lifecycle, Document Library, Notifications, Settings) — `/personnel/lifecycle`, `/personnel/documents`, `/personnel/notifications`, `/personnel/settings` and `/partner/monitoring`, `/partner/documents`, `/partner/notifications`, `/partner/settings` all already existed, each rendering its own role's sidebar (Personnel's four reuse `administrator/*.ejs` view templates with a role-specific sidebar passed in — a "shared rendering" strategy already matching the task's own reuse-over-duplication guidance; Partner's four use fully separate `potential_partner/*.ejs` templates). Administrator's Settings (`/admin/settings`) was likewise already dedicated. So no new pages were created — none were missing. What Phase 1 *did* find broken: (1) the bare, unprefixed `GET /notifications` and `GET /documents` routes (Administrator's own pages, `administrator/notifications.ejs`/`administrator/documents.ejs` with the default sidebar) were gated `requireAuth` — reachable by **any** authenticated role, not just Administrator, so a View-Only or potential_partner user who typed either URL directly would see the Administrator's own sidebar and nav (violates "no page displays another role's sidebar"; also a restatement of the already-tracked S7 View-Only `/documents` exposure). (2) Three navigation entry points still pointed at those bare routes instead of each role's already-correct dedicated equivalent: `sidebar_personnel.ejs`'s topbar quick-link ("Notifications" → `/notifications` instead of `/personnel/notifications`); the shared `views/partials/header.ejs` (included by all 24 other role pages) — its bell-dropdown "View All Notifications" button was hardcoded to `/notifications` for every role with no role branching at all, and its user-dropdown `notificationsHref` variable special-cased only `potential_partner`, defaulting Auth. Personnel to the same wrong bare route; `views/administrator/registry.ejs`'s OCR-panel "Document Library" link (reachable by Personnel too, since `/registry` is `requirePersonnel`) was hardcoded to `/documents`. **Fix:** (a) `cirl.js` — `GET /notifications` and `GET /documents` middleware changed `requireAuth` → `requireAdmin`, making them genuinely Administrator-only rather than an accidental shared/unscoped fallback; (b) `header.ejs` — added a role-aware `notificationsHref` computed once near the top (mirroring the file's existing `homeDashboard`/`settingsHref` pattern: Auth. Personnel → `/personnel/notifications`, potential_partner → `/partner/notifications`, else bare `/notifications`), applied to both the bell-panel link and the user-dropdown link, and removed the old, narrower duplicate declaration; (c) `sidebar_personnel.ejs` — topbar link corrected to `/personnel/notifications`; (d) `registry.ejs` — added a one-line role check (`docLibraryHref`) so Personnel gets `/personnel/documents` and Administrator keeps `/documents`. No new routes, no new views, no duplicated business logic — every fix reuses an existing dedicated page or an existing role-branching pattern already present in the same file. | Static: `node -c cirl.js` clean; all four edited `.ejs` files compiled successfully via `ejs.compile()`. Live, one disposable test account cycled through all four roles via direct MongoDB role updates (Administrator/Auth. Personnel/potential_partner/View-Only), real HTTP against the running server: **View-Only** — `/notifications` and `/documents` now correctly `302` (previously reachable), `/viewonly/dashboard` and `/viewonly/request-access` unchanged `200`. **Auth. Personnel** — `/personnel/dashboard`, `/personnel/lifecycle`, `/personnel/documents`, `/personnel/notifications`, `/personnel/settings` all `200` (regression-clean); bare `/notifications`/`/documents` now correctly `302`; rendered `/personnel/dashboard` HTML inspected directly — every "Notifications" link on the page (bell-panel "View All Notifications", "View Strategic Alerts" tab, header user-dropdown, sidebar topbar dropdown, main sidebar nav item — 5 distinct locations) now points to `/personnel/notifications`, zero remaining bare `/notifications` references; `/registry` rendered with `href="/personnel/documents"` in the OCR-panel Document Library link. **Administrator** — `/dashboard`, `/notifications`, `/documents`, `/admin/settings`, `/registry`, `/users` all `200`; `/registry` still correctly renders `href="/documents"` (unchanged for this role); `/documents` page confirmed rendering the Administrator sidebar (`/users` link present). **potential_partner** — `/partner/dashboard`, `/partner/monitoring`, `/partner/documents`, `/partner/notifications`, `/partner/settings` all `200` (full regression, all pre-existing and untouched); bare `/notifications`/`/documents` now correctly `302` (was never used by Partner's own nav, so no functional change, only a closed leak); `GET /api/partnerships` still `200` (from the earlier `requireUploader` fix); header's `notificationsHref` still correctly resolves to `/partner/notifications` in rendered HTML. `npm test` 26/26. No browser-automation tool (Playwright or otherwise) was available in this environment, so "no JavaScript console errors" and visual rendering were not verified in an actual browser — verification here is HTML-response-level (server-rendered `href` correctness, status codes, sidebar-partial presence) rather than a rendered-DOM/console check; flagged as a limitation below. | The role-specific-pages request, taken literally, implied more net-new page creation than was actually needed — Personnel and Partner already had dedicated routes and sidebars for all four requested pages before this task began; documenting that explicitly here so the gap between the request's framing and the codebase's actual prior state is traceable. **Not fixed / explicitly out of scope, carried forward:** R2/M3 (Auth. Personnel still has no sidebar nav link to `/registry`, despite being backend-authorized — `/registry` itself still renders with the default Administrator sidebar for Personnel when reached directly, since it has no role-specific dedicated equivalent; this is the same pre-existing, separately-tracked gap, not introduced or fixed here). No browser/Playwright verification was possible in this environment (see Verification column) — recommend a manual pass in an actual browser, or wiring up Playwright/a similar tool in this environment, before treating this as fully verified end-to-end. |

| — | **Product decision** — Administrator/Auth. Personnel scoped to "own records only" for Documents and Notifications, matching potential_partner's existing ownership model | 2026-07-18 | `cirl.js`, `views/administrator/notifications.ejs` | Explicit user request, not an audit finding. Before implementing, three scope-defining questions were asked and answered: (1) the Partnership/Access/Document Request **review queues stay site-wide** — Admin/Personnel still see and can approve/reject every submission, since that's the core purpose of the role and scoping it would have disabled the B1/S9/U8 workflow just fixed above; (2) Document Library "own uploads" means a literal `uploadedByEmail === self` match, identical to potential_partner; (3) Notifications lose the shared staff feed entirely and are scoped to `targetEmail === self` only, same as potential_partner — explicitly accepted with the caveat (stated in the question and confirmed) that most existing notifications aren't targeted at individual staff emails. **Discovered during Phase 1 inspection, before writing code:** of 29 real notification documents in the database, 28 have no `targetEmail` at all — including every automatic lifecycle "Partnership Expiring/Expired" alert (`cirl.js` toNotify/insertMany block) — so this change makes those 28 invisible to Administrator/Auth. Personnel going forward; only 1 real notification (a document-request-fulfilled alert already targeted at a specific Personnel member's email) survives the new scope. This was surfaced explicitly as a consequence, not silently absorbed. **Implementation:** added one `OWN_SCOPE_ROLES = ['Administrator', 'Auth. Personnel', 'potential_partner']` constant (`cirl.js`) reused across every touched route rather than repeating the role list; `GET /api/notifications` now unconditionally queries `{targetEmail: self}` (this route is already `requirePersonnel`-only, so no other role reaches it); `GET /api/notifications/unread-count`'s existing potential_partner-only scoped branch extended to include Administrator/Auth. Personnel (View-Only remains on the old unscoped branch — unchanged, pre-existing B8 field-name bug, not fixed here); `GET /api/documents` and the `GET /uploads/documents/:filename` file-serving route both had their existing `role === 'potential_partner'` ownership check widened to `OWN_SCOPE_ROLES.includes(role)` (View-Only remains unscoped on both — unchanged, pre-existing S7); `canActOnNotification()` — previously gave Administrator/Auth. Personnel a blanket bypass regardless of ownership — simplified to a single universal rule (`notif.targetEmail === user.email`) for every role, since staff should no longer have special access once their own view is scoped; the unscoped `PATCH /api/notifications/markallread` route (would have marked every notification in the database as read, including other users', once staff have "own" semantics) was removed entirely as newly-dangerous dead code, and `administrator/notifications.ejs`'s `markAllRead()` was repointed to the pre-existing `PATCH /api/notifications/markallread/mine` route (already used by `partner_notifications.ejs` — reused, not duplicated). Stale comments describing the old "shared staff feed" model were updated throughout. | Live-tested against real data, not synthetic inserts (per explicit instruction this pass): one disposable test account cycled through all four roles. **Administrator & Auth. Personnel** (test account owns zero real documents/notifications): `GET /api/documents` and `GET /api/notifications` both returned `[]`, exactly matching the pre-existing, already-correctly-scoped `/api/documents/mine` and `/api/notifications/mine` routes byte-for-byte — proving the same ownership query is now active on both pairs of routes. `GET /api/requests` (Partnership Requests review) returned the identical real count (7) for both Administrator and Auth. Personnel, confirming the review queue is still fully site-wide as decided. Ownership boundary re-tested against **real, pre-existing data**: `PATCH`/`DELETE /api/notifications/29` (a real notification targeted at `santos@cspc.edu.ph`, not the test account) both correctly `403`'d with no mutation (confirmed via an unchanged before/after count of untargeted notifications, 28); `GET /uploads/documents/...` for a real file uploaded by a different real user (`bombi@gmail.com`) correctly `403`'d. `markAllRead()` (repointed to `/mine`) returned `{success:true}` as a safe no-op (test account owned nothing to mark). The old bare `markallread` route is confirmed gone (no route matches it anymore). **potential_partner** and **View-Only** regression-checked and unaffected: partner's own pages/API all unchanged `200`/scoped-as-before; View-Only's `/api/documents` still returns the full unscoped 11-document set, confirming the pre-existing S7 gap was neither fixed nor worsened. `npm test` 26/26. Disposable test account deleted afterward, no residue. **Process note:** mid-verification, a stale `node cirl.js` process from an earlier session turn was found still bound to port 3000 and silently serving pre-change code, producing a false-negative first test pass; identified via `netstat`/`Get-CimInstance Win32_Process` cross-check (two processes, different start times, only one actually `LISTENING`), killed, and the full test pass was re-run cleanly against a single confirmed server instance. | Every Administrator/Auth. Personnel account that hasn't personally uploaded a document or been the direct target of a notification will now see an empty Document Library and near-empty Notifications feed — this is the explicitly-requested behavior, not a defect, but is a significant, user-visible change from the previous shared-feed model. Nothing currently creates a notification targeted at an Administrator's own email (Administrators only ever appear as the *actor* in `logActivity`/approval flows, never as a `targetEmail`), so an Administrator account will likely see zero notifications going forward until/unless something is built to target them directly. No browser/Playwright verification was possible in this environment (none available) — this pass, like the prior two, is HTTP-response/DB-level verification only. |
| S11 | Roadmap v2 Phase G2 — Rate limiting (`loginLimiter`) was applied only to `/login`; `/signup`, the three password-change routes, and the public unauthenticated `/api/institutions` proxy had none, open to account-creation spam, password brute-forcing, and abuse of the app as a relay against the third-party university API | 2026-07-19 | `cirl.js` | Re-confirmed all five target routes directly against current source (`/signup` line 419, `/api/admin/password` 2045, `/api/personnel/password` ~2098 at time of fix, `/api/institutions` ~2282, `/api/partner/password` ~2598 — all drifted slightly from the audit's originally-cited lines due to earlier fixes this session). **Bug caught and fixed during this item's own live verification, before marking it Resolved:** the first implementation reused a single shared `rateLimit()` instance (`apiLimiter`) across all four non-login routes, and reused `loginLimiter` itself (unmodified) on `/signup`. Live-testing exposed two real defects this caused: (1) express-rate-limit keys solely by IP by default, so a single shared instance across multiple routes means they all draw from **one combined counter per IP** — probing `GET /api/institutions` 10 times from one IP left zero budget for an unrelated `POST /api/admin/password` request from the same IP moments later, incorrectly 429-ing a legitimate password change; (2) reusing `loginLimiter` on `/signup` meant a 429 on `/signup` would incorrectly render the `'index'` (login) page template instead of `/signup`'s own `'signup'` template. **Fixed:** replaced both with a `makeRateLimiter(jsonMessage, htmlHandler)` factory that returns a fresh, independent `rateLimit()` instance per call (same 10-req/15-min window, same `NODE_ENV=test` bypass as the original `loginLimiter`); instantiated six named, independent limiters — `loginLimiter` (now used only on `/login`, its original sole purpose), `signupLimiter` (own instance, 429 handler renders the correct `'signup'` template with re-populated `formData`), `institutionsLimiter`, `adminPasswordLimiter`, `personnelPasswordLimiter`, `partnerPasswordLimiter` (four independent JSON-response instances, one per route, no cross-route sharing). | Static: `node -c cirl.js` clean at every stage, including after the mid-verification fix. Live, single fresh server instance (confirmed via `netstat`/`Get-CimInstance Win32_Process`): `GET /api/institutions` — 10 requests `200`, 11th `429` with correct JSON body. `POST /signup` — 10 requests processed normally (validation-error `200`s), 11th `429` with body confirmed containing "Too many signup attempts" (the `/signup`-specific message, proving the correct dedicated handler/template fired, not `loginLimiter`'s). **Cross-route independence re-verified after the fix** (the exact scenario that caught the bug): exhausted `/api/institutions`' budget (10 requests) from one IP, then immediately performed a real password change via `POST /api/admin/password` from the same IP using a disposable Administrator test account — succeeded (`{"success":true}`), confirmed by a subsequent successful login with the new password (`302`); also confirmed `/signup`'s exhausted budget has zero effect on `/login` from the same IP (`302`, unaffected) — proving all six limiters now hold fully independent per-route counters. `npm test` 26/26 (both before and after the mid-verification fix). Test account and temp helper script deleted afterward, no residue. | None known. Each limiter's 10-request/15-minute window is shared per-IP across all legitimate users behind that IP (e.g. a NAT'd office network) — a pre-existing characteristic of `loginLimiter`'s original design, now consistently extended to the five new routes rather than a new limitation introduced here. |
| S12 | Roadmap v2 Phase G3 — Avatar and OCR/document upload middleware validated file type by client-controlled `mimetype`/extension only (`fileFilter`), not actual file content — a script renamed with a `.png`/`.pdf` extension and a matching declared mimetype would pass unchecked | 2026-07-19 | `middleware/verifyMagicBytes.js` (new), `cirl.js`, `routes/ocrRoutes.js` | Confirmed root cause directly: multer's `fileFilter` only ever sees header metadata (`originalname`, `mimetype`) before any bytes are read off the stream, so no amount of logic inside `fileFilter` itself can inspect real content — validation has to happen after multer's `diskStorage` has already saved the file. `file-type` (an npm package) is not an installed dependency; `sharp` is, but only covers image formats, not the PDF case `uploadMiddleware.js` also needs to cover — so rather than add a new dependency (against "avoid introducing new frameworks"), wrote a small manual magic-byte check (JPEG `FF D8 FF`, PNG's 8-byte signature, PDF's `%PDF-` ASCII header) as one shared `verifyMagicBytes(file)` helper, reused by both of the app's two upload call sites rather than duplicated: `cirl.js`'s `POST /api/partner/avatar` (the only avatar-upload route in the app — confirmed via grep, no admin/personnel equivalent exists) and `routes/ocrRoutes.js`'s `POST /api/ocr/extract`. Each call site invokes it right after its existing `multer.single(...)` callback confirms `!err && req.file`; on mismatch, the just-saved file is deleted and a `400` is returned before the route's real logic (profile update / OCR job start) ever runs. | Static: `node -c` clean on all three touched/new files. Unit-level, direct calls to `verifyMagicBytes()` against synthetic files (no HTTP): a real PNG signature with `image/png` mimetype → accepted, file untouched; a plain-text file claiming `image/png` → rejected, file deleted; a real `%PDF-` signature with `application/pdf` mimetype → accepted; a real PDF's bytes mislabeled as `image/png` (cross-type mismatch) → rejected, file deleted. Live, full HTTP round-trip against a single fresh server instance with a disposable `potential_partner` test account (this role satisfies `requirePartner` for the avatar route and `requireUploader` for OCR): a plain-text file renamed to `fake_avatar.png` with `type=image/png` sent to `/api/partner/avatar` → `400` "Unsupported file type...", confirmed zero residue left in `uploads/avatars/`; a real 8-byte-PNG-signature file sent the same way → `200` with a real `avatarUrl`, file present on disk as expected. Same pattern for `/api/ocr/extract`: a fake `%`-free text file renamed to `.pdf` → `400`, zero residue in `uploads/tmp/`; a real `%PDF-`-signed file → `202` with a real `jobId` returned, confirming the legitimate OCR pipeline still starts correctly. `npm test` 26/26. Test account, uploaded test avatar file, and temp test files all deleted afterward, no residue. | None known. This closes the "renamed-file-with-a-matching-declared-mimetype" gap specifically; it does not implement full content-format validation (e.g. a corrupt-but-correctly-signed PDF would still pass, same as before) — that was already understood to be out of scope per the original finding's own framing ("reduced-severity... still a 'not actually validated' gap" closed to the extent of catching an outright content/extension mismatch). |
| S13 | Roadmap v2 Phase G4 — No global 4-arg Express error-handling middleware existed. Every route wraps itself in try/catch (confirmed still true), but nothing caught a genuinely unexpected failure (a throw outside any try/catch, or — since this app runs Express 5.2.1, confirmed via `node_modules/express/package.json` — an unhandled async-handler promise rejection, which Express 5 auto-forwards to error middleware) | 2026-07-19 | `cirl.js` | Added one final `app.use((err, req, res, next) => {...})` after the existing 404 catch-all (Express recognizes error middleware by its 4-argument arity regardless of position, but placing it last is the standard convention). It logs the real error server-side via `console.error`, then responds with a generic message only — never `err.message` or the stack — regardless of `NODE_ENV`; it branches JSON vs. HTML based on the request path (`/api/*`) or `Accept` header, matching the app's existing per-route convention of JSON-for-API/HTML-for-pages. Includes a `res.headersSent` guard (forwards to Express's default handler instead of double-responding if headers already went out — relevant for the streaming routes in G5, resolved next). | Static: `node -c cirl.js` clean. Live verification methodology: added two temporary diagnostic routes (`/__temp_g4_test_json`, `/__temp_g4_test_html`, each an async handler that unconditionally threw an `Error('SECRET_STACK_TRACE_DETAIL')`) on a single fresh server instance, confirmed both correctly returned a generic `500` (JSON body via `Accept: application/json`; HTML body otherwise) with **zero occurrences** of the real error text in either response (verified via `grep -c` on the actual response body, count `0`), while the real error/stack **was** present in the server's own log — confirming "log internally, never leak to client" holds for both response formats. Confirmed the server process stayed alive and listening after both unhandled throws (Express 5's auto-catch working as expected, no crash). Removed both temporary routes immediately after (re-confirmed via `grep -c '__temp_g4_test'` → `0`), restarted a clean single-instance server, and spot-checked normal routes (`GET /` → `200`, `GET /nonexistent-page` → `404`) to confirm the 404 handler and normal routing are unaffected. `npm test` 26/26. | None known. This is a last-resort fallback, not a replacement for any existing route's own try/catch — every route's own specific error messages (validation failures, etc.) are completely unchanged and unaffected by this addition. |
| S14 | Roadmap v2 Phase G5 — PDF/Excel export routes (`renderTablePdf`; the two Excel routes) set response headers and stream data with no `stream error`/`res.on('error')` handling. If PDFKit/ExcelJS threw mid-stream, or a client disconnected mid-download, the surrounding `catch` block's `res.status(500).json(...)` would conflict with already-sent headers | 2026-07-19 | `cirl.js` | Confirmed all four export routes (`partnerships/pdf`, `partnerships/excel`, `activitylog/pdf`, `activitylog/excel`) plus the shared `renderTablePdf` helper had the identical unguarded pattern. Added: (1) a `res.headersSent` check (falling back to a plain `res.end()` when headers already went out, instead of attempting a second `res.status()`) to all four routes' `catch` blocks, plus a `console.error` so the real error is never silently swallowed; (2) on `renderTablePdf`'s `doc`/`res` streams specifically, a `doc.on('error', ...)` handler (for a genuine PDFKit-internal failure) and a `res.on('close', ...)` handler that logs only when `!res.writableEnded` (a client disconnecting mid-download fires `'close'`, not `'error'`, on the response — discovered empirically during this fix's own live verification, see Limitations). | Static: `node -c cirl.js` clean at every stage. Live, single fresh server instance, disposable Auth. Personnel test account: **normal-flow regression** — all four export routes still `200` with valid output (`partnerships.pdf` 2 real pages, `activitylog.pdf` 10 real pages, both `.xlsx` files well-formed, confirmed via `file` and byte counts). **Forced-failure verification, three independent real-disconnect techniques tried, in order of increasing realism:** (a) `curl --max-time 0.05` — aborted before any bytes arrived (too fast to prove anything); (b) `curl --limit-rate 2k --max-time 2` — genuine partial download (4KB of 19.9KB) confirmed via the truncated output file, curl exit 28; (c) a raw Node `net.Socket` that sent the real HTTP request over a real TCP connection with a real session cookie, then called `socket.destroy()` (an abrupt RST, not a graceful close) after receiving a partial chunk — done separately against both the PDF and Excel export routes. **In all three cases, across both PDF and Excel, the server remained alive and listening afterward (`netstat`-confirmed) with no crash, no unhandled exception, and no "headers already sent" error in the log.** `npm test` 26/26. Test account and all temp files/scripts deleted afterward, no residue. | **Honest limitation, matching a caveat already present in this document's own "Notes on scope and confidence" section:** at this app's current data volume (a few dozen partnership records, a few hundred activity-log entries), PDF/Excel generation completes so fast on localhost that even an abrupt raw-socket RST consistently arrived only after the server had already finished writing (in the raw-socket test, 88% and 100%-minus-a-few-bytes of the response had already been flushed before the destroy signal could have reached the server) — meaning the exact "headers already sent, catch block conflicts" race could not be forced to manifest live at current scale, across three different realistic attempts. The defensive code (headersSent guards, doc/res error and close listeners) is correct and directly addresses the documented root cause, and the server's survival of every disconnect attempt confirms the crash-prevention goal is met — but the single specific catch-block race this finding names was not empirically reproduced this pass; it would need either a much larger export (thousands of rows) or an artificial generation delay to force open at this data scale. |
| S15 | Roadmap v2 Phase G6 — `GET /api/ocr/status/:jobId` had no ownership check — any `requireUploader` session could poll another user's OCR job if they obtained/guessed the jobId | 2026-07-19 | `services/ocrService.js`, `controllers/ocrController.js` | Root cause, confirmed by reading the actual job lifecycle: `createJob()` never persisted the uploader's identity onto the in-memory job object at all — `meta.uploadedByEmail` was passed through to `runJob()`/`archiveUpload()` for the Document Library record, but the `jobs` Map entry itself (what `getJob()`/`status()` actually reads) never carried it, so there was nothing to check ownership against even if `ocrController.status()` had tried. Fixed by threading `uploadedByEmail` into `createJob(uploadedByEmail)` so it's stored directly on the job record, and having `startJob()` pass `meta.uploadedByEmail` through; `ocrController.status()` now 403s when `job.uploadedByEmail` is set and doesn't match `req.session.user.email` (mitigated already by the jobId being an unguessable `crypto.randomUUID()` with a 10-minute TTL — this closes the gap for the case where a jobId does leak or get shared). | Static: `node -c` clean on both modified files. Live, single fresh server instance, two disposable `potential_partner` test accounts (User A, User B): User A started a real OCR job via `POST /api/ocr/extract` (a real, if minimal, `%PDF-`-signed file — this also incidentally exercised the G3 magic-byte check from earlier in this same session), then polled its own job's status — `200`, real job data returned (the minimal placeholder PDF correctly produced a real "Invalid PDF structure" processing error, unrelated to this fix, proving the ownership check doesn't interfere with the real response shape). User B, a completely different session, polled User A's real jobId — `403` "You do not have access to this job." Regression: an unrelated, nonexistent jobId still correctly `404`s for either user, confirming the not-found path is untouched. `npm test` 26/26. Both test accounts and the temp test file deleted afterward, no residue. | None known. |

## Re-Verification Pass — 2026-07-18

Full baseline re-verification requested: every finding not already in the Resolved/Partially Resolved tables above was re-checked directly against the current codebase (not assumed from the prior write-up), via four parallel deep-dive passes covering Security (S5–S17), Broken Features (B2–B11), Database/mass-assignment (D1–D3 plus the Phase 4 items), and RBAC/UI-UX/Missing-Features (R1–R4, U3–U5, F1–F3, plus the Phase 2 items). No code was changed while producing this section.

**Result: every previously-Open finding is still open.** Nothing was found already-fixed-but-undocumented, and no entirely new defect classes were discovered. The only changes are (1) file:line references drifting from the original 2026-07-16/17 line numbers, because this session's earlier fixes (mass-assignment allowlists, the `requireUploader` change, the Sign Up as Partner removal) shifted surrounding code, and (2) two small nuances noted below. Corrected current line numbers for every re-checked finding are listed here; the original numbered findings further down this document are left untouched as the historical record, per this document's "never edit historical findings" rule.

**Nuance 1 — U3 (duplicate "Partnership Requests" buttons).** The two Quick Action buttons on `views/administrator/admin_dashboard.ejs:387-402` no longer share an identical label — one now reads "Partnership Requests" (line 389, → `/requests`) and the other "Partnership Requests Hub" (line 401, → `/partnership-requests`). The exact-duplicate-label claim from the original finding is stale, but the underlying confusion (two buttons, overlapping "requests" destinations) persists in substance — kept **Open**, description adjusted.

**Nuance 2 — B10 (`DELETE` routes skip `deletedCount`).** `DELETE /api/calendarevents/:id` (`cirl.js:1991-1999`) still does a bare `deleteOne` → `{success:true}` with zero pre-check, exactly as documented. `DELETE /api/notifications/:id` (`cirl.js:1935-1949`) turns out to have partial protection not credited in the original finding: it does a `findOne` existence + ownership check (lines 1939-1943) before deleting — so a nonexistent id is already caught upstream of the delete. It still never checks the `deleteOne` call's own `deletedCount`, so a delete racing another delete of the same id could still report false success, just a narrower window than the finding implied. Kept **Open**, description refined.

## RBAC Investigation — Administrator & Auth. Personnel (2026-07-18)

Requested: investigate reported RBAC issues affecting Administrator and Auth. Personnel following the role-specific navigation changes made earlier the same day (the `/notifications`/`/documents` `requireAdmin` tightening and the four navigation-link fixes, see the Resolved table above). Explicit instruction: trace the complete flow, do not assume, check for role-string mismatches ("Administrator", "Auth. Personnel", "Auth Personnel", "Personnel", "admin", "administrator"). No Playwright/browser-automation tool is available in this environment (checked again this pass); verification below is HTML/HTTP-response-level, same methodology as the prior role-navigation task.

**Result: no defect found.** Every check below came back clean. Documenting the full trail since the deliverable is a verification report, not a fix — there was nothing to fix.

**Database:** `db.collection('users').distinct('role')` returned exactly four values — `Administrator`, `Auth. Personnel`, `View-Only`, `potential_partner` — confirmed byte-for-byte via character-code inspection (no stray whitespace, no casing variants, no `Auth Personnel`/`Personnel`/`admin`/`administrator` anywhere in the 17-document `users` collection). Every real Administrator (1) and Auth. Personnel (10, two `Inactive`) account has a numeric `id`, matching `status`/`role` field types — no structural anomalies.

**Code:** grepped `cirl.js` and all of `views/` for every variant listed in the request (`role === 'Admin'`, `'Personnel'`, `'Auth Personnel'`, `'administrator'`, `'admin'`, `.toLowerCase()`, `.trim()` on role comparisons) — zero matches anywhere. Every role check in the codebase uses the exact same two canonical strings the database actually stores.

**Middleware, re-read directly from source (not from memory):** `requireAuth`, `requirePersonnel`, `requireAdmin`, `requirePartner`, `requireUploader` (`cirl.js:161-207`) and `homeForRole()` (`cirl.js:210-215`) all compare against `'Administrator'` / `'Auth. Personnel'` / `'potential_partner'` exactly as stored — no typos, no logic inversion, unchanged by the earlier navigation-fix task. The global session-revalidation middleware (`cirl.js:106-136`, registered before every route) re-fetches the live role from MongoDB on every request by numeric `id`, so a session never runs on a stale role snapshot.

**Live verification, real login flow, one account cycled through both roles via direct MongoDB role assignment (fresh session each time, not reused):**
- **Administrator** — every item from the request's checklist tested and `200`: `/dashboard`, `/documents`, `/notifications`, `/admin/settings`, `/registry`, `/api/partnerships`, `/users`, `/api/users`; also `/partnership-requests`, `/lifecycle`, `/calendar`, `/reports`.
- **Auth. Personnel** — every item `200`: `/personnel/dashboard`, `/personnel/lifecycle` (Monitoring), `/personnel/documents`, `/personnel/notifications`, `/personnel/settings`, `/registry` (Registry access, backend-authorized per `requirePersonnel`); also `/personnel/calendar`, `/personnel/reports`, `/personnel/requests`. Admin-only routes correctly still blocked with a clean `302` (not an error): `/dashboard`, `/users`, and the now-`requireAdmin` bare `/notifications`/`/documents`.
- The API endpoints `documents.ejs`/`notifications.ejs` call client-side (`GET /api/documents`, `GET /api/notifications`, `PATCH /api/documents/:id`, `PATCH/DELETE /api/notifications/:id`, `PATCH /api/notifications/markallread`) were independently re-checked — all pre-existing, all still correctly gated (`requirePersonnel`/`requireAuth`+ownership), none touched by the earlier navigation-fix task, none blocking Personnel.

**Specifically ruled out, per the request's list of possible causes:** session role missing/incorrect (revalidation middleware confirmed re-syncing correctly); inconsistent role-comparison strings (none found, DB and code both clean); middleware rejecting valid roles (byte-verified correct); routes redirecting to the wrong dashboard (`homeForRole()` correct, and every checklist item landed exactly where expected); sidebar receiving incorrect user data (both `sidebar.ejs` and `sidebar_personnel.ejs` unchanged since the last verified-clean pass); shared header generating incorrect links (`header.ejs`'s rendered output re-confirmed correct for both roles — no change since the prior task, which already verified this file's actual rendered HTML); the `requireAdmin` tightening blocking intended access (tested directly above — it does not; Personnel and Partner both have their own dedicated equivalents and never route through the bare paths). One item from the request's file list, `sidebar_admin.ejs`, does not exist in this codebase — the Administrator sidebar file is named `sidebar.ejs`; confirmed via `Glob`/`Grep` that no file or reference by that name exists anywhere, so it was not a hidden second sidebar causing a conflict.

**Files changed:** none — this was a verification-only pass, no code was modified.

**Verification performed:** `npm test` 26/26; server start/connect clean; full live checklist above via a disposable test account (created, cycled through both roles, deleted afterward — no residue).

**Outstanding:** if a specific RBAC failure is still being observed in a live environment, it was not reproducible through this investigation's methodology (direct HTTP requests with fresh sessions against the current source). The most likely explanation for a discrepancy at this point would be an artifact outside the code itself — a browser tab holding a session cookie from before the day's changes were deployed, or a stale running server process serving old in-memory state (one such stray process, unrelated to this session's own test servers, was found listening on port 3000 at the start of this investigation and was stopped before testing began, precisely to rule this out as a source of confusion). Recommend reproducing with the exact URL, role, and observed symptom (redirect target / status code / console error) if the issue persists, and wiring up a browser-automation tool (Playwright or equivalent) in this environment so future RBAC verification can include actual rendered-DOM and console-error checks rather than HTTP-response-level inspection alone.

## 1. Security Issues

| # | Finding | File:Line | Severity |
|---|---|---|---|
| S1 | `GET/PATCH/DELETE /api/notifications` have **no ownership check at all** — any authenticated user (including a self-signup View-Only account) can read, mark-read, or delete **any** notification in the system, including other organizations' internal notices. The scoped `/mine` variant exists and is correctly used elsewhere, just not enforced here. | `cirl.js:1123-1131`, `1862-1882` | **Critical** |
| S2 | `GET /api/calendarevents` and `POST/PATCH/DELETE /api/calendarevents/:id` have **no role or ownership check** — any authenticated user can create/edit/delete **any** calendar event system-wide by id. `GET /calendar` itself is `requireAuth` (not `requirePersonnel`), so any role gets the full editable admin calendar UI, even though a dedicated read-only partner calendar exists. | `cirl.js:1885-1930`, `2278-2280` | **Critical** |
| S3 | `/api/reports/partnerships/pdf` and `/api/reports/partnerships/excel` are `requireAuth`-only and query the **entire unscoped `partnerships` collection**. `buildPartnershipFilter()` passes raw `req.query` values straight into the MongoDB filter with no type validation, which Express's query parser turns into operator injection for bracket-notation params (e.g. `?status[$ne]=x`). Net effect: any authenticated `potential_partner` or `View-Only` user can download a full PDF/Excel of every partnership in the system — every organization's contacts, coordinators, remarks — by hitting the URL directly, bypassing the correctly-scoped `/api/partnerships/mine` pattern that exists specifically to prevent this (see the code's own comment at line 605-608). | `cirl.js:1280-1288`, `1378-1431` | **Critical** |
| S4 | Same pattern, worse scope: `/api/reports/activitylog/pdf`, `/api/reports/activitylog/excel`, and `GET /api/activitylogs` are all `requireAuth`-only and return the **entire unfiltered system audit trail** (every ADD/EDIT/DELETE/APPROVE/REJECT by every Administrator/Personnel, with names and record details) to any logged-in role. This directly contradicts the RBAC matrix displayed in `views/users.ejs`, which shows "Export Audit Log" and "View Audit Trail" as Administrator-only. | `cirl.js:1433-1484`, `1796-1804` | **Critical** |
| S5 | `POST /api/activitylogs` is `requireAuth`-only, spreads `req.body` directly into an inserted document with zero validation. Not called by any frontend (real logging goes through the trusted server-side `logActivity()` helper) — but it's live and reachable, letting any authenticated user forge arbitrary fake audit entries, undermining the audit trail's integrity. | `cirl.js:1806-1823` | **High** |
| S6 | `POST /api/notifications` is `requireAuth`-only, and `targetEmail` is fully client-controlled with no check that it matches the sender. Not called by any frontend, but live and reachable — any authenticated user can forge a notification that appears to come from the system, targeted at any other user. | `cirl.js:1826-1837` | **High** |
| S7 | `GET /api/documents` (metadata) is `requireAuth`-only and unscoped — returns every document's title/institution/uploader-email/file-link to any role. The actual file-serving route (`/uploads/documents/:filename`) **is** correctly ownership-checked, so file bytes are protected, but the metadata (including confirmation of file links and who-uploaded-what) is not. A `potential_partner` can reach this by navigating to `/documents` directly instead of `/partner/documents`. | `cirl.js:1169-1178`, `2274` | **High** |
| S8 | `POST /api/access-requests` trusts the client-supplied `email` field verbatim instead of using `req.session.user.email` (every other request subsystem does this correctly). Since approval promotes/creates a user account for whatever email is on the request, any authenticated user can submit a spoofed request under someone else's email; if approved, that person's account is silently promoted/created without their knowledge. | `cirl.js:2043-2075` | **High** |
| S9 | The Partnership Requests admin hub's Approve/Reject buttons look up the record with `x._id || x.id`. Every document from `GET /api/requests` includes MongoDB's `_id` (always truthy), so this **always** resolves to the Mongo ObjectId string, never the app's numeric `id` the row was keyed by — the lookup never matches, and the function returns before the API call ever fires. **The primary admin workflow for approving/rejecting partnership requests is completely non-functional via this button.** (Also filed under §2 Broken Features — flagged here too because it blocks the core authorization workflow the rest of this report assumes works.) | `views/administrator/partnership_requests.ejs:969, 994` | **Critical** |
| S10 | No CSRF token anywhere in the app; the only mitigation is `sameSite: 'lax'` on the session cookie (a real but partial mitigation — blocks cross-site POST/PATCH/DELETE from another origin, but not top-level GET navigation). `GET /logout` performs a state change (destroys the session) via GET, which **is** reachable via top-level navigation under `sameSite=lax` — a "logout CSRF" (low-impact nuisance, not data-exposing). | `cirl.js:91-102`, `544` | Medium |
| S11 | Rate limiting (`loginLimiter`) is applied only to `/login`. `/signup`, ~~`/signup/partner`~~, the three password-change routes, and the public, unauthenticated `/api/institutions` proxy have no rate limiting — open to account-creation spam, password brute-forcing (session-gated, lower risk), and abuse of the app as a relay against the third-party university API. *(`/signup/partner` removed 2026-07-18 — Sign Up as Partner feature removal, see Status Tracker; finding otherwise unchanged.)* | `cirl.js:63-70` (only usage at `333`) | Medium |
| S12 | Avatar upload and OCR upload middleware validate file type by `mimetype`/extension only (both client-controlled), not actual file content/magic bytes. Reduced-severity because served extensions are locked to image/PDF types and there's no `nosniff` header gap that matters for those extensions, but it's still a "not actually validated" gap. | `middleware/avatarUploadMiddleware.js`, `middleware/uploadMiddleware.js` | Medium |
| S13 | No global 4-arg Express error-handling middleware. Every route currently wraps itself in try/catch (good), but if that pattern is ever missed, Express's default handler can leak stack traces to the client depending on `NODE_ENV`. | `cirl.js` (absent) | Low |
| S14 | PDF/Excel export routes (`renderTablePdf`, the Excel routes) set response headers and then stream data with no `stream error`/`res.on('error')` handling. If PDFKit/ExcelJS throws mid-stream, or the client disconnects mid-download, the catch block's `res.status(500).json(...)` will hit a "headers already sent" conflict. | `cirl.js:1312-1365`, `1395-1431` | Medium |
| S15 | `GET /api/ocr/status/:jobId` has no ownership check — any `requireUploader` session could poll another user's OCR job if they obtained the jobId. Mitigated by the jobId being an unguessable `crypto.randomUUID()` with a 10-minute TTL. | `controllers/ocrController.js:28-42` | Low |
| S16 | The `/viewonly*` route family uses an **exclude-list** (blocks only Administrator/Auth. Personnel) instead of an **allow-list** like every other role-gated route family in the app. A `potential_partner` user can reach `/viewonly/dashboard` by typing the URL directly. | `cirl.js:2378-2410` | Medium |
| S17 | DSS "Nearest Expiring"/"Longest-Overdue" widgets on the Reports pages build HTML via string concatenation and unescaped `.innerHTML` using partnership fields (`p.inst`, `p.country`, `p.type`) that Personnel/Admin can set with zero server-side sanitization on `POST /api/partnerships`. Low severity since only trusted internal roles can write that data today, but it's a genuine unescaped-HTML pattern. | `views/administrator/reports.ejs:698,714`, `views/auth. personnel/personnel_reports.ejs:460,476` | Low |

## 2. Broken Pages/Features and Their Causes

| # | Finding | Cause | File:Line | Severity |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| B1 | Partnership Requests admin Approve/Reject buttons do nothing (no network call, no error, no state change). | `x._id || x.id` lookup bug — see S9. | `views/administrator/partnership_requests.ejs:969,994` | **Critical** |
| B2 | The legacy `/requests` admin page is still live, still linked in the shared sidebar (reachable by Auth. Personnel too, via `requirePersonnel`), and shows **false "approved/rejected successfully" toasts** even when nothing happened — because `PATCH /api/requests/:id` is `requireAdmin`-only, so when an Auth. Personnel user clicks Approve, the request 302-redirects to their dashboard; `fetch` follows it and returns `ok:true` on the redirected HTML, and the code never checks `res.ok` before declaring success. | `views/administrator/requests.ejs:421-446`; route gate mismatch between `cirl.js:2262` (`requirePersonnel`) and `897` (`requireAdmin`) | **High** |
| B3 | On that same legacy page, the big stat cards always show fake hardcoded numbers (15/5/5/3/2) instead of real counts — `updateCounts()` correctly computes and displays the real numbers, but `initCounters()` runs immediately after and re-animates every stat element from 0 up to its hardcoded `data-target` attribute, overwriting the real value. The filter-tab badges on the same page show the correct numbers, so the page visibly contradicts itself. | `views/administrator/requests.ejs:80,99,118,137,156` (data-target attrs) vs `286-304`/`450-462` (JS) | Medium |
| B4 | No status-transition guards on any of the three request-review PATCH endpoints. A request can be approved twice, rejected after approval, or (racing a user's withdrawal) approved after being withdrawn — each re-run re-executes side effects: duplicate partnership-extension updates, duplicate notifications, duplicate activity-log entries. | `cirl.js:897-909` (partnership), `2101-2112` (access), `1077-1089` (document) | High |
| B5 | Rejecting an already-**Accepted** access request (i.e. the user already has the promoted role) does not revert their role — leaves an inconsistent state: request shows "Rejected" while the account is still `Auth. Personnel`. | `cirl.js:2149-2150` | Medium |
| B6 | Approving a brand-new access request generates a one-time `tempPassword` in the API response, but **no frontend anywhere reads or displays it** — the new Auth. Personnel account's login credentials are generated and then permanently lost. | `cirl.js:2117-2156` (generation); no `tempPassword` reference anywhere in `views/` | **High** |
| B7 | Marking a document request "Fulfilled" sends a notification claiming the document "has been fulfilled and added to the Document Library" — no code actually adds anything to the Document Library. It's purely a status flip; the admin must separately upload the file through the unrelated Document Library module. Misleading to the requester. | `cirl.js:1111` (notification text) vs `1077-1121` (handler, no `documents` insert) | Medium |
| B8 | Notification unread-count badge is permanently stuck at 0 for Administrator, Auth. Personnel, and View-Only (only `potential_partner` works correctly). The query filters on `{ read: false }`, but every notification document in the app actually uses the field name `unread` — the query never matches anything. | `cirl.js:1145` vs schema usage at `1131,1832,1842,1855` and `n.unread` in the view JS | Medium |
| B9 | `GET /api/dashboard/stats` filters on lowercase status strings (`'active'`, `'pending'`, etc.) while every status field in the app is capitalized (`'Active'`, `'Pending'`) — would always return all-zero counts. Currently harmless only because no frontend calls this route. | `cirl.js:559-592` | Low (dead code, but fix if ever wired up) |
| B10 | `DELETE /api/calendarevents/:id` and `DELETE /api/notifications/:id` never check `deletedCount` — always respond `{success:true}` even when nothing was deleted. | `cirl.js:1921-1930`, `1873-1882` | Low |
| B11 | Several backend routes are dead code — built but never called by any frontend: `GET /api/me`, `GET /api/dashboard/stats`, `POST /api/lifecycle/recompute`, `POST /api/activitylogs`, `POST /api/notifications`, `DELETE /api/notifications/:id`, `DELETE /api/users/:id` (no delete-user UI exists — User Management only supports Add/Edit/Deactivate). | various | Low |

This is a **confirmed-clean** item, listed for completeness. | — | — |

| M3 | Auth. Personnel has **no navigation link** to `/registry` (`sidebar_personnel.ejs` has none), even though the backend's `requirePersonnel` gate on `/registry` and on `POST/PATCH/DELETE /api/partnerships` explicitly authorizes this role to add/edit/delete partnerships. Their only reachable partnership-touching page (`/personnel/lifecycle`) only supports editing/renewing existing records — no Add, no Delete. A fully backend-authorized capability has no UI path for the role it was built for. | `views/partials/sidebar_personnel.ejs` (absent link) vs `cirl.js:2258,1210,1224,1238` | High |

## 4. Database-Related Issues

| # | Finding | Severity |
|---|---|---|
| D1 | **No indexes are created anywhere in the codebase** (`db.js` only connects; no `createIndex` calls exist). Most importantly: no unique index on `users.email` — email-uniqueness is enforced purely at the application level (check-then-insert), which is inherently racy (two near-simultaneous signups/creates could both pass the existence check before either commits, producing duplicate accounts with the same email and breaking login's uniqueness assumption). Also no index on any of the `id` fields used for essentially every lookup/update/delete across every collection (`users`, `partnerships`, `requests`, `documentrequests`, `accessRequests`, `calendarevents`, `notifications`, `activitylogs`) — every one of those is a full collection scan today; fine at current scale, a real cost as data grows. No index on `notifications.targetEmail`, used by every `/mine`-scoped query. | High |
| D2 | The `id`-mass-assignment pattern (`{ id: nextId, ...req.body }`, spreading `req.body` *after* the server-assigned id, letting a client override it) was found and fixed for `users` earlier this session, but the identical pattern still exists, unfixed, on `POST /api/calendarevents` (`cirl.js:1900`) and was not checked against `partnerships`, `requests`, `document-requests`, or `access-requests` POST routes — needs a systematic sweep. | Medium |
| D3 | Legacy `agreements` collection (present in `Database_backup/ciprms.agreements.json`, structurally a superseded predecessor of `partnerships`) is not referenced anywhere in the live application. Dead data, not a missing feature — recommend dropping it or explicitly archiving the backup and noting it's historical. | Low |

## 5. Role and Permission Issues

| # | Finding | Severity |
|---|---|---|
| R1 | The RBAC permission matrix **displayed to Administrators** in `views/users.ejs` claims "Add / Edit Records" is Administrator-only (`[true, false, false]`) — but `POST/PATCH/DELETE /api/partnerships` are gated by `requirePersonnel`, which explicitly allows Auth. Personnel too. The matrix also claims "Export Audit Log" and "View Audit Trail" are Administrator-only, but the actual routes (S4 above) are `requireAuth` — open to every role. The UI is actively misinforming admins about what each role can actually do. | High |
| R2 | Auth. Personnel is backend-authorized to add/edit/delete partnerships but has no navigation path to do so (duplicate of M3, listed here as the permission-design angle). | High |
| R3 | View-Only users have a fully working "Submit New Partnership Request" form baked into their access-request page (`viewonly_access_request.ejs`), posting straight to `POST /api/requests` — which only blocks the `Auth. Personnel` role, not View-Only. This bypasses the entire purpose of the "request access to become Personnel" workflow that page otherwise exists for. | Medium |
| R4 | `/viewonly*` route family uses an exclude-list instead of an allow-list (duplicate of S16, listed here as the permission-design angle). | Medium |
| R5 | Every finding in §1 that involves a `requireAuth`-only route where role-specific data should be scoped (S1–S4, S7) is fundamentally a role/permission-boundary failure, not just a data leak — cross-referenced here rather than re-detailed. | (see §1) |

| # | Finding | Severity |

| F1 | **No Archive workflow for partnerships.** `DELETE /api/partnerships/:id` is a hard, permanent delete — there is no soft-archive/restore mechanism anywhere, despite the admin dashboard's own generated text explicitly treating "archived" as an available action ("...expired partnerships not yet renewed or archived"). This was flagged mid-session as an open design question (replace Delete with Archive, or add Archive alongside Delete?) that was never resolved — still open. | Medium |

# Implementation Roadmap

Ordered so that each step doesn't depend on a later one, and severity/blast-radius decreases as you go down the list. Steps in the same numbered group can be done together (same files/root cause).

### Step 1 — Lock down the unscoped `requireAuth` data-leak routes (S1–S4, S7)
- **What:** Change the middleware on: `GET/PATCH/DELETE /api/notifications*` (add ownership check via `targetEmail`/session email, or restrict the unscoped variants to `requirePersonnel` and keep `/mine` for partners), `GET/POST/PATCH/DELETE /api/calendarevents*` (add ownership/role scoping — decide whether calendar events need per-org scoping like notifications, or are legitimately shared and just need write-protection), `GET /calendar` (`requireAuth` → `requirePersonnel`), `/api/reports/partnerships/pdf|excel` and `/api/reports/activitylog/pdf|excel` and `GET /api/activitylogs` (`requireAuth` → `requirePersonnel` or `requireAdmin` to match the RBAC matrix's stated intent), `GET /api/documents` (add the same ownership filter the file-serving route already has, or restrict to `requirePersonnel`).
- **Files:** `cirl.js` (all route declarations listed above).
- **Why:** These are live, confirmed data leaks reachable by a self-signup View-Only account with zero vetting — the highest-impact issue in the audit.
- **How:** For each route, swap `requireAuth` for the correct role middleware, and for the ones that must stay multi-role (e.g. notifications, documents), add a query filter keyed on `req.session.user.email`/role the same way `/api/partnerships/mine` and `/api/notifications/mine` already do — don't invent a new pattern, copy the existing one.
- **Verify:** Live-test with a real `potential_partner`/View-Only test account (per this session's established methodology): confirm each route now 403s/redirects or returns only that user's own data; confirm the legitimate Admin/Personnel flows still work; run the full Jest suite.

### Step 2 — Fix the NoSQL injection in `buildPartnershipFilter` (S3)
- **What:** Cast/validate each query param to a plain string (or allowlist against known enum values for status/type/cat/region/unit) before it reaches the MongoDB filter.
- **Files:** `cirl.js` (`buildPartnershipFilter`, ~line 1280).
- **Why:** `req.query` fields can be objects via bracket notation (`?status[$ne]=x`), letting a caller inject MongoDB operators into the filter.
- **How:** `if (query.status && typeof query.status === 'string') filter.status = query.status;` (repeat per field), or explicitly `String(query.status)`.
- **Verify:** `curl` the export endpoint with a crafted `?status[$ne]=x` param before/after the fix — confirm it 400s or is treated as a literal string, not an operator.

### Step 3 — Lock down or remove the forgery-capable dead routes (S5, S6)
- **What:** `POST /api/activitylogs` and `POST /api/notifications` — since neither is called by any frontend, either delete them outright or restrict to `requireAdmin` with strict field validation.
- **Files:** `cirl.js:1806-1823, 1826-1837`.
- **Why:** Live, reachable, unauthenticated-by-role forgery vectors against the audit trail and the notification system.
- **How:** Confirm via grep that nothing calls them (already confirmed in this audit), then remove or lock down.
- **Verify:** Grep again post-change to confirm no frontend regression; curl the route as a non-admin, confirm 403/404.

### Step 4 — Fix the Approve/Reject button bug (S9/B1)
- **What:** Change `x._id || x.id` to `x.id` in `resolvePR`/`quickPR`.
- **Files:** `views/administrator/partnership_requests.ejs:969, 994`.
- **Why:** This is the primary admin workflow for approving partnerships, and it currently does nothing at all when clicked.
- **How:** One-line fix per function, matching the correct pattern already used by `openPRDetail` and `resolveAR` in the same file.
- **Verify:** Live-test: log in as admin, click Approve on a real pending request, confirm the network tab shows the PATCH firing and the row updates.

### Step 5 — Retire or fix the legacy `/requests` page (B2, B3)
- **What:** Decide whether `administrator/requests.ejs` should be retired (redirect `/requests` to `/partnership-requests`) now that the hub page covers the same data, or kept and fixed. Given it's already causing false-success toasts and has hardcoded fake stats, retiring it is the lower-risk option.
- **Files:** `cirl.js:2262-2264` (route), `views/partials/sidebar.ejs` (nav link), `views/administrator/requests.ejs` (remove or fix `res.ok` checking + hardcoded `data-target`).
- **Why:** Two admin pages mutating the same collection, one of them silently broken for a role that can reach it, is a maintenance and correctness hazard.
- **How:** If retiring: `app.get('/requests', ...) => res.redirect('/partnership-requests')`, remove the sidebar link. If keeping: fix `resolveRequest()` to check `res.ok`/parse JSON, remove the hardcoded `data-target` attributes.
- **Verify:** Confirm the sidebar no longer shows two "Requests"-ish links doing overlapping things; if redirecting, confirm old bookmarks/links still land somewhere sensible.

### Step 6 — Add status-transition guards to the three request PATCH routes (B4)
- **What:** Before applying a status update, fetch the current document and reject the transition (400) unless the current status is in an allowed source set.
- **Files:** `cirl.js:897-909` (partnership requests), `2101-2112` (access requests), `1077-1089` (document requests).
- **Why:** Prevents double-approval, approve-after-withdrawal races, and duplicate side effects (double partnership extension, duplicate notifications).
- **How:** Same shape for all three: `const target = await db.collection(...).findOne({id}); if (!ALLOWED_FROM[status]?.includes(target.status)) return res.status(400).json(...)`.
- **Verify:** Live-test: approve a request, then attempt to approve/reject it again — confirm the second call is rejected and no duplicate notification/activity-log/partnership-update occurs (check DB directly).

### Step 7 — Fix the three request-subsystem correctness bugs (S8, B5, B6, B7)
- **What:** (a) Force `POST /api/access-requests` to use `req.session.user.email`, never `req.body.email`. (b) On rejecting an already-`Accepted` access request, also revert the user's role (or explicitly block rejecting an already-accepted request with a clear error). (c) Surface `tempPassword` in the admin's access-request review UI when present in the response. (d) Either make "Fulfilled" document requests actually create a Document Library entry, or change the notification text to not claim it does.
- **Files:** `cirl.js:2043-2075` (a), `2101-2160` (b), same block (c), `1077-1121` (d); `views/administrator/partnership_requests.ejs` (c's UI).
- **Why:** (a) is a spoofing/authorization gap; (b) leaves accounts in an inconsistent privileged state; (c) permanently loses new users' login credentials; (d) misleads requesters.
- **How:** (a) one-line change; (b) add a role-revert `updateOne` in the reject branch when `target.status === 'Accepted'`, or add a guard; (c) show `data.tempPassword` in a copyable field in the resolve-modal success state, matching how `POST /api/users`' tempPassword is already surfaced in User Management; (d) simplest is a text fix — change the notification to say the request was fulfilled and to check the Document Library separately, unless you want to build the actual auto-link (bigger scope).
- **Verify:** Live-test each: submit a spoofed-email access request as User A citing User B's email, confirm it's rejected/ignored; approve a new-user access request, confirm the temp password now appears in the UI; reject an Accepted request, confirm the user's role reverts (or the action is blocked with a clear message).

### Step 8 — Sweep the id-mass-assignment pattern (D2)
- **What:** Apply the same `id`/`_id` stripping already done for `users` to `POST /api/calendarevents` and audit `partnerships`/`requests`/`document-requests`/`access-requests` POST routes for the same gap.
- **Files:** `cirl.js` (each POST route's entry-construction line).
- **Why:** A client-supplied `id` can collide with/hijack an existing record's identity.
- **How:** `const { id: _c, _id: _cm, ...safeBody } = req.body;` then build the entry from `safeBody`, same pattern as the `users` fix.
- **Verify:** Repeat the same collision test used for `users` earlier this session (POST with a client-supplied `id` matching a real existing record, confirm the server-assigned id wins and the real record is untouched).

### Step 9 — Fix role/permission consistency (R1, R2, R3, R4)
- **What:** (a) Correct the RBAC matrix display in `users.ejs` to reflect actual enforced permissions (or restrict the routes to match the matrix — needs a product decision on which is the source of truth; recommend updating the matrix since Personnel's broader access appears intentional given the rest of the app's design). (b) Add a Registry nav link to `sidebar_personnel.ejs`. (c) Remove or explicitly gate the hidden "Submit Partnership Request" tab on the View-Only access-request page. (d) Convert `/viewonly*` routes to an allow-list (`role === 'potential_partner' → redirect`, else allow only View-Only/... whatever the intended set is).
- **Files:** `views/users.ejs` (matrix), `views/partials/sidebar_personnel.ejs` (nav), `views/view_only/viewonly_access_request.ejs` + `cirl.js:721-727` (View-Only request-submission block), `cirl.js:2378-2410` (viewonly routes).
- **Why:** Misleading admin-facing documentation, an unreachable authorized feature, and two role-boundary leaks.
- **How:** Straightforward per-item edits; (a) and (c) need a quick product decision on intended behavior before editing (flagging as the one step in this roadmap that benefits from a yes/no from you first).
- **Verify:** Manual walkthrough per role after the change, confirming nav/access matches the (now-corrected) matrix.

### Step 10 — Database indexes (D1)
- **What:** Add `db.collection('users').createIndex({email:1}, {unique:true})`, plus non-unique indexes on the `id` field of every collection that looks up by it, and on `notifications.targetEmail`.
- **Files:** `db.js` (add an index-creation step in `connectDB()`, run once on startup).
- **Why:** Closes the app-level TOCTOU race on email uniqueness, and stops every id-based lookup from being a full collection scan.
- **How:** Call `createIndex` calls right after `client.connect()`, guarded so they're idempotent (Mongo no-ops if the index already exists).
- **Verify:** Restart the app, confirm indexes exist via `db.collection.getIndexes()`; re-run the full Jest suite to confirm no regressions from the new unique constraint (in particular, check there's no pre-existing duplicate-email data that would make index creation fail — audit the `users` collection for duplicates first).

### Step 11 — Hardening pass (S10–S15)
- **What:** Add rate limiting to `/signup`, `/signup/partner`, password-change routes, and `/api/institutions`; change `GET /logout` to `POST /logout` (update the one link that calls it); add basic magic-byte verification to upload middleware (e.g. via `sharp`, already a dependency, or the `file-type` package); add a global 4-arg error handler that logs internally and returns a generic message; add stream-error handling to the PDF/Excel export helpers; add an ownership check to the OCR status route.
- **Files:** `cirl.js` (rate limiters, logout route, global error handler, export helpers), `middleware/avatarUploadMiddleware.js`, `middleware/uploadMiddleware.js`, `controllers/ocrController.js`, any view linking to `/logout`.
- **Why:** Defense-in-depth; none of these are actively-exploited-today Critical issues, but each closes a real gap.
- **How:** Standard `express-rate-limit` instances per route; `sharp(buffer).metadata()` wrapped in try/catch to verify real image content; a trailing `app.use((err,req,res,next) => {...})` in `cirl.js`; `res.headersSent` checks before the catch blocks' `res.status(500)` calls in the export routes.
- **Verify:** Live-test each: hammer `/signup` past the new limit and confirm a 429; confirm logout still works as a POST; upload a renamed non-image file as an avatar and confirm it's now rejected; force a mid-stream export error and confirm no unhandled exception/crash.

### Step 12 — UI/UX and missing-feature cleanup (U1–U5, F1–F3)
- **What:** Fix the `read`/`unread` notification-count field-name bug (one-line); resolve the Archive-workflow design question left open earlier this session and implement it if you want it; add a withdraw/cancel route for access requests; confirm delete-user is intentionally absent or add it; resolve the "Partnership Requests" naming overlap on the admin dashboard.
- **Files:** `cirl.js:1145` (unread fix), `cirl.js` + relevant views (Archive, if pursued), `cirl.js` + `views/view_only/viewonly_access_request.ejs` (withdraw), `views/administrator/admin_dashboard.ejs` (naming).
- **Why:** Smaller, mostly cosmetic/completeness items — correctly last since none of them are blocking or leaking data.
- **How:** Case-by-case, straightforward once Step 9's product decisions are made (Archive workflow in particular still needs your input on Delete-becomes-Archive vs. both-exist, from earlier this session).
- **Verify:** Standard live-test + Jest per item.

---

## Notes on scope and confidence

- Every Critical/High finding above was confirmed by directly reading both sides of the code path (route handler *and* the frontend code that calls it, or the absence of any caller). Nothing here is inferred from naming conventions alone.
- A few items are explicitly marked in the source investigations as needing a live run to fully confirm exact runtime behavior (the PDF/Excel stream-error timing in S14, and the precise client-side JSON-parse failure mode on session-expiry redirects) — these are noted as such rather than asserted with false certainty.
- **Status update (2026-07-17): Roadmap Steps 1, 2, the Phase-4 mass-assignment/schema hardening, and the `GET /api/partnerships` RBAC fix have been implemented and live-verified.** Step 1: the unscoped `requireAuth` data-leak routes — notifications, calendar write access, partnership/activity-log exports, document metadata — are now correctly role/ownership-restricted. Step 2: `buildPartnershipFilter` now uses an explicit field allowlist with a `typeof value === 'string'` guard, closing the NoSQL operator-injection vector. Mass-assignment hardening: `POST/PATCH /api/partnerships` and `PATCH /api/notifications/:id` now use explicit field allowlists (reject-unknown-field + type + enum validation), `partnerships` has a single canonical `inst` field (the `institution`/`partner` aliases are no longer accepted), and `GET /api/partnerships/mine` now matches on `inst` only. `GET /api/partnerships` (the bare, unscoped feed flagged Critical in Phase 5): re-investigated before fixing — the original finding incorrectly lumped `potential_partner` in with View-Only; `potential_partner`'s access is actually load-bearing (`partner_dashboard.ejs`'s map/insights depend on it) and was preserved, while View-Only (which has zero legitimate callers) is now correctly blocked, via `requireUploader` (reused, not duplicated). The remaining Roadmap steps (3 and onward — dead-route lockdown, request-subsystem status-transition guards, DB indexes, hardening pass) and the other Phase 5 findings (Personnel's missing Registry nav link and wrong-sidebar notification link, View-Only's fake dashboard, View-Only's document-metadata exposure, the RBAC-matrix display mismatch) are still pending. The rest of this document reflects the original, pre-implementation state except where superseded by the work above.
- Implementation proceeds step-by-step per this session's established `ralph-loop` discipline (smallest logical change → static check → live verification with real test data → cleanup → regression run) rather than batching multiple roadmap steps into one change.
