# Phase 2 — Complete Audit (2026-07-17)

Reorganizes every finding above (cross-referenced by its original code, e.g. B1/S9) into the requested taxonomy, plus newly-confirmed items from a targeted follow-up pass (marked **NEW**). No code was changed while producing this section.

## Missing Features

### Missing Pages
- No self-service "Forgot Password" page. **NEW.** The login page (`views/index.ejs`) has no such link anywhere — confirmed via full-text search. The only related UI text is the login error "Your account needs a password reset. Please contact the Administrator," shown for legacy plain-text accounts. There is no password-recovery page, route, or email flow of any kind.
- No Archive/Archived-partnerships view (F1) — follows directly from there being no archive feature at all.

### Missing Workflows
- **Password recovery.** **NEW.** No forgot-password flow exists anywhere in `cirl.js` (confirmed via full-text search for "forgot"/"reset"). Any user who forgets their password has no self-service path — an Administrator must manually issue a new one via User Management. For a system intended to be "production-ready," this is a real gap, though its urgency depends on how large/self-service you want the user base to be.
- **Archive workflow for partnerships** (F1) — `DELETE /api/partnerships/:id` is a hard, permanent delete; no soft-archive/restore exists, despite the admin dashboard's own generated text treating "archived" as an available action. Still an open design question from earlier this session (Delete-becomes-Archive vs. both-exist alongside each other).
- **Document deletion.** **NEW.** There is no `DELETE` route for the `documents` collection at all (confirmed: the only `app.delete('/api/...')` routes in the whole app are `requests`, `document-requests`, `partnerships`, `users`, `notifications`, `calendarevents` — documents is absent from that list), and no delete button anywhere in `views/administrator/documents.ejs`. Once a document is OCR'd/uploaded into the Library, it can never be removed by any role, including Administrator — only its metadata can be corrected (`PATCH /api/documents/:id`).
- **Access-request withdraw/cancel** (F2) — a submitted role-upgrade request cannot be cancelled by the requester, unlike the other two request types.

### Missing CRUD Operations
| Collection | Create | Read | Update | Delete |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `documents` | ✅ (OCR upload) | ✅ | ✅ (metadata correction only) | ❌ **no route, no UI, any role — NEW** |
| `users` | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ⚠️ backend route exists (`DELETE /api/users/:id`), **no UI** — likely intentional (deactivate is the safer pattern) but unconfirmed as a deliberate decision (F3) |
| `accessRequests` | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ (approve/reject) | ❌ no withdraw/cancel, requester or admin (F2) |

### Unfinished Modules
- **Archive/Archived-records module** — referenced in generated UI copy (admin dashboard's "not yet been renewed or archived" text), zero backend or frontend implementation anywhere (F1).
- **Password recovery module** — implicitly referenced by the "contact the Administrator" error message, no actual self-service flow built (see above, **NEW**).

---

## Broken Features

### Broken Pages
- **`administrator/requests.ejs`** (`/requests`, the legacy admin request-review page — B2/B3). Broken two ways on the same page: (1) shows false "approved/rejected successfully" toasts, because `resolveRequest()` never checks `res.ok`/parses the JSON body — when a non-admin Auth. Personnel user (who can reach this `requirePersonnel`-gated page, but not the underlying `requireAdmin`-gated API) clicks Approve/Reject, the request 302-redirects and `fetch` reports that as a success; (2) the five big stat cards permanently display hardcoded fake numbers (`data-target="15/5/5/3/2"`) — `initCounters()` overwrites the correctly-computed real numbers that `updateCounts()` just set, moments earlier, on page load. The page visibly contradicts itself: the stat cards show fake totals while the filter-tab badges directly below them show the real ones.

### Broken Buttons
- **Approve / Reject buttons on the Partnership Requests hub** (`views/administrator/partnership_requests.ejs` — B1/S9). Clicking either does *nothing at all*: no network request, no error, no visible change. Root cause, precisely, at three specific lines:
  - Lines 969 and 994 (`resolvePR()`, `quickPR()`): `prData.find(x => String(x._id || x.id) === uid)`. Every document returned by `GET /api/requests` includes MongoDB's own `_id` (an ObjectId string, always truthy), so `x._id || x.id` **always** evaluates to `x._id` — never `x.id`, the app's numeric id that `uid` was actually built from (`String(r.id || i)` at line 803). The `.find()` therefore never matches any row; `r` is `undefined`; both functions hit `if (!r) return;` and exit before the API call at line 974 is ever reached.
  - Line 974 has the **same bug a second time**, independently: `fetch('/api/requests/' + (r._id || r.id), ...)`. Even after fixing the `.find()` lookup above, this line would still build the request URL from `r._id` (the ObjectId string) instead of `r.id` — `parseInt()` on the backend (`cirl.js:897`) would parse a garbled/truncated number out of the ObjectId string's leading digits rather than the intended id. **Both occurrences need the same one-line fix, or the button will still silently hit the wrong record after only a partial fix.**
  - Confirmed isolated to this one file/workflow — grepped the entire `views/` tree for the same `_id || id` pattern; no other occurrences exist.

### Broken Forms
- No confirmed broken form submissions were found anywhere in the app. Every POST/PATCH body checked against its backend handler (`/api/users`, `/api/partnerships`, `/api/requests`, `/api/calendarevents`, `/api/notifications`, `/api/document-requests`, the admin/personnel/partner profile forms) sends exactly the fields the backend reads — no silently-dropped or misnamed fields (M1). This is a genuinely clean area of the codebase.

### Broken Modals
- The Partnership Requests **detail/review modal** (same file as above) opens and displays data correctly — it is not broken as a modal. Its Approve/Reject buttons inside the modal call the identical `resolvePR()`/`quickPR()` functions as the table's quick-action buttons, so they fail for the exact same reason listed above. Not a separate bug — the same root cause, reachable from two different UI entry points.

### Broken APIs
- **`GET /api/notifications/unread-count`** (B8) — for every role except `potential_partner`, the query is `countDocuments({ read: false })`, but every notification document in the app actually uses the field name `unread` (confirmed at the insert sites, the update sites, and the frontend's `n.unread` reads) — there is no `read` field anywhere, so this always returns `0`. The notification bell badge is permanently stuck at zero for Administrator, Auth. Personnel, and View-Only.
- **`GET /api/dashboard/stats`** (B9) — filters on lowercase status strings (`'active'`, `'pending'`, `'approved'`, `'rejected'`, `'expiring'`, `'expired'`) while every status field the rest of the app writes is capitalized (`'Active'`, `'Pending'`, etc.) — would always return all-zero counts. Currently harmless only because no frontend calls this route (confirmed dead code).
- **`POST /api/activitylogs`** and **`POST /api/notifications`** (S5/S6) — not "broken" in the sense of malfunctioning; broken in the sense of doing exactly what an attacker would want and nothing an admin intended: neither is called by any frontend, both are reachable by any authenticated user regardless of role, and both accept arbitrary unvalidated `req.body` — letting any logged-in user forge fake audit-log entries or spoofed notifications.
- **`DELETE /api/calendarevents/:id`** and **`DELETE /api/notifications/:id`** (B10) — never check `deletedCount`; both always respond `{success:true}`, even when the id didn't match anything and nothing was deleted.

### Broken Workflows
- **All three request-review workflows** (Partnership, Access, Document — B4). None of the three admin-facing `PATCH .../:id` endpoints checks the request's *current* status before applying a new one. Concretely reproducible: a request can be approved twice (re-running every side effect a second time — for a renewal request, this means a second, duplicate partnership-extension update); rejected after already being approved; or approved by an admin racing a user's own withdrawal, silently overriding the withdrawal.
- **Access-request approval for a brand-new user** (B6) — the server generates a one-time `tempPassword` and includes it in the API response (`cirl.js:2156`), but no frontend anywhere reads or displays that field (confirmed: zero matches for `tempPassword` across all of `views/`). The account is created, a random password is generated for it, and that password is then permanently unrecoverable through the app — there is no way for the admin to actually hand the new Auth. Personnel user their login credentials.
- **Access-request rejection after acceptance** (B5) — rejecting a request whose status is already `Accepted` (meaning the user already has the promoted role and is using it) flips the request's status to `Rejected` but never touches the `users` collection — the account silently keeps its `Auth. Personnel` role forever, with no corresponding UI indication that this state is inconsistent.
- **Document-request "Fulfilled" notification** (B7) — tells the requester their document "has been fulfilled and added to the Document Library," but no code performs that action; it's purely a status flip on the request record. The admin must separately, manually upload the actual file through the unrelated Document Library upload flow — the notification overpromises what actually happened.

---

## What's genuinely fine (confirmed, not just unchecked)
- No field-name mismatches on any checked form (Broken Forms, above).
- No orphaned view templates — every `.ejs` page has a matching route.
- No other occurrences of the `_id || id` bug pattern anywhere else in the codebase.

---

# Phase 3 — Frontend↔Backend Mismatches & Security Issues (2026-07-17)

No code was changed while producing this section. Two items below were newly checked this pass and are marked **NEW**; everything else reorganizes and cross-references findings already in this document.

## Frontend ↔ Backend Mismatches

### Routes that no longer exist
**None found.** The full-project `fetch()` inventory (every `.ejs` view plus every custom file under `assets/js/pages/`) was cross-referenced against every backend route; every frontend call resolves to a real, existing route. Confirmed exhaustively, not sampled.

### Frontend calling wrong APIs
**None found as a distinct issue.** The nearest relative is the Approve/Reject button bug (Broken Buttons, above) — it targets the *correct* endpoint (`/api/requests/:id`), but a lookup bug means the call never actually fires. That's a broken button, not a wrong-API call.

### Missing endpoints
- `DELETE /api/documents/:id` doesn't exist (ties to the Missing CRUD finding) — not currently a frontend problem since no delete button exists yet either, but would need to be built first if that feature is added.
- The `tempPassword` field returned by access-request approval has no frontend consumer — not a missing *endpoint* (the data already comes back from the existing route), just a missing UI read of it. Already covered under Broken Workflows.

### Incorrect payloads
**None found.** Confirmed field-for-field on `/api/users`, `/api/partnerships`, `/api/requests` (+ `/edit`, `/submit`), `/api/calendarevents`, `/api/notifications`, `/api/document-requests`, `/api/access-requests`, and all three profile-update forms (admin/personnel/partner).

### Unused endpoints
- `GET /api/me`
- `GET /api/dashboard/stats` (also internally broken — filters on lowercase status strings that never match anything real)
- `POST /api/lifecycle/recompute`
- `POST /api/activitylogs`
- `POST /api/notifications`
- `DELETE /api/notifications/:id` — no delete/trash UI exists anywhere for notifications; this route got an ownership check in Step 1 as a defensive fix (it's live and reachable even though unused), but remains uncalled by any current frontend.
- `DELETE /api/users/:id` — no delete-user UI exists (User Management only supports Add/Edit/Deactivate).

### Dead code
Same list as Unused Endpoints, from the backend side — each is a fully-built route with zero callers. Frontend-side dead code (unused JS functions within view `<script>` blocks) was not exhaustively swept this pass; flag if you want that specific check done.

---

## Security Issues

### Authentication
- Login, signup, and Google OAuth all correctly call `req.session.regenerate()` on success (session-fixation-safe).
- `verifyPassword()` correctly refuses to compare against a non-bcrypt value rather than doing a raw string comparison, so legacy/malformed password fields can't be brute-forced via timing or accidentally matched.
- No rate limiting beyond `/login` (S11, unchanged) — `/signup` and the three password-change routes remain open to spam/brute-force. (`/signup/partner` no longer exists — removed 2026-07-18, see Status Tracker.)
- No self-service password recovery exists (Phase 2 Missing Features) — not a vulnerability per se, but the only recovery path is an Administrator manually resetting via User Management.

### Authorization / RBAC
- S1–S4 and S7 (notifications, calendar, partnership/activity-log exports, document metadata) — **fixed in Step 1**, live-verified.
- Still open: R1 (the RBAC matrix displayed in `users.ejs` doesn't match what's actually enforced), R2 (Auth. Personnel has no nav path to the Registry despite being backend-authorized), R3 (View-Only can submit full partnership requests via a hidden tab, bypassing the access-upgrade gate), R4/S16 (`/viewonly*` routes use an exclude-list instead of an allow-list, so `potential_partner` can reach them directly).

### Session handling
- `httpOnly`, `sameSite: 'lax'`, `secure` in production, `rolling: true`, session regenerated on every login path — all correct.
- The global session-revalidation middleware (added earlier this session, before this audit began) re-checks the live `users` record on every request — deactivation, deletion, and role changes now take effect immediately rather than only at next login. Re-confirmed correct while reviewing this area again.
- Default in-memory `MemoryStore` (no MongoStore/Redis) — sessions don't survive a restart and won't scale across multiple processes. Not a vulnerability at current single-process scale; an operational limitation to know about before any horizontal scaling.

### Password security
- bcrypt at cost factor 12 — appropriate.
- The 8-char/upper/lower/digit policy is enforced server-side consistently everywhere a password is set, including the three self-service change-password routes (fixed earlier this session to key off session `id` rather than the mutable `email`).
- No password-reuse history or breach-list checking (e.g., a k-anonymity HaveIBeenPwned check) — reasonable to omit at this scale, noted for completeness only.

### Validation
- The `role`/`status` allowlisting and `id`/`_id` mass-assignment stripping done earlier this session only covers `users`. The identical `{id: nextId, ...req.body}` gap still exists, unfixed, on `calendarevents` (D2), and was never checked against `partnerships`, `document-requests`, or `access-requests` — still open.
- `partnerships` and `calendarevents` accept arbitrary extra fields via unrestricted `req.body` spreading, with no schema validation of any kind (by design, given the schemaless native-driver approach) — not itself a vulnerability, but means a malformed or unexpected field can silently attach to a record with no server-side complaint.

### File uploads
- S12, unchanged: mimetype/extension-only validation (not magic-byte) on both avatar and OCR upload middleware. Path traversal is correctly prevented via server-generated filenames. Size limits are correctly enforced.

### MongoDB queries / Injection risks
- **S3's NoSQL injection in `buildPartnershipFilter` — fixed (Step 2, 2026-07-17).** The function now iterates a fixed `PARTNERSHIP_FILTER_FIELDS` allowlist and only accepts a value for each field if `typeof value === 'string' && value.length > 0`. Since Express's query parser (`qs`) always turns bracket/dot-notation input into an object or array rather than a string, this one check rejects every operator-injection shape without needing to pattern-match `$`/`.`/array syntax individually. Live-verified with real MongoDB operator payloads (`?status[$ne]=Active`, `?status[$exists]=true`, `?category[$in][]=A`, `?institution[$regex]=.*`) against real test data — each behaved identically to sending no filter at all, proving the operators were neutralized rather than executed. Legitimate filters (`?status=Active`, `?status=Expired`) still scope correctly.
- Every other query reviewed either uses a hardcoded field name with a properly-typed value, or is incidentally protected by a `.trim()`/`.toLowerCase()` call that would throw on a non-string (object-typed) input before it ever reached MongoDB (login, signup, the `users` routes). No other injection vectors found.

### Ownership checks
- Fixed in Step 1: notifications per-id PATCH/DELETE (staff-or-own), documents metadata list (partner sees only their own uploads).
- Calendar events write access is now staff-only rather than ownership-checked — correct for a shared institutional calendar (there's no "owner" to check against), not a gap.

### CSRF
- S10, unchanged: no CSRF token anywhere; `sameSite: 'lax'` is a real but partial mitigation. `GET /logout` remains a state-changing GET (minor logout-CSRF exposure).

### Rate limiting
- S11, unchanged: only `/login` is rate-limited.

### Data leaks
- S1–S4, S7 — fixed for the *access-control* side in Step 1. S3's injection angle (see MongoDB queries above) is now also fixed as of Step 2.

### Privilege escalation
- **NEW, checked this pass, confirmed clean:** none of the three request-submission endpoints (`POST /api/requests`, `POST /api/document-requests`, `POST /api/access-requests`) let a client set their own `status` field — all three hardcode it server-side (`'Draft'`/`'Pending'`) and build the inserted document from an explicit field allowlist, never a raw `{...req.body}` spread of the whole body. `PATCH /api/requests/:id/edit` (the one self-service edit route) also uses an explicit allowlist that excludes `status`. There is no path for a `potential_partner` to self-approve their own partnership request, or for Auth. Personnel/View-Only to self-approve a document/access request.
- **NEW, checked this pass, confirmed clean:** none of the three profile-update routes (`/api/admin/profile`, `/api/personnel/profile`, `/api/partner/profile`) ever reads or sets a `role` field from the client — confirmed via a targeted grep across `cirl.js`. No self-promotion path exists through profile editing.
- The one real escalation-*adjacent* issue remains S8: a spoofed `email` on `POST /api/access-requests` could get someone else's account promoted — but only if an admin approves the spoofed request without noticing the mismatch. This requires human error in the loop; it is not a direct, automatic escalation exploit.

---

# Phase 4 — Database Issues (2026-07-17)

No code was changed while producing this section. New findings from a fresh systematic pass are marked **NEW**; everything else cross-references what's already in this document.

### Collections
10 collections are actively read/written by the live code (confirmed via `db.collection('...')` grep across `cirl.js` and `services/`): `users`, `partnerships`, `requests`, `documentrequests`, `accessRequests`, `documents`, `notifications`, `calendarevents`, `activitylogs`, `profiles`. A 11th, `agreements`, exists only in the DB backup and is referenced nowhere in code (see Dead Collections).

### Indexes
**None exist anywhere.** `db.js` only opens a connection (`client.connect()`) — there is not a single `createIndex` call in the entire codebase. Every query in the app, including every lookup-by-`id` (used by virtually every PATCH/DELETE/detail-fetch route across all 10 collections) and every ownership-scoped query (`targetEmail`, `uploadedByEmail`, `submittedByEmail`, `requestedByEmail`), runs as a full collection scan. Harmless at current data volume; a real cost as any collection grows past a few thousand documents. (D1, restated here as the canonical Indexes finding.)

### Schema consistency
- **NEW.** The `partnerships` collection has no enforced schema (see Mass Assignment below), and the code defends against it: four separate `logActivity()` calls (lines 914, 1224, 1238, 1251) read the institution name as `entry.inst || entry.institution || entry.partner`, implying the collection has historically stored this under three different field names depending on which code path created the record. **Checked against the actual current data** (`Database_backup/ciprms.partnerships.json`, 27 records): every single record uses `inst`; zero use `institution` or `partner`. So this is not an active data-integrity bug today — but nothing *prevents* a future insert from using a different key (see Mass Assignment), and `GET /api/partnerships/mine`'s ownership match only checks `inst`/`institution` (`$or: [{inst:...}, {institution:...}]`) — it does **not** check `partner`. If a record were ever created with only a `partner` field, it would silently vanish from a partner's own "my partnerships" view. Currently dormant, but a real latent gap directly caused by the lack of field validation on `POST /api/partnerships`.
- No other field-naming inconsistencies were found within any other collection.

### Duplicate risks
- `users.email` — no unique index; uniqueness is enforced only by an application-level check-then-insert in `POST /api/users`, `PATCH /api/users/:id`, and `/signup`. Classic TOCTOU race: two near-simultaneous requests can both pass the existence check before either commits (D1). (`/signup/partner` had the identical pattern but no longer exists — removed 2026-07-18, see Status Tracker.)
- **NEW.** The same class of risk exists for "one active request per institution" — `POST /api/requests` checks `findOne({institution, status: {$in: ['Pending','Under Review']}})` before inserting, and `POST /api/requests/:id/submit` does the equivalent check before flipping a Draft to Pending — both are check-then-insert with no unique index backing them, so two concurrent submissions for the same institution could both slip through. Lower real-world likelihood than the email race (requires two genuinely concurrent requests for the *same* institution name), but the same underlying pattern.
- No duplicate-prevention check of any kind exists on `document-requests` or `calendarevents` — not necessarily a bug (there's no obvious "should be unique" constraint for either), just noting the asymmetry.

### Mass assignment
- **Fixed already (`users`, earlier this session):** `POST /api/users` and `PATCH /api/users/:id` now strip client-supplied `id`/`_id` before use.
- **Still open, confirmed this pass via a full sweep of every collection's write routes:**
  - **`POST /api/partnerships`** (`cirl.js:1222`, **NEW** confirmation) — `{ id: nextId, ...req.body }`. A client-supplied `id` in the body silently overrides the server-computed one. Reachable by both Administrator and Auth. Personnel.
  - **`PATCH /api/partnerships/:id`** (`cirl.js:1235`, **NEW** confirmation) — `updateOne({id}, {$set: req.body})`, completely unrestricted. A client can `$set` the record's own `id` field to an arbitrary value (renaming/colliding its identity) or attach any arbitrary extra field, since there is zero schema validation on this collection.
  - **`POST /api/calendarevents`** (`cirl.js:1941`) and **`PATCH /api/calendarevents/:id`** (`cirl.js:1953`) — same two patterns, already flagged (D2), now confirmed as the same `{id: nextId, ...req.body}` / raw `{$set: req.body}` shapes. Both routes are `requirePersonnel`-gated since Step 1, which reduces exposure to trusted roles, but doesn't close the gap.
  - **`POST /api/notifications`** (`cirl.js:1849`) and **`POST /api/activitylogs`** (`cirl.js:1829`) — same `{id: nextId, ...req.body}` shape. Already flagged (S5/S6) as forgery-capable dead routes pending Roadmap Step 3 (lockdown/removal); the id-collision angle is a second, independent reason to fix or remove them, not just the forgery angle.
  - **`PATCH /api/notifications/:id`** (`cirl.js:1899`, **NEW** confirmation) — `updateOne({id}, {$set: req.body})`, unrestricted. Now behind Step 1's ownership check (caller must own the notification or be staff), which meaningfully limits the blast radius, but a caller who legitimately owns/manages a notification could still use this to rename its `id` or overwrite fields like `targetEmail`.
  - **Confirmed clean:** `POST /api/requests`, `PATCH /api/requests/:id/edit`, `POST /api/requests/:id/submit`, `PATCH /api/requests/:id` (admin approve), `POST /api/document-requests`, `PATCH /api/document-requests/:id`, `POST /api/access-requests`, `PATCH /api/access-requests/:id` — every one of these builds its update/insert from either explicit destructured variables or an explicit field allowlist, never a raw `{...req.body}` spread or unrestricted `$set`.

### Performance
- No indexes (see above) means every `id`-based lookup across all 10 collections is a full collection scan — fine today, will degrade linearly as each collection grows.
- `filterByDateRange()` (`cirl.js`, used by the partnership PDF/Excel export routes) fetches the *entire* already-status/type/cat/region/unit-filtered result set into memory and then does date-range filtering in JavaScript, because `end` is stored as a formatted display string (e.g. `"Apr 12, 2026"`) rather than a real `Date` — this is a pre-existing, explicitly-commented design tradeoff, reasonable at current scale (a few dozen partnerships), but means date-range filtering can never be pushed down to MongoDB without a schema change (storing a real `Date` alongside the display string).
- No pagination exists anywhere — every list endpoint (`GET /api/partnerships`, `/api/requests`, `/api/activitylogs`, etc.) returns its entire collection in one response. Fine at current scale; would need addressing before the data volume gets large.

### Missing indexes
Concrete list of what should exist, derived directly from the query patterns actually used in the code:
- `users.email` — **unique**. Closes the Duplicate Risks TOCTOU race and speeds up every login.
- `{collection}.id` — non-unique, on every one of the 10 collections. Backs every PATCH/DELETE/detail-fetch route.
- `notifications.targetEmail` — backs `/api/notifications/mine` and the unread-count query.
- `documents.uploadedByEmail` — backs `/api/documents/mine` and the Step-1 ownership filter on `/api/documents`.
- `requests.submittedByEmail`, `documentrequests.requestedByEmail` — back their respective `/mine` routes.
- `requests` compound index on `{institution, status}` — backs the duplicate-active-request check on both `POST /api/requests` and `POST /api/requests/:id/submit`, and would let that check also serve as a real uniqueness constraint if made a partial unique index (only enforcing uniqueness for `status: {$in: ['Pending','Under Review']}`).

### Dead collections
- **`agreements`** — present in `Database_backup/ciprms.agreements.json`, referenced nowhere in any code file. Structurally a superseded predecessor of `partnerships` (same `inst`/`type`/`cat`/`start`/`expiry`/`status` shape, fewer fields). Confirmed dead, not just unreferenced-but-planned (D3).

### Unused data
- The `agreements` collection in its entirety (see above).
- **NEW.** The `profiles` collection's `{role: 'admin'}` and `{role: 'personnel'}` documents each carry a `password` field (e.g. `"password": "admin123"`, visible in `Database_backup/ciprms.profiles.json`) that is never read or written by any current code — confirmed via a targeted grep across `cirl.js` for any `profiles`+`password` combination. Real authentication is exclusively against `users.password` (bcrypt-hashed). This field is inert leftover data, not a live vulnerability, but it's the kind of thing that looks alarming to anyone inspecting the database directly (a plaintext-looking password sitting next to a real, hashed one) and is worth deleting for hygiene.
- The file-serving route for documents, the OCR pipeline's failure handling, and the avatar-upload size/path-traversal protections are all correctly implemented (see the original Reports/OCR/Avatar findings above).

---

# Phase 5 — Role Permission Issues, Per Role (2026-07-17)

No code was changed while producing this section. Built by cross-referencing the current, post-fix route table (117 routes, re-pulled fresh from `cirl.js` this pass) against every sidebar's actual nav links, for all four roles. New findings marked **NEW**; everything else cross-references what's already in this document.

## Administrator
**Clean.** Every `requireAdmin`-gated page and API is correctly linked in `sidebar.ejs`, and Administrator passes every `requirePersonnel`/`requireAuth` check too, so nothing is unreachable. No inconsistencies found for this role.

## Auth. Personnel
- **R2 (restated).** Backend-authorized to add/edit/delete partnerships (`POST/PATCH/DELETE /api/partnerships` and the `/registry` page are all `requirePersonnel`), but `sidebar_personnel.ejs` has no link to `/registry` anywhere. A fully-granted permission with no way to reach it through navigation.
- **NEW.** The topbar user-dropdown's "Notifications" link (`views/partials/sidebar_personnel.ejs:53`) points to `/notifications` — the generic, unprefixed route — instead of `/personnel/notifications`. That route (`cirl.js:2405`) renders `administrator/notifications.ejs` **without** passing a `sidebarPartial` option, and that view's sidebar-include defaults to `'sidebar'` (the Administrator sidebar) whenever `sidebarPartial` is undefined. So clicking "Notifications" from a Personnel account's own topbar dropdown lands them on a page wearing the **Administrator's** navigation chrome, not their own. Not a data leak — the underlying `GET /api/notifications` is correctly `requirePersonnel`-gated since Step 1, and every link in that wrong sidebar still individually enforces its own permissions — but it's a genuine, confusing navigation bug: a Personnel user briefly sees admin-only nav items (User Management, the Partnership Requests hub, Admin Settings) that they cannot actually use.
- **R1 (restated).** The RBAC matrix in `views/users.ejs`, shown to every Administrator, claims Personnel cannot "Add/Edit Records" — directly contradicted by the above.
- The legacy `/requests` page remains reachable by Personnel (`requirePersonnel`) while its mutating API is `requireAdmin`-only (B2) — restated here as: Personnel can *open* this page but every action on it silently fails via the false-success-toast bug already documented.

## View-Only
- **NEW, and the most significant finding in this section.** View-Only's entire dashboard (`views/view_only/viewonly_dashboard.ejs`) shows **100% fabricated data**, not real data from any database:
  - The four stat cards (`Active`/`Expiring`/`Expired`/`Total`, lines 55-140) read `typeof stats !== 'undefined' ? stats.X : <hardcoded fallback>` — but `GET /viewonly/dashboard` (`cirl.js:2523-2533`) only ever passes `{activePage, user}` to `res.render()`, **never** a `stats` object. The condition is therefore always false, and the hardcoded fallback numbers (37 active / 9 expiring / 5 expired / 51 total) are the *only* numbers this page has ever shown, regardless of what's actually in the `partnerships` collection.
  - The "Top Partners" list (line 300-306) is a hardcoded inline array of exactly 7 countries with fabricated counts (Japan 12, USA 9, South Korea 7, Philippines 6, China 5, Australia 4, Germany 3) — not derived from any query.
  - The interactive world map (line 525-539) plots a hardcoded array of 14 specific, real-sounding universities (Ateneo de Manila, Harvard, MIT, Osaka University, etc.) with fabricated coordinates and statuses — again, entirely disconnected from the real `partnerships` collection.
  - Per the RBAC matrix, "View Partnerships" is View-Only's one defining, granted permission (`[true, true, true]` — all three documented roles get it). Their own dashboard is the only place this role would exercise that permission, and it shows nothing real. Functionally, View-Only cannot actually view partnerships through the feature built for exactly that purpose.
- **R3 (restated).** `/viewonly/request-access` includes a hidden "Partnership Request" tab that posts directly to `POST /api/requests`, which only blocks the `Auth. Personnel` role (`cirl.js:723`) — View-Only is not blocked, letting them submit and have reviewed a full new-partnership request despite the role's read-only design intent.
- **Residual from Phase 3, restated precisely.** `/documents` (`cirl.js:2409`) is `requireAuth` — reachable by View-Only even though it's not linked in their sidebar. `GET /api/documents`'s ownership filter (`cirl.js:1171-1182`) only scopes `role === 'potential_partner'`; for View-Only the filter is `{}` — fully unscoped. A View-Only user who navigates to `/documents` directly sees every organization's document metadata (title, institution, uploader email, file link).
- **R4/S16 (restated).** `/viewonly`, `/viewonly/dashboard`, and `/viewonly/request-access` (`cirl.js:2515-2547`) each individually re-implement the same exclude-list check (`if (role === 'Administrator' || role === 'Auth. Personnel') redirect`) instead of a shared allow-list — meaning any *other* role (today, `potential_partner`) that isn't explicitly excluded can reach them.
- **Confirmed correctly fixed since Step 1:** `/calendar` and `/reports` are now `requirePersonnel`, so View-Only is correctly blocked from both (previously reachable).

## Potential Partner
- **RESOLVED (2026-07-17) — but the original finding was partially wrong, corrected before fixing.** `GET /api/partnerships` (`cirl.js:594-603`, the bare/unscoped feed) was flagged above as exposing every org's data to `potential_partner`. Before implementing a fix, a fresh trace of every frontend caller of this exact route turned up `views/potential_partner/partner_dashboard.ejs:636`, which this audit's earlier passes had missed. That file's own code comment states the explicit design intent: *"/api/partnerships and /api/partnerships/stats are the shared, read-only registry of established institutional partnerships (public record — safe for any authenticated role)."* The partner dashboard's map, top-countries widget, expiring-table, and insights panel all depend on the **full** list, not just `/mine`. So `potential_partner`'s access to this route is intentional and load-bearing, not an oversight — the audit's "Critical" rating for this role was incorrect, and restricting it to `requirePersonnel` (the originally-implied fix) would have broken a real, working feature.
  What *was* correctly identified: **View-Only has zero confirmed legitimate use of this route** — its dashboard (`viewonly_dashboard.ejs`) makes no `fetch()` calls of any kind (it's the hardcoded-data page described above), and its other page (`viewonly_access_request.ejs`) only calls `/api/access-requests*` and `/api/requests*`, never `/api/partnerships`.
  **Fix implemented:** `GET /api/partnerships`'s middleware changed from `requireAuth` to `requireUploader` — an existing middleware (already used to gate the OCR upload routes) whose semantics are exactly "Administrator OR Auth. Personnel OR potential_partner, View-Only excluded." No new authorization logic was written; this reuses the one existing middleware that already matches the verified-correct access set. **Now protected:** `GET /api/partnerships` only (the one route in scope for this fix — `GET /api/partnerships/mine`, `GET /api/partnerships/stats`, and all `/api/reports/partnerships/*` were already correctly scoped/gated by earlier steps and were not touched).
  **Live-verified:** Administrator, Auth. Personnel, and potential_partner all still receive `200` with the full real dataset (confirmed 28 real records returned, not empty); View-Only now receives a `302` redirect. `GET /api/partnerships/mine` re-confirmed unaffected. All five relevant dashboard/report pages (`/dashboard`, `/personnel/reports`, `/partner/dashboard`, plus `/viewonly/dashboard` for completeness) still return `200`. `npm test` → 26/26, no regressions. Only `cirl.js` was modified.
- **R4/S16 cross-reference.** `potential_partner` can still reach `/viewonly/dashboard` directly — unrelated to the fix above, still open.
- Every `/partner/*` page and `/api/partner/*`/`/api/*/mine` API is correctly `requirePartner`-gated and correctly linked in `sidebar_partner.ejs` — no missing or extra nav links found. Correctly blocked from every Administrator/Personnel-only page and API (`/registry`, `/lifecycle`, `/requests`, `/reports`, `/calendar`, `/documents`, `/users`, `/dashboard`, `/partnership-requests`, all `/admin/*`) — all correctly `requirePersonnel`/`requireAdmin`-gated with partner excluded. This role's own page set is otherwise clean.

## Summary table

| Role | New/confirmed-still-open issue | Severity |
|---|---|---|
| Administrator | None | — |
| Auth. Personnel | No nav link to an authorized page (Registry) | High |
| Auth. Personnel | Topbar "Notifications" link renders the wrong (Admin) sidebar — **NEW** | Medium |
| Auth. Personnel | RBAC matrix display contradicts real permissions | High |
| View-Only | Dashboard is 100% hardcoded/fake data — core permission is non-functional — **NEW** | High |
| View-Only | Can submit full Partnership Requests, bypassing the access-upgrade gate | Medium |
| View-Only | Can see every org's document metadata via direct `/documents` navigation | High |
| View-Only | Reachable by other roles due to exclude-list instead of allow-list | Medium |
| Potential Partner | ~~`GET /api/partnerships` unscoped exposure~~ — **RESOLVED 2026-07-17** (was `requireAuth`, now `requireUploader`; audit's original scope was corrected — the leak was View-Only-only, not potential_partner) | ~~Critical~~ Resolved |
| Potential Partner | Can reach View-Only's dashboard directly | Low |

# Phase 6 — UI/UX Problems (2026-07-17)

No code was changed while producing this section. New findings from a fresh pass are marked **NEW**; everything else cross-references what's already in this document, reorganized under the categories requested.

### Hardcoded data
- **View-Only's entire dashboard** — stat cards (fallback numbers 37/9/5/51), a 7-country "Top Partners" list, and a 14-university interactive map, none connected to the real `partnerships` collection (Phase 5, full detail there).
- **`administrator/requests.ejs`'s stat cards** — hardcoded `data-target="15/5/5/3/2"` permanently overwrite the real computed counts on page load (B3).

### Incorrect counters
- **Notification bell badge** — stuck at `0` for Administrator, Auth. Personnel, and View-Only. `GET /api/notifications/unread-count` queries `{read: false}`, but every notification document actually uses the field name `unread` — the query never matches anything real (B8).
- **`administrator/requests.ejs`'s stat cards vs. its own filter-tab badges** — the same page shows two different counts for the same data: fake hardcoded numbers on the stat cards, correct live numbers on the tab badges directly below them (B3, same root cause as the Hardcoded Data entry above, listed separately here because the *symptom* is a visibly wrong counter, not just stale content).
- **View-Only's stat cards** — same shape of bug as `administrator/requests.ejs`, but 100% of the time rather than as an animation-overwrite race (Phase 5).

### Broken navigation
- **Auth. Personnel: no sidebar link to `/registry`**, despite being backend-authorized to add/edit/delete partnerships there (R2).
- **NEW.** Auth. Personnel's topbar "Notifications" quick-link points to `/notifications` instead of `/personnel/notifications`, landing on a page rendered with the **Administrator's** sidebar instead of their own (traced in Phase 5: that route never passes `sidebarPartial`, which defaults to the admin sidebar).
- **Admin dashboard's duplicate "Partnership Requests" buttons** — two separate Quick Action buttons on `admin_dashboard.ejs`, both labeled "Partnership Requests," pointing to two different pages (`/partnership-requests` and `/requests`) — flagged mid-session, still unresolved (U3).
- **View-Only/`potential_partner` role-boundary leaks** — `/viewonly*` routes reachable by `potential_partner` due to an exclude-list instead of an allow-list (R4/S16); `/documents` reachable directly by View-Only even though it's not in their nav (Phase 5).

### Duplicate pages
- **`administrator/requests.ejs` vs. `administrator/partnership_requests.ejs`** — both still live, both linked from the shared admin sidebar, both operate on the same `requests` collection. The older page is the one with the false-success-toast bug and the hardcoded stat cards; the newer hub page is the intended replacement. Roadmap Step 5 (retire or fix the legacy page) has not been implemented yet — still two admin pages doing overlapping things today.

### Confusing workflows
- **One "Partnership Requests" label covers three different request types** — new/renew Partnership requests, role-upgrade Access requests, and MOA/MOU/Accreditation Document requests are all bundled under the admin hub's single "Partnership Requests" name (U3).
- **Document-request "Fulfilled" notification overpromises** — tells the requester their document "has been fulfilled and added to the Document Library," but that's purely a status flip; the admin must separately, manually upload the file elsewhere (B7).
- **Access-request reject-after-accept leaves an inconsistent state** with no UI indication anything is wrong — the request shows "Rejected" while the account silently keeps its promoted role (B5).
- **View-Only's access-request page has a hidden second workflow** — a full "Partnership Request" tab sitting inside what's supposed to be an "upgrade my access" page (R3).

### Incorrect success messages
- **`administrator/requests.ejs`'s Approve/Reject buttons** show a green "approved/rejected successfully" toast even when nothing happened in the database — the fetch response's `res.ok` is never checked, so a 302-redirect-as-200 (when a non-admin Personnel user is bounced by `requireAdmin`) is read as success (B2).
- **Cross-reference, not yet fixed:** the newer hub page's Approve/Reject buttons (`administrator/partnership_requests.ejs`) have the opposite problem — they show *no* message at all and do nothing, because the `x._id || x.id` lookup bug (B1/S9) means the click handler returns before the `fetch()` call ever fires. Confirmed still present as of this pass (re-checked directly in the current file).

### Missing loading states
- **NEW.** Checked every page that calls `fetch()` for an initial data load, for any loading indicator at all (spinner, skeleton, "Loading…" text). Eight have none: `admin_settings.ejs`, `calendar.ejs`, `notifications.ejs`, `partnership_requests.ejs`, `requests.ejs`, `personnel_settings.ejs`, `partner_settings.ejs`, `users.ejs`. The most consequential is `partnership_requests.ejs`: its `init()` runs `await Promise.all([loadPR(), loadAR(), loadDR()])` — three parallel collection fetches — on page load with zero visual feedback while they're in flight. On a slow connection, an admin sees stat cards reading "0" and empty tables with nothing indicating data is still loading, easily misread as "there's nothing to review" rather than "still loading."
- Pages that *do* have some loading indicator (`admin_dashboard.ejs`, `documents.ejs`, `reports.ejs`, `personnel_reports.ejs`, the `partner_*` pages) were not individually verified for correctness/consistency — only presence was checked.

### Missing validation
- **Already fixed this session:** `users.ejs` (Unit/Department made properly optional both sides), `POST/PATCH /api/users` and `POST/PATCH /api/partnerships` (server-side required-field, type, and enum validation added).
- **Still open:** `partnerships`/`calendarevents` PATCH routes accept any subset of their allowlisted fields with no cross-field consistency check (e.g., nothing stops `end` being set earlier than `start`).
- **Confirmed fine:** the Registry Add form (`submitPartnership()`) does give inline feedback — a red border via `highlight(field, true)` — on each invalid required field before refusing to submit; this is a good pattern, not present everywhere (see Poor UX below).

### Poor user experience
- **Inconsistent validation feedback pattern.** The Registry Add form highlights invalid fields in red. Other forms checked this session either do the same (`users.ejs`'s `is-invalid` class) or fail silently. No single consistent pattern across the app.
- **Confirmed fine, worth stating positively:** destructive actions were spot-checked for confirmation prompts — Registry's delete uses a proper Bootstrap confirmation modal, and Calendar's delete uses a native `confirm()` dialog naming the specific event being deleted. Both good patterns.
- **Naming/labeling inconsistency**: "Partnership Requests" is simultaneously a specific request type, a hub page containing three request types, and the label on two different dashboard buttons pointing to two different pages (see Confusing Workflows and Broken Navigation above) — the single biggest source of user confusion identified across this whole audit series, appearing in multiple categories because it touches navigation, labeling, and workflow structure all at once.

---

## UI/UX Problems — Additional Audit Findings

Documentation-only addendum to Phase 6. No application code was inspected further or changed while producing this section beyond re-confirming prior findings; all claims below trace to file:line evidence already gathered during this audit series.

### U6 — Missing Loading States

**Severity: Medium**

Eight pages perform an initial asynchronous data fetch on load with no visual loading indicator of any kind (no spinner, skeleton, or "Loading…" text): `admin_settings.ejs`, `calendar.ejs`, `notifications.ejs`, `partnership_requests.ejs`, `requests.ejs`, `personnel_settings.ejs`, `partner_settings.ejs`, and `users.ejs`.

**`views/administrator/partnership_requests.ejs` is the most significant example.** Its `init()` function (line 1248-1251) runs:

```js
async function init() {
  await Promise.all([loadPR(), loadAR(), loadDR()]);
  updateStats();
}
```

This fires three parallel collection fetches (Partnership Requests, Access Requests, Document Requests) against the backing API on every page load. While all three are in flight, the page shows empty tables and stat cards reading zero across the board — visually indistinguishable from "there is nothing to review." On a slow connection or a heavily-loaded server, an Administrator has no way to tell "still loading" from "genuinely empty" until the requests resolve.

**Recommendation:** adopt one standardized loading-state pattern and apply it consistently across all eight affected pages (and audit the remaining pages for correctness, since presence of *some* indicator was not verified for correctness this pass):
- A visible spinner or skeleton placeholder shown immediately on `DOMContentLoaded`, before any fetch resolves.
- A distinct empty-state message ("No requests to review") shown only after a fetch resolves with zero results — never reused as the default pre-load state.
- A distinct error-state message shown if a fetch rejects, rather than leaving the page silently empty (several of these pages currently have no `.catch()` handling visible in front of the table-render step).

### U7 — Grep Context Rendering False Positives

**Severity: Process note (no application defect)**

During this audit series, `Grep` output using context flags (`-A` / `-B`) twice displayed apparent path corruption that did not exist in the actual source files:

- `views/administrator/calendar.ejs` lines 240-241 — the `apiPost`/`apiPatch` helper functions appeared to call `fetch('\api\calendarevents', ...)` (backslashes, no leading slash) when viewed via `Grep` with context lines.
- `cirl.js`'s `/api/notifications/:id` route context (near the `DELETE` route immediately following it) showed a similarly malformed path when viewed the same way.

In both cases, reading the actual file directly (via `Read`, not `Grep`) confirmed the real source is correct — `fetch('/api/calendarevents', ...)` and `app.delete('/api/notifications/:id', ...)` respectively, with proper forward slashes throughout. No code defect existed in either case; the malformed rendering was isolated to how that tool call's context output was displayed, not the file content itself.

**Recommendation:** any anomaly surfaced through `Grep` context output — especially anything that looks like path or string corruption — should be verified against the actual file content directly before being reported as a defect. This was correctly caught both times during this audit (neither false positive made it into a prior findings list), but it's worth documenting as a standing verification step for future audit passes.

### U8 — Partnership Request Approval Workflow Still Broken

**Severity: Critical — highest-impact functional defect currently identified**

Reconfirmed directly against the current source (not assumed from prior findings): the Approve/Reject workflow in `views/administrator/partnership_requests.ejs` remains unresolved as of this pass. Lines 969, 974, and 994 still contain:

```js
const r = prData.find(x => String(x._id || x.id) === uid);   // lines 969, 994
await fetch('/api/requests/' + (r._id || r.id), { ... });     // line 974
```

Every document returned by `GET /api/requests` includes MongoDB's own `_id` (an ObjectId string, always truthy), so `x._id || x.id` always evaluates to `_id` — never `id`, the numeric identifier the row was actually keyed by (`uid = String(r.id || i)`, line 803). The `.find()` lookup therefore never matches any row, and both `resolvePR()` and `quickPR()` hit their `if (!r) return;` guard and exit before the `fetch()` call on line 974 is ever reached.

**No approval or rejection action executes as a result of this defect.** Clicking either button produces no network request, no error, and no state change — silent, total failure of the primary administrative workflow for reviewing partnership requests. This has been documented since Phase 2 of this audit and remains the single highest-impact functional defect identified across the entire audit series.

---

## Positive Findings

Confirmed-good UI/UX implementations, documented alongside the problems above so this audit reflects the full picture, not only defects:

- **Registry delete confirmation modal** (`assets/js/pages/registry-gridjs.init.js`, `openDeleteModal()`/`confirmDelete()`) — deleting a partnership record requires an explicit confirmation step through a proper Bootstrap modal before the `DELETE` request fires. Correct pattern for a destructive action.
- **Calendar delete confirmation dialog** (`views/administrator/calendar.ejs`, the `#btn-delete` click handler) — uses a native `confirm('Delete "' + currentEv.title + '"?')` that names the specific event being deleted before calling `apiDelete()`. Correct pattern, and an improvement over a generic "Are you sure?" since it names the actual record.
- **Registry inline validation feedback** (`assets/js/pages/registry-gridjs.init.js`, `submitPartnership()`) — invalid required fields are highlighted with a red border via `highlight(field, true)` before the submission is blocked, giving the user immediate, specific feedback rather than a silent no-op or a generic alert.

# Implementation Roadmap v2 — 2026-07-18 (Full Dependency-Ordered Plan, All Remaining Open Findings)

Supersedes the numbering (not the content — nothing below is deleted) of the original `# Implementation Roadmap` (Steps 1–12, above) for whatever work is still outstanding. Steps 1, 2, 4, and the `users`/`partnerships`/`notifications` mass-assignment and `GET /api/partnerships` portions of that original roadmap are done and live-verified (see Status Tracker at the top of this document) and are **not** repeated here. Two items the original roadmap listed as open are also now done and excluded below: B1/S9/U8 (Approve/Reject buttons — resolved 2026-07-18) and Auth. Personnel's `/personnel/notifications` sidebar link (resolved the same day, part of the "Role-specific pages and navigation" fix) — U8's "still unresolved" text earlier in this document and the Re-Verification Pass table's corresponding row are both superseded by those two Resolved-table entries and are left untouched only because historical findings are never edited.

Every item below follows the required six-field format: **Problem**, **Root Cause**, **Files Affected**, **Implementation Plan**, **Verification Plan**, **Regression Tests**. The Phase-letter grouping (A, B, C…) reflects shared files/root cause — items inside the same numbered Phase are meant to be implemented together in one pass, per this project's `ralph-loop` discipline (smallest logical change → static check → live verification with real data → regression → update this tracker).

## Execution Order — Category-Priority Sequencing (added 2026-07-18, per explicit instruction)

Scheduling now follows an explicit 10-category priority order (Critical security → Authentication/authorization → RBAC → Broken workflows → Backend/frontend mismatches → Database improvements → Performance → Missing features → UI/UX → Cleanup/refactoring), overriding the Phase-letter order below for *sequencing* purposes only — the lettered Phases' content (Problem/Root Cause/Files/Plan/Verify/Regression) is unchanged and unmoved; this table only says which order to execute them in. One correction made while building this table: Phase G's items are all security hardening and belong in category 1, immediately after Phase B, not after the RBAC/broken-workflow phases as their letter position implied. One gap found and fixed: "no pagination on any list endpoint" was a documented Open finding in the original audit (§4 Database-Related Issues, §Re-Verification Pass) that never got its own roadmap item — added below as **P1** under category 7.
