### P1. No pagination on any list endpoint
- **Problem:** Every list endpoint (`GET /api/partnerships`, `/api/requests`, `/api/activitylogs`, `/api/documents`, `/api/notifications`, `/api/calendarevents`, etc.) returns its *entire* collection in a single response — fine at current data volume, a real cost once any collection grows past a few thousand documents, and was a documented Open finding (D1/Phase 4 "Performance", Re-Verification Pass) that this roadmap never gave its own implementation item until now.
- **Root Cause:** Every list route uses a bare `.find({}).toArray()` (or an equivalently unbounded query) with no `skip`/`limit`, and no frontend view has any pager UI to request a subset.
- **Files Affected:** `cirl.js` — `GET /api/partnerships` (~529-538), `GET /api/requests` (~560-569), `GET /api/activitylogs` (~1836), `GET /api/documents`, `GET /api/notifications`, `GET /api/calendarevents`, and their `/mine` variants; the corresponding list-rendering views (`views/administrator/registry.ejs`, `requests.ejs`, `partnership_requests.ejs`, `documents.ejs`, `reports.ejs`, and role-equivalent Personnel/Partner pages) would need a pager control if this is implemented UI-side, not just API-side.
- **Implementation Plan:** Add optional `?page`/`?limit` query params to each list route, applied via `.skip((page-1)*limit).limit(limit)`, **defaulting to the current unbounded behavior when the params are absent** — this keeps every existing caller (which sends no pagination params today) working identically, and lets pagination be adopted incrementally per-page rather than as one big-bang change.
- **Verification Plan:** Against real data, request a route with a small `?limit=`, confirm only that slice returns and a total/hasMore indicator is correct; request the same route with no params, confirm the full real dataset still returns byte-for-byte identical to pre-change behavior.
- **Regression Tests:** `npm test` 26/26; confirm every existing page that calls these list endpoints without pagination params still renders the full dataset exactly as before (this is the main regression risk — an accidentally-added default `limit` would silently truncate every existing view).

Given the scale of "every list endpoint across 10 collections," recommend scoping this as its own dedicated implementation pass once reached in sequence, rather than folding it into a single small change. — Close remaining forgery/mass-assignment gaps (S5, S6, D2-remainder)

### A1. Forgery-capable dead routes: `POST /api/activitylogs`, `POST /api/notifications` — **RESOLVED 2026-07-18, see Status Tracker's Resolved table (S5/S6 row) for full implementation/verification detail**
- **Problem:** Any authenticated user of any role can forge an arbitrary audit-log entry or a spoofed notification (including setting `targetEmail` to any other user), even though no legitimate frontend anywhere calls either route.
- **Root Cause:** Both routes are gated `requireAuth` only (not `requireAdmin`/`requirePersonnel`) and build the inserted document from an unrestricted `{ id: nextId, ...req.body }` spread — no field allowlist, no role restriction, and (for notifications) no check that `targetEmail` matches the caller.
- **Files Affected:** `cirl.js:1836-1853` (`POST /api/activitylogs`), `cirl.js:1856-1866` (`POST /api/notifications`).
- **Implementation Plan:** Confirmed via repeated grep across this audit series that neither route has any frontend caller. Recommended fix is removal (simplest, zero regression risk since nothing calls them): delete both route handlers. If a future admin-triggered "manual log entry" or "manual notification" feature is wanted instead of removal, gate to `requireAdmin` and replace the raw spread with an explicit field allowlist (mirroring the `PARTNERSHIP_FIELDS` pattern already used for `partnerships`), and force `notifications.targetEmail`/`activitylogs.actorEmail`-equivalent fields from the session, never the body.
- **Verification Plan:** `node -c cirl.js` clean; `grep` confirms zero remaining references to either route path anywhere in `views/`; live `curl` as any authenticated role returns `404` post-removal (or `403` for a non-admin if gated instead of removed).
- **Regression Tests:** `npm test` 26/26; confirm real activity-log writes (via `logActivity()`, the trusted server-side helper — untouched) and real notification writes (via the lifecycle/approval code paths — untouched) still function, since only the public POST routes are being removed/locked down, not the internal helper functions.

### A2. Mass-assignment on `POST/PATCH /api/calendarevents` — **RESOLVED 2026-07-18, see Status Tracker's Resolved table (D2 (calendarevents) row) for full implementation/verification detail**
- **Problem:** A client can override the server-assigned `id` on create, or `$set` arbitrary/renamed fields (including `id` itself) on update, for any calendar event.
- **Root Cause:** Same unresolved pattern already fixed elsewhere in the app (`users`, `partnerships`, `notifications`): `{ id: nextId, ...req.body }` on POST, raw `{$set: req.body}` on PATCH, with zero allowlist.
- **Files Affected:** `cirl.js` (`POST /api/calendarevents` ~1970, `PATCH /api/calendarevents/:id` ~1982).
- **Implementation Plan:** Apply the identical fix pattern already used for `partnerships`: destructure/strip client-supplied `id`/`_id` before building the insert; on PATCH, build `$set` from an explicit allowlist of legitimate calendar-event fields (title, start, end, description, etc. — read off the existing create form in `views/administrator/calendar.ejs` to enumerate the real field set) rather than the raw body.
- **Verification Plan:** Repeat this session's established id-collision test: POST a new event with a client-supplied `id` matching a real existing event's id, confirm the server-assigned id wins and the real event is untouched; PATCH with an unknown field, confirm it's silently dropped (or 400s, matching whatever convention `partnerships` used).
- **Regression Tests:** `npm test` 26/26; live-test the real Calendar page's Add/Edit/Delete flow end-to-end (Administrator and Auth. Personnel, both `requirePersonnel`-gated) to confirm no legitimate field was accidentally excluded from the new allowlist.

## Phase B — Close the View-Only document-metadata leak (residual S7)

### B1. `GET /api/documents` still fully unscoped for View-Only — **RESOLVED 2026-07-18, see Status Tracker's Resolved table (S7 (residual) row) for full implementation/verification detail. Product decision confirmed by user: block via `requireUploader` (option b in the original write-up below, labeled "recommended" there — the user's own message used different lettering; implemented option matches the "gate the route" choice).**
- **Problem:** A View-Only account (self-signup, zero vetting) can call `GET /api/documents` directly and receive every organization's document metadata — title, institution, uploader email, file link — for all 11 real documents in the collection.
- **Root Cause:** The 2026-07-18 "own-scope" product decision deliberately widened the ownership filter to `Administrator`/`Auth. Personnel`/`potential_partner` (`OWN_SCOPE_ROLES`) but explicitly left View-Only on the old unscoped branch, matching the pre-existing (not previously fixed) S7 precedent — this was a conscious scope boundary in that task, not an oversight, but it leaves a real, still-live data exposure for the one role with no legitimate document-library use case at all.
- **Files Affected:** `cirl.js` (`GET /api/documents` ~1114, same file's `GET /uploads/documents/:filename` ~2316 for the parallel file-serving check).
- **Implementation Plan:** This needs one small product decision before coding (flagging it explicitly, the same way the original Step 1/9 items did): either (a) add `View-Only` to `OWN_SCOPE_ROLES` so they see only documents they personally uploaded (almost certainly zero, same empty-by-default outcome Admin/Personnel now have), or (b) since View-Only has no legitimate document-library use case at all (confirmed in Phase 5 — their nav has no Documents link and their dashboard never calls this route), gate the route itself to `requireUploader` (Administrator/Personnel/potential_partner only, View-Only excluded) instead of widening the ownership filter. Recommend (b) — it matches the exact precedent already set for `GET /api/partnerships` in the earlier `requireUploader` fix, is simpler than adding a fourth role to the scope list, and correctly reflects that View-Only was never meant to reach this route at all.
- **Verification Plan:** Live-test with a real View-Only test account: confirm `GET /api/documents` now `403`/`302`s instead of returning the full 11-document set; confirm Administrator/Auth. Personnel/potential_partner are all unaffected (still get their own-scope results, per the 2026-07-18 decision).
- **Regression Tests:** `npm test` 26/26; re-confirm the file-serving route's existing 403 ownership check is untouched; re-confirm no View-Only page currently depends on this route (already confirmed in Phase 5, re-check is just a regression guard).

## Phase C — Request-subsystem correctness (S8, B4, B5, B6, B7, B8)

### C1. Spoofable `email` on `POST /api/access-requests` — **RESOLVED 2026-07-19, see Status Tracker's Resolved table (S8 row) for full implementation/verification detail. This closes Category 2 (Authentication/Authorization).**
- **Problem:** Any authenticated user can submit an access-upgrade request under someone else's email address; if an admin approves it without noticing the mismatch, that other person's account is silently promoted/created.
- **Root Cause:** The route trusts `req.body.email` verbatim instead of using `req.session.user.email`, unlike every other request subsystem in the app (partnership/document requests both correctly use the session email).
- **Files Affected:** `cirl.js:2113-2145` (email used at 2114, 2134).
- **Implementation Plan:** Replace every read of `req.body.email` in this handler with `req.session.user.email`. One-line-per-usage change, no schema change needed.
- **Verification Plan:** Log in as a real test account, POST an access request with a different real user's email in the body, confirm the created request record's email is the session's own, not the spoofed one.
- **Regression Tests:** `npm test` 26/26; confirm the legitimate access-request flow (real user requesting their own upgrade) still creates a correctly-attributed request end-to-end through approval.

### C2. No status-transition guards on the three request-review PATCH routes — **RESOLVED 2026-07-19, see Status Tracker's Resolved table (B4 row). This fix's own side effect also fully resolved C3 below (see that item's annotation).**
- **Problem:** A partnership/access/document request can be approved twice, rejected after approval, or approved after being withdrawn — each re-run re-executes side effects (duplicate partnership-extension update, duplicate notification, duplicate activity-log entry).
- **Root Cause:** None of the three `PATCH .../:id` handlers checks the request's *current* stored status before applying a new one; they unconditionally `$set` the new status and fire the associated side effects every time.
- **Files Affected:** `cirl.js:830` (partnership requests — corrected line per Re-Verification Pass, `PATCH /api/requests/:id`), `cirl.js:1010` (document requests), `cirl.js:2171` (access requests).
- **Implementation Plan:** Same shape for all three: `const target = await db.collection(...).findOne({id}); if (!target) return res.status(404)...; if (!ALLOWED_FROM[req.body.status]?.includes(target.status)) return res.status(400).json({error: 'Invalid status transition'});` before any side-effecting code runs. Define the allowed-transition map per collection (e.g. `Pending → Approved|Rejected`, terminal states have no further transitions).
- **Verification Plan:** Approve a real request, then attempt to approve/reject it again — confirm the second call is rejected `400` and, via direct MongoDB read, that no duplicate notification/activity-log entry or duplicate partnership-extension update occurred.
- **Regression Tests:** `npm test` 26/26; re-run the full legitimate single-approval flow for all three request types end-to-end (this is the same workflow the B1/S9/U8 fix depends on — re-verify Approve/Reject still works after adding the guard, since this touches the same handlers).

### C3. Reject-after-accept doesn't revert the user's role — **RESOLVED 2026-07-19 as a direct side effect of Phase C2's status-transition guard (option (b) from this item's own decision framing — block, don't auto-revert). No separate code was written; see the B5 row in the Status Tracker's Resolved table for live verification of this exact scenario.**
- **Problem:** Rejecting an access request that's already `Accepted` (the user already has the promoted role and is actively using it) flips the request to `Rejected` but leaves the account at its promoted role forever — an inconsistent, silent state with no UI indication anything is wrong.
- **Root Cause:** The reject branch only updates the request document; it never touches the `users` collection to revert the role that the earlier accept branch granted.
- **Files Affected:** `cirl.js:2149-2150` (reject branch) vs. `2192-2217`/`cirl.js:2219-2220` (accept branch, for the pattern to mirror).
- **Implementation Plan:** Needs a quick product decision (flagging explicitly, same as the original roadmap did): either (a) revert the user's role back to their pre-promotion value when rejecting an already-`Accepted` request, or (b) block rejection of an already-`Accepted` request outright with a clear error ("already accepted; demote the user via User Management instead"). Recommend (b) — reverting a role automatically has its own side effects (what if the user has since acted in the promoted role?) that a human admin should decide on explicitly, not have silently automated.
- **Verification Plan:** Attempt to reject an `Accepted` request; confirm either the role correctly reverts (option a) or the action is blocked with a clear message (option b), matching whichever is chosen.
- **Regression Tests:** `npm test` 26/26; confirm rejecting a still-`Pending` request (the normal case) is unaffected.

### C4. `tempPassword` generated but never surfaced in the UI — **RESOLVED 2026-07-19, see Status Tracker's Resolved table (B6 row) for full implementation/verification detail — including the discovery that the "new user" branch is now unreachable via the legitimate flow as a side effect of the earlier C1 fix, though the UI fix remains correct and necessary for other paths.**
- **Problem:** Approving a brand-new access request generates a one-time temporary password server-side, but no frontend anywhere reads or displays it — the new Auth. Personnel account's login credentials are generated and then permanently lost.
- **Root Cause:** `cirl.js` generates and returns `tempPassword` in the API response, but zero references to that field exist anywhere under `views/`.
- **Files Affected:** `cirl.js:2202` (generation), `cirl.js:2226` (returned in response); `views/administrator/partnership_requests.ejs` (the resolve-modal UI that needs to read and display it).
- **Implementation Plan:** In the resolve-modal's success handler, check for `data.tempPassword` in the API response and, if present, show it in a copyable field (e.g. a read-only input plus a "Copy" button) in the modal's success state, mirroring how `POST /api/users`' own tempPassword is already surfaced in User Management (reuse that existing UI pattern rather than inventing a new one).
- **Verification Plan:** Approve a real brand-new access request end-to-end; confirm the temp password now appears in the resulting UI and is copyable; confirm the account can actually log in with it.
- **Regression Tests:** `npm test` 26/26; confirm approving a request for an *existing* user (no new account/tempPassword involved) doesn't show a stray empty credential field.

### C5. Document-request "Fulfilled" notification overpromises — **RESOLVED 2026-07-19, see Status Tracker's Resolved table (B7 row) for full implementation/verification detail.**
- **Problem:** Marking a document request "Fulfilled" tells the requester their document "has been fulfilled and added to the Document Library" — but nothing actually adds anything to the Document Library; it's purely a status flip, and the admin must separately, manually upload the file through the unrelated Document Library module.
- **Root Cause:** The notification text was written assuming an auto-link to the upload flow that was never built.
- **Files Affected:** `cirl.js:1010-1054` (handler), notification text at `1044`.
- **Implementation Plan:** Simplest fix (recommended, matches current actual behavior): change the notification text to something accurate, e.g. "your document request has been marked fulfilled — check the Document Library for the uploaded file separately." Larger-scope alternative (only if actually wanted): have the "Fulfilled" action include an actual file upload step that creates a real `documents` entry, tying the two together for real — bigger scope, needs a product decision on whether it's worth building versus just fixing the copy.
- **Verification Plan:** Mark a real test document request "Fulfilled," confirm the resulting notification's text matches whichever behavior is actually implemented.
- **Regression Tests:** `npm test` 26/26; confirm the "Fulfilled" status transition itself (now also guarded by C2's transition check) still fires correctly.

### C6. Notification unread-count field-name mismatch
- **Problem:** The notification bell badge is permanently stuck at 0 for every role except `potential_partner` (and, since the 2026-07-18 own-scope change, also effectively for Administrator/Auth. Personnel on their now-scoped branch — this bug sits on the one remaining unscoped branch, currently only reached by View-Only).
- **Root Cause:** `GET /api/notifications/unread-count`'s fallback query filters on `{ read: false }`, but every notification document in the app actually uses the field name `unread` — the query never matches anything real.
- **Files Affected:** `cirl.js:1080` (query) vs. actual field writes at `875, 1039, 1782, 1861`.
- **Implementation Plan:** One-line fix: change `{ read: false }` to `{ unread: true }` in the fallback (currently View-Only-only) branch.
- **Verification Plan:** As a real View-Only account with at least one real unread notification targeted or visible to them, confirm the badge count now reflects a real, non-zero number matching a direct MongoDB count.
- **Regression Tests:** `npm test` 26/26; re-confirm the already-correct `potential_partner`/`OWN_SCOPE_ROLES` branch (added 2026-07-18) is untouched and still counts correctly.

## Phase D — Legacy `/requests` page (B2, B3, U4)

### D1. Retire or fix the legacy `/requests` admin page — **RESOLVED 2026-07-19, see Status Tracker's Resolved table (B2/B3/U4 row). This fix's own side effect also fully resolved I1/U3 (see that item's entry) — Category 4 (Broken Workflows) is now fully complete.**
- **Problem:** Two admin pages (`/requests` and `/partnership-requests`) both mutate the same `requests` collection. The legacy one silently shows false "approved/rejected successfully" toasts to an Auth. Personnel user (who can open the page via `requirePersonnel` but whose action 302-redirects against the underlying `requireAdmin`-only API), and its five stat cards permanently display hardcoded fake numbers that contradict the correct live numbers on its own filter-tab badges directly below them.
- **Root Cause:** (B2) `resolveRequest()` never checks `res.ok`/parses the response body before declaring success — a redirected-to-login/dashboard response reads as `ok:true`. (B3) `initCounters()` runs immediately after `updateCounts()` on page load and re-animates every stat card from 0 up to a hardcoded `data-target` attribute, overwriting the real number `updateCounts()` had just set.
- **Files Affected:** `cirl.js:2262-2264` (page route, `requirePersonnel`) vs. `897` (mutating API, `requireAdmin`); `views/partials/sidebar.ejs:108-114` (nav link); `views/administrator/requests.ejs:80,99,118,137,156` (hardcoded `data-target`s), `286-304`/`450-462` (`updateCounts`/`initCounters`), `421-447` (`res.ok` handling).
- **Implementation Plan:** Needs one product decision (retire vs. fix) before coding — retiring is the lower-risk option given the hub page (`/partnership-requests`) already covers the same data and workflow correctly. If retiring: change `app.get('/requests', ...)` to `res.redirect('/partnership-requests')`, remove the sidebar link. If keeping: fix `resolveRequest()` to check `res.ok`/parse the JSON body before showing a success toast, and delete the hardcoded `data-target` attributes so `initCounters()` has nothing fake to animate to (or remove `initCounters()` entirely if `updateCounts()` already covers the same UI).
- **Verification Plan:** If retiring: confirm old `/requests` links now redirect cleanly to `/partnership-requests` for every role that previously had access; confirm the sidebar no longer shows two overlapping "Requests" links. If fixing: log in as Auth. Personnel, click Approve, confirm an honest failure indication (not a false success toast) since the underlying API is still `requireAdmin`-only; confirm the stat cards now match the tab badges.
- **Regression Tests:** `npm test` 26/26; confirm `/partnership-requests` (the surviving/primary hub, and its recently-fixed Approve/Reject buttons) is completely unaffected by whichever change is made here.

## Phase E — RBAC/navigation/documentation consistency (R1, R2/M3, R3, S16/R4)

### E1. RBAC matrix in `views/users.ejs` mismatches real gating — **RESOLVED 2026-07-19, see Status Tracker's Resolved table (R1 row) for full implementation/verification detail.**
- **Problem:** The permission matrix shown to every Administrator claims "Add/Edit Records" and "Export/View Audit Trail" are Administrator-only, but the real routes are `requirePersonnel` (records) and `requirePersonnel` (audit trail, since the S4 fix) — actively misinforming admins about what each role can actually do.
- **Root Cause:** Documentation/display drift — the matrix was never updated when the underlying routes' real gating was set (or changed).
- **Files Affected:** `views/users.ejs:177` (Add/Edit Records), `180-181` (Audit Trail/Export).
- **Implementation Plan:** Update the matrix's displayed values to `true` for Auth. Personnel on both rows, matching the actual `requirePersonnel` gating already confirmed correct and intentional (Personnel's broader access is real product design, not a bug — see the S4 Resolved-table note explicitly confirming Personnel's working Reports export button depends on this).
- **Verification Plan:** Visually/HTML-diff confirm the matrix now reads `[true, true, false]`-shape (or whatever the real three-role breakdown is) instead of `[true, false, false]`.
- **Regression Tests:** None beyond a static template render check (`ejs.compile()`) — this is a display-only text change, no logic touched.

### E2. Auth. Personnel has no nav link to `/registry` despite being backend-authorized — **RESOLVED 2026-07-19, see Status Tracker's Resolved table (R2/M3 row) for full implementation/verification detail — also closed the "wrong sidebar on /registry" gap this item had flagged as a side effect if left unaddressed.**
- **Problem:** Personnel can add/edit/delete partnerships per `requirePersonnel` on `/registry` and `POST/PATCH/DELETE /api/partnerships`, but `sidebar_personnel.ejs` has no link to `/registry` anywhere — a fully-granted backend permission with no UI path to reach it (their only reachable partnership-touching page, `/personnel/lifecycle`, only supports edit/renew, not Add/Delete).
- **Root Cause:** The nav link was simply never added when `/registry` was opened up to Personnel.
- **Files Affected:** `views/partials/sidebar_personnel.ejs:70-149` (full nav inventory, no `/registry` href).
- **Implementation Plan:** Add a `/registry` nav item to `sidebar_personnel.ejs`, in the same style/position as the other Monitoring/Lifecycle-adjacent links. Note this also means `/registry` will render with the **default Administrator sidebar** when Personnel reaches it directly (a separate, already-documented gap, carried forward, not fixed here) — worth deciding at the same time whether `/registry` should also gain a `sidebarPartial` branch for Personnel, matching the pattern already used for `/personnel/documents` etc.
- **Verification Plan:** As a real Auth. Personnel test account, confirm the new nav link is present, and confirm clicking it successfully reaches `/registry` with working Add/Edit/Delete partnership actions (already backend-authorized, now UI-reachable).
- **Regression Tests:** `npm test` 26/26; confirm Administrator's own `/registry` nav/link and sidebar are unaffected.

### E3. View-Only can submit full Partnership Requests via a hidden tab — **RESOLVED 2026-07-19, see Status Tracker's Resolved table (R3 row) for full implementation/verification detail — this closes Category 3 (RBAC). This fix caught and corrected a pre-existing test (`test/requests.test.js`) that had encoded the bug as expected behavior.**
- **Problem:** `/viewonly/request-access` includes a working "Submit New Partnership Request" tab that posts straight to `POST /api/requests`, bypassing the entire purpose of the page (requesting an access-level upgrade), since that endpoint only blocks the `Auth. Personnel` role, not View-Only.
- **Root Cause:** The partnership-request guard on `POST /api/requests` was written as an exclude-list for one specific role rather than an allow-list of who legitimately should submit partnership requests.
- **Files Affected:** `cirl.js:654-660` (guard, blocks only `Auth. Personnel`); `views/view_only/viewonly_access_request.ejs:342-344` (hidden tab), `605` (its POST call).
- **Implementation Plan:** Needs a product decision: remove the hidden tab from the View-Only page entirely (recommended — it doesn't belong on an "upgrade my access" page), or, if View-Only submitting partnership requests is actually intended, change the guard to an explicit allow-list (`Administrator`, `Auth. Personnel` excluded by design already, `potential_partner`) rather than leaving it as a single-role exclude.
- **Verification Plan:** As a real View-Only test account, confirm the tab is gone (or, if kept intentionally, confirm the guard now correctly reflects the intended allow-list).
- **Regression Tests:** `npm test` 26/26; confirm `potential_partner`'s own legitimate `POST /api/requests` flow (their main use case) is unaffected.

> **SUPERSEDED 2026-07-20 — explicit product decision reverses this item.** Per direct user instruction ("View-Only users should: ... Submit Partnership Requests"), followed by an explicit choice of "Full restore of the original hidden-tab UI" when asked how much to restore, this block was reverted: the tab/panel, its OCR auto-fill pipeline (Tesseract.js/pdf.js), and the `POST /api/requests` View-Only guard were all restored to their pre-E3 state. See the **"View-Only Role Feature Update (2026-07-20)"** section for full detail. This entry is left unedited above, per the standing rule never to delete historical findings — treat the note here, not the original resolution above, as authoritative for View-Only's current Partnership Request access.
>
> **SUPERSEDED AGAIN, SAME DAY 2026-07-20 — Access Requests removed entirely.** A second, later instruction this same day ("Remove completely: Access Requests") — explicitly confirmed via clarifying question as "the whole feature," not just the legacy `/admin/access-requests` URL — removed the `accessRequests` collection's entire code path: the AR review tab in `partnership_requests.ejs`, all `/api/access-requests*` routes, and View-Only's Access Upgrade Request panel from `viewonly_access_request.ejs` (the very panel enhanced by the C1/G3/E3-reversal work described elsewhere in this document). View-Only's *Partnership Request* submission ability (the subject of the revert directly above) is **unaffected** by this second change — only the separate Access Upgrade/role-promotion workflow was removed. See the **"Administrator Role & Workflow Update (2026-07-20)"** section for full detail, including the resulting RBAC consequence (View-Only has no self-service path to Auth. Personnel anymore; only a manual promotion via User Management remains).

### E4. `/viewonly*` routes use an exclude-list instead of an allow-list — **RESOLVED 2026-07-19, see Status Tracker's Resolved table (S16/R4 row) for full implementation/verification detail. This closes Category 1 (Critical Security) — see the Phase Completion Report below.**
- **Problem:** `potential_partner` can reach `/viewonly/dashboard` and the other `/viewonly*` pages directly by URL, seeing View-Only's (currently fake-data) dashboard, because the guard only blocks Administrator/Auth. Personnel.
- **Root Cause:** Each of the three `/viewonly*` routes independently re-implements the same `if (role === 'Administrator' || role === 'Auth. Personnel') redirect` check rather than sharing a single allow-list gate.
- **Files Affected:** `cirl.js:2450-2480` (`/viewonly` at 2452, `/viewonly/dashboard` at 2461, `/viewonly/request-access` at 2472).
- **Implementation Plan:** Replace the three duplicated exclude-checks with one shared middleware, e.g. a new `requireViewOnly` (mirroring the existing `requireAdmin`/`requirePersonnel`/`requirePartner` pattern already used everywhere else in the file) that allow-lists `role === 'View-Only'` only, applied to all three routes.
- **Verification Plan:** As a real `potential_partner` test account, confirm all three `/viewonly*` routes now `302` instead of `200`; confirm real View-Only accounts are unaffected.
- **Regression Tests:** `npm test` 26/26; confirm Administrator/Auth. Personnel are still correctly blocked (unchanged behavior, just consolidated).

## Phase F — Database hardening (D1)

### F1. No indexes anywhere, including no unique index on `users.email`
- **Problem:** Every `id`-based lookup across all 10 live collections is a full collection scan (fine today, a real cost as data grows); more importantly, `users.email` uniqueness is enforced only by an app-level check-then-insert, which is racy — two near-simultaneous signups/creates can both pass the existence check before either commits, producing duplicate accounts with the same email.
- **Root Cause:** `db.js` only opens a connection; there is not a single `createIndex` call anywhere in the codebase.
- **Files Affected:** `db.js:1-33` (add index creation in `connectDB()`).
- **Implementation Plan:** Add idempotent `createIndex` calls right after `client.connect()`: unique on `users.email`; non-unique on the `id` field of every one of the 10 collections; non-unique on `notifications.targetEmail`, `documents.uploadedByEmail`, `requests.submittedByEmail`, `documentrequests.requestedByEmail`. **Before adding the unique email index**, audit the live `users` collection for any pre-existing duplicate emails (a duplicate would make index creation fail outright) and resolve any found first.
- **Verification Plan:** Restart the app, call `db.collection.getIndexes()` per collection to confirm each index exists; attempt to insert a duplicate email directly via MongoDB to confirm the unique constraint now rejects it at the database layer (not just the app-level check).
- **Regression Tests:** `npm test` 26/26 (confirms the unique index doesn't reject any legitimate concurrent-looking test data); full login/signup/user-creation regression pass across all four roles.

## Phase G — Security hardening pass (S10–S15, S17)

### G1. No CSRF protection; `GET /logout` is a state-changing GET — **DEPRIORITIZED 2026-07-19.** An initial implementation attempt (adding an inline `onclick` handler to each logout link to POST via `fetch` instead of following the plain GET href) was started — two of the ten logout links across `sidebar.ejs`/`sidebar_personnel.ejs` were edited — before being explicitly paused mid-way. All edits were reverted (confirmed via grep: every logout link across all 6 partial files is back to its original plain `href="/logout"`, `cirl.js`'s `/logout` route untouched) before moving on, to avoid shipping a half-converted state where some links would silently fail (POSTing to a route that still only accepted GET). Not resolved; still fully Open, unchanged from its original finding.
- **Problem:** No CSRF token exists anywhere; `sameSite: 'lax'` is a real but partial mitigation (blocks cross-site POST/PATCH/DELETE, not top-level GET navigation). `GET /logout` destroys the session via a GET request, which *is* reachable via top-level navigation under `sameSite=lax` — a low-impact "logout CSRF."
- **Root Cause:** No CSRF middleware was ever added; `/logout` was implemented as a GET rather than a POST from the start.
- **Files Affected:** `cirl.js:91-102` (cookie config), `cirl.js:472-479` (`/logout`); every view with a logout link/form.
- **Implementation Plan:** Change `/logout` from `app.get` to `app.post`; update every logout link in the views to a small form (or a `fetch` POST) instead of a plain `<a href>`. Full CSRF-token coverage for every other state-changing route is a larger, separate effort (a token-based CSRF middleware plus updating every form/fetch call across ~24 views) — worth scoping as its own follow-up rather than bundling into this pass, given `sameSite: 'lax'` already covers the higher-value POST/PATCH/DELETE surface.
- **Verification Plan:** Confirm `GET /logout` now 404s/405s; confirm the updated logout link/button still successfully logs a real session out via POST.
- **Regression Tests:** `npm test` 26/26; click logout from every role's UI to confirm the updated mechanism works everywhere it's used.

### G2. Rate limiting only on `/login` — **RESOLVED 2026-07-19, see Status Tracker's Resolved table (S11 row) for full implementation/verification detail, including a real cross-route rate-limit-sharing bug caught and fixed during this item's own live verification.**
- **Problem:** `/signup`, the three password-change routes, and the public unauthenticated `/api/institutions` proxy have no rate limiting — open to account-creation spam, password brute-forcing, and abuse of the app as a relay against the third-party university API.
- **Root Cause:** `loginLimiter` was built and applied only to `/login`; never extended to the other routes with similar abuse potential.
- **Files Affected:** `loginLimiter` defined `cirl.js:63-77`; needs applying to `/signup` (`404`), `/api/admin/password` (`2036`), `/api/personnel/password` (`2089`), `/api/partner/password` (`2580`), `/api/institutions` (`2273`).
- **Implementation Plan:** Reuse the existing `loginLimiter` (or a slightly looser sibling instance, if signup/institution-proxy traffic patterns warrant different thresholds) as middleware on each listed route.
- **Verification Plan:** Hammer each route past its new limit, confirm a `429` response; confirm normal single-request usage of each route is unaffected.
- **Regression Tests:** `npm test` 26/26; confirm legitimate signup/password-change flows still succeed under normal (non-abusive) usage.

### G3. Upload validation by mimetype/extension only — **RESOLVED 2026-07-19, see Status Tracker's Resolved table (S12 row) for full implementation/verification detail.**
- **Problem:** Avatar and OCR upload middleware validate file type by client-controlled `mimetype`/extension only, not actual file content/magic bytes.
- **Root Cause:** Neither middleware performs any content-based verification.
- **Files Affected:** `middleware/avatarUploadMiddleware.js:9-10,22-28`, `middleware/uploadMiddleware.js:9-10,23-29`.
- **Implementation Plan:** Add a magic-byte check using the already-installed `sharp` dependency (`sharp(buffer).metadata()` wrapped in try/catch — a non-image buffer will throw) for avatar uploads, or the `file-type` package for a more general content-sniffing check across both middlewares.
- **Verification Plan:** Upload a renamed non-image file (e.g. a `.exe` renamed to `.png`) as an avatar, confirm it's now rejected; confirm a real image upload still succeeds.
- **Regression Tests:** `npm test` 26/26; confirm real OCR document uploads (PDF/image) still succeed post-change.

### G4. No global 4-arg Express error handler — **RESOLVED 2026-07-19, see Status Tracker's Resolved table (S13 row) for full implementation/verification detail.**
- **Problem:** Every route wraps itself in try/catch individually (good), but if that pattern is ever missed, Express's default handler can leak stack traces to the client depending on `NODE_ENV`.
- **Root Cause:** No trailing `app.use((err, req, res, next) => {...})` exists in `cirl.js`.
- **Files Affected:** `cirl.js` (absent; file currently ends at `app.listen`).
- **Implementation Plan:** Add a final 4-arg error-handling middleware after every route registration that logs the error internally and returns a generic `500` JSON/HTML response, never the raw stack trace, regardless of `NODE_ENV`.
- **Verification Plan:** Deliberately trigger an unhandled error path (e.g. a temporary throw in a test route) and confirm the client receives a generic message, not a stack trace.
- **Regression Tests:** `npm test` 26/26; confirm normal error responses from existing try/catch blocks (e.g. validation 400s) are unaffected — the global handler should only catch what individual routes miss.

### G5. Export routes stream with no error handling — **RESOLVED 2026-07-19, see Status Tracker's Resolved table (S14 row) for full implementation/verification detail, including an honest limitation on how thoroughly the exact race was reproduced live.**
- **Problem:** PDF/Excel export routes set response headers and then stream data with no `stream error`/`res.on('error')` handling — if PDFKit/ExcelJS throws mid-stream or the client disconnects mid-download, the catch block's `res.status(500).json(...)` will conflict with already-sent headers.
- **Root Cause:** The export helpers were written assuming the happy path; no stream-level error listener was added.
- **Files Affected:** `renderTablePdf` `cirl.js:1342-1395`; Excel routes `1425-1461`, `1490-1508`.
- **Implementation Plan:** Add `res.headersSent` checks before any `catch` block's `res.status(500)` call, and attach `stream.on('error', ...)` handlers that log and gracefully end the response rather than attempting a second header write.
- **Verification Plan:** Force a mid-stream error (e.g. temporarily inject a throw partway through a real export) and confirm no unhandled exception/process crash, and no "headers already sent" error surfaces to the client.
- **Regression Tests:** `npm test` 26/26; confirm normal, successful PDF/Excel exports for both Partnerships and Activity Log are unaffected.

### G6. `GET /api/ocr/status/:jobId` has no ownership check — **RESOLVED 2026-07-19, see Status Tracker's Resolved table (S15 row) for full implementation/verification detail.**
- **Problem:** Any `requireUploader` session can poll another user's OCR job status if they obtain/guess the jobId.
- **Root Cause:** The route never checks that the job belongs to the requesting session.
- **Files Affected:** `controllers/ocrController.js:28-42`; mounted `cirl.js:2299`.
- **Implementation Plan:** Store the initiating user's email/id alongside the job at creation time; check it against `req.session.user` before returning status. Low severity today since jobIds are unguessable `crypto.randomUUID()` values with a 10-minute TTL, but worth closing while touching this area.
- **Verification Plan:** As User A, start a real OCR job; as User B, attempt to poll User A's jobId; confirm it's now rejected.
- **Regression Tests:** `npm test` 26/26; confirm a user polling their own job's status still works.

### G7. DSS widgets build HTML via unescaped `.innerHTML` — **RESOLVED 2026-07-19, see Status Tracker's Resolved table (S17 row) for full implementation/verification detail.**
- **Problem:** "Nearest Expiring"/"Longest-Overdue" widgets on the Reports pages build HTML via string concatenation and unescaped `.innerHTML` using partnership fields (`p.inst`, `p.country`, `p.type`) with zero server-side sanitization on write.
- **Root Cause:** The widget-rendering code was written assuming trusted input, since only Administrator/Personnel can currently write partnership data — but that's an access-control assumption baked into a rendering choice, not a real guarantee against a stored-XSS-shaped field value.
- **Files Affected:** `views/administrator/reports.ejs:698-719`, `views/auth. personnel/personnel_reports.ejs:460-481`.
- **Implementation Plan:** Replace the string-concatenation `.innerHTML` calls with either DOM-node construction (`textContent` for each field) or an HTML-escaping helper applied to each interpolated value before concatenation.
- **Verification Plan:** Create a real test partnership with an HTML/script-like value in `inst`/`country`/`type` (e.g. `<img src=x onerror=alert(1)>`), confirm the Reports widget now renders it as inert text, not executable markup.
- **Regression Tests:** `npm test` 26/26; confirm the widgets still render normal, legitimate partnership data correctly (name/country/type visible, styling intact).

## Phase H — Dead-code and stale-data cleanup (B9, B11, D3, profiles.password)

### H1. Remove or fix confirmed dead/broken routes and collections
- **Problem:** Several backend routes are built but never called by any frontend (`GET /api/me`, `GET /api/dashboard/stats`, `POST /api/lifecycle/recompute`, `DELETE /api/notifications/:id`, `DELETE /api/users/:id`), one of them (`/api/dashboard/stats`) is also internally broken (filters on lowercase status strings that never match the app's actual capitalized values); the `agreements` collection is dead data with zero code references; the `profiles` collection carries a stray, unused, alarming-looking plaintext `password` field on two documents.
- **Root Cause:** Iterative feature development left behind routes/fields that were superseded or never wired up to a frontend caller.
- **Files Affected:** `cirl.js:487-520` (`/api/dashboard/stats`), `482` (`GET /api/me`), `1816` (`POST /api/lifecycle/recompute`), `1935` (`DELETE /api/notifications/:id`), `1668` (`DELETE /api/users/:id`); `Database_backup/ciprms.agreements.json` (no live-code references); `profiles` collection documents with a `password` field (no live-code references).
- **Implementation Plan:** For each dead route, confirm via grep (already done in this audit series) zero callers exist, then either delete the route outright or, for the two with plausible future use (`DELETE /api/users/:id` pairs with a possible future delete-user UI, tracked separately as F3 below; `DELETE /api/notifications/:id` already got a defensive ownership check in an earlier fix), leave as-is but documented as intentionally-dead. Fix `/api/dashboard/stats`'s lowercase-status filters to match real capitalization if the route is ever going to be wired up, or delete it if not. Drop the `agreements` collection (or explicitly archive/rename it as historical) since nothing references it. Remove the stray `password` field from the two `profiles` documents that carry it (a `db.collection('profiles').updateMany({password:{$exists:true}}, {$unset:{password:""}})`-shaped one-time cleanup, not a code change).
- **Verification Plan:** Re-grep post-change to confirm no frontend regression from any route removal; confirm the two `profiles` documents no longer carry a `password` field via direct MongoDB read.
- **Regression Tests:** `npm test` 26/26; smoke-test that nothing in the app (any role, any page) was silently depending on any of the removed dead routes.

## Phase I — Remaining UI/UX and missing-feature items (U3, U6, F1, F2, F3, Forgot Password)

### I1. Duplicate "Partnership Requests" dashboard buttons — **RESOLVED 2026-07-19 as a direct side effect of Phase D1 (retiring the legacy `/requests` page also removed its redundant dashboard button). No separate code was written; see the U3 row in the Status Tracker's Resolved table.**
- **Problem:** The admin dashboard has two Quick Action buttons whose destinations overlap in purpose ("Partnership Requests" → `/requests`, "Partnership Requests Hub" → `/partnership-requests`) — confusing given Phase D above may retire or repurpose one of those two pages.
- **Root Cause:** Both buttons were added at different times referencing the two parallel admin request-review pages that still coexist.
- **Files Affected:** `views/administrator/admin_dashboard.ejs:387-402`.
- **Implementation Plan:** Resolve after Phase D's retire-or-fix decision on the legacy `/requests` page — if retired, remove this button entirely (its destination now just redirects); if kept, give the two buttons clearly distinct labels reflecting genuinely distinct purposes.
- **Verification Plan:** Visual check of the dashboard's Quick Actions section post-change; confirm both remaining (or single) button(s) land on the intended page.
- **Regression Tests:** None beyond a static template render check — purely a labeling/link change.

### I2. Missing loading states on eight pages
- **Problem:** `admin_settings.ejs`, `calendar.ejs`, `notifications.ejs`, `partnership_requests.ejs`, `requests.ejs`, `personnel_settings.ejs`, `partner_settings.ejs`, and `users.ejs` all perform an initial async data fetch with zero visual loading indicator — most consequentially `partnership_requests.ejs`, whose `init()` runs three parallel fetches while showing empty tables and all-zero stat cards, indistinguishable from "nothing to review."
- **Root Cause:** No loading-state pattern was ever standardized across the app; each page was built independently.
- **Files Affected:** the eight `.ejs` files listed above.
- **Implementation Plan:** Adopt one standard loading-state pattern (a visible spinner/skeleton shown immediately on `DOMContentLoaded`, before any fetch resolves; a distinct "No results" empty-state shown only after a fetch resolves with zero results; a distinct error-state shown if a fetch rejects) and apply it consistently across all eight pages, starting with `partnership_requests.ejs` given it's the highest-traffic admin workflow page.
- **Verification Plan:** Throttle network speed in a real browser (or simulate via a delayed test endpoint) and confirm each page now shows a loading indicator before data arrives, rather than a misleading empty state.
- **Regression Tests:** `npm test` 26/26; confirm each page's final rendered state (after data loads) is unchanged from before.

### I3. Archive workflow for partnerships (open design question, still unresolved)
- **Problem:** `DELETE /api/partnerships/:id` is a hard, permanent delete; there is no soft-archive/restore mechanism, despite the admin dashboard's own generated text ("...not yet been renewed or archived") explicitly treating "archived" as an available action.
- **Root Cause:** The Archive feature was referenced in UI copy but never actually built; flagged as an open design question earlier this session and never resolved.
- **Files Affected:** `cirl.js:1257-1268` (current hard-delete route, needs a sibling or replacement); `views/administrator/admin_dashboard.ejs:594` (the text referencing "archived"); `views/administrator/registry.ejs`/`assets/js/pages/registry-gridjs.init.js` (the delete UI, if Archive is added alongside or replaces Delete).
- **Implementation Plan:** Needs a product decision first (same open question from earlier this session): replace Delete with Archive (add an `archived: true`/`status: 'Archived'` flag instead of a real delete, with a restore path), or add Archive as a new action alongside the existing hard Delete. Recommend the former for data-retention/audit-trail integrity (an accidentally-deleted partnership record is unrecoverable today; archiving preserves history) but this is genuinely the user's call to make, not a technical one.
- **Verification Plan:** Once implemented, archive a real test partnership, confirm it disappears from the default active list but is recoverable/visible in an archive view; confirm restore (if built) brings it back intact.
- **Regression Tests:** `npm test` 26/26; confirm the existing hard-delete path (if kept alongside Archive) still works for records genuinely meant to be permanently removed.

### I4. No self-service withdraw/cancel for Access Requests
- **Problem:** Partnership Requests and Document Requests both let the submitter withdraw/cancel; Access (role-upgrade) Requests don't.
- **Root Cause:** The withdraw/cancel feature was built for two of the three request types and never extended to the third.
- **Files Affected:** `cirl.js` (needs a new `PATCH`/`DELETE /api/access-requests/:id/withdraw`-shaped route, modeled on the existing partnership/document-request withdraw routes); `views/view_only/viewonly_access_request.ejs` (needs a withdraw button/action, modeled on the equivalent existing partnership/document-request UI).
- **Implementation Plan:** Copy the existing partnership/document-request withdraw pattern (route + guard that only the submitter, and only while status is still `Pending`, can withdraw) to Access Requests.
- **Verification Plan:** Submit a real access request as a test account, withdraw it, confirm its status updates correctly and an admin can no longer approve/reject an already-withdrawn request (ties to C2's transition guard — withdrawn should be a terminal state).
- **Regression Tests:** `npm test` 26/26; confirm the existing partnership/document-request withdraw flows (the pattern being copied) are unaffected.

### I5. No delete-user UI (confirm intentional, or build it)
- **Problem:** `DELETE /api/users/:id` exists and works server-side, but User Management only exposes Add/Edit/Deactivate — no delete button anywhere.
- **Root Cause:** Unclear whether this is a deliberate product decision (deactivate is generally the safer, more auditable pattern than hard-delete) or an oversight.
- **Files Affected:** `cirl.js:1668` (existing, working backend route); `views/users.ejs:323-331` (renders Edit/Deactivate only).
- **Implementation Plan:** This is a yes/no product decision, not a technical task — confirm whether Deactivate-only is intentional (in which case, consider removing the now-pointless `DELETE /api/users/:id` route per Phase H's dead-code cleanup instead of building UI for it) or whether a real delete-user button should be added (in which case, add it with a confirmation modal, matching the existing Registry-delete confirmation-modal pattern already used elsewhere in the app).
- **Verification Plan:** Once the decision is made: either confirm the route is cleanly removed with no UI regression, or confirm a new delete button works end-to-end with proper confirmation.
- **Regression Tests:** `npm test` 26/26; confirm Deactivate (the currently-used, safer action) is completely unaffected either way.

### I6. No self-service Forgot Password flow
- **Problem:** Any user who forgets their password has no self-service recovery path; an Administrator must manually issue a new one via User Management. The only related UI text is a login error directing legacy plain-text accounts to "contact the Administrator."
- **Root Cause:** This feature was never built — a net-new gap, not a regression.
- **Files Affected:** `cirl.js` (new routes needed: request-reset, verify-token, set-new-password); `views/index.ejs` (new "Forgot password?" link); a new email-sending mechanism (none currently exists in this codebase) or an admin-mediated token/reset-code flow if email isn't available.
- **Implementation Plan:** Needs a scoping decision first: full email-based reset-link flow (requires adding an email-sending dependency/service, meaningfully larger scope) vs. a simpler admin-notified/token-based flow that doesn't require email infrastructure. Given this project's current single-server, no-email-service state, recommend scoping this as its own dedicated task once a decision is made, rather than folding it into this roadmap's smaller items.
- **Verification Plan:** Once built: a real test account requests a reset, completes it via whichever mechanism is chosen, and successfully logs in with the new password; confirm the reset token/link is single-use and expires.
- **Regression Tests:** `npm test` 26/26; confirm normal login (including the existing legacy-plaintext-password redirect message) is unaffected.

---

**Items requiring a product/scope decision before implementation begins** (flagged individually above, collected here for visibility): B1's replacement — N/A (already resolved); Phase B1 (extend `OWN_SCOPE_ROLES` to View-Only vs. gate the route via `requireUploader`); Phase C3 (revert role on reject-after-accept vs. block the action); Phase C5 (fix notification copy vs. build the real auto-link); Phase D1 (retire vs. fix the legacy `/requests` page); Phase E3 (remove View-Only's hidden Partnership Request tab vs. formally allow it — **superseded 2026-07-20, see below**); Phase G1 (full CSRF-token rollout is intentionally scoped out of this pass as a separate, larger effort); Phase I3 (Archive-replaces-Delete vs. Archive-alongside-Delete); Phase I5 (confirm Deactivate-only is intentional, or build delete-user UI); Phase I6 (email-based vs. admin-mediated password reset).

# View-Only Role Feature Update — 2026-07-20

Out-of-roadmap feature work requested directly by the user: a set of concrete UI/RBAC changes to the View-Only role, independent of the Implementation Roadmap v2 phase sequence above. Documented here per the same living-audit standard (files changed, UI changes, RBAC changes, verification performed) rather than as a numbered roadmap item, since it originated as a direct feature request rather than an audit finding.

## Summary of changes

| Area | Change |
|---|---|
| Request Access — file upload | View-Only can now attach one supporting document (PDF/JPG/PNG, ≤10MB) when submitting an Access Request. Reuses the existing secure upload workflow (`uploadMiddleware.js`'s multer instance + `verifyMagicBytes.js`, the same pairing `ocrRoutes.js` uses) rather than inventing a new one. |
| Request Access form | The "Reason for Access Upgrade" field (frontend input, backend required-field check, and its admin-review display) has been removed completely. Submission still succeeds without it. |
| Dashboard — Partnership Overview | Section title/intro changed to reflect "To Be a Partner with CSPC"; the first info-card (previously "Expiring Soon," an admin-facing concern out of place on a prospective-partner's dashboard) was repurposed to "Why Partner with CSPC." |
| Dashboard — chart heading | "Partnerships by College / Unit" → "Partnerships by Institution" (the only occurrence on this page). |
| Dashboard — widget removal | The Row 1 "Expiring Soon" stat card and the Row 4 "Quick Actions" panel were removed outright; both rows were reflowed (4-column stat row → 3 even columns; the remaining Row 4 table now spans full width) so no empty space or broken alignment was left behind. |
| RBAC — Access Request attachment ownership | New `GET /uploads/access-requests/:filename` route: only the requester who owns the `accessRequests` entry, or an Administrator, may fetch the file — mirrors the existing `GET /uploads/documents/:filename` ownership pattern. |
| RBAC — Partnership Request submission | **Reverted** the 2026-07-19 E3 fix (see superseded note above): View-Only can once again submit full Partnership Requests, per explicit product decision. |

## Request Access — file upload

- **Files Affected:** `cirl.js` (new `ACCESS_REQUEST_DOCS_DIR`, rewritten `POST /api/access-requests` handler, new `GET /uploads/access-requests/:filename` route); `views/view_only/viewonly_access_request.ejs` (new `#f-attachment` file input, `submitAccessRequest()` switched from JSON to `FormData`); `views/administrator/partnership_requests.ejs` (AR review modal now shows a "Supporting Document" link when present).
- **Implementation:** `POST /api/access-requests` now wraps `uploadDoc.single('attachment')` (the same generic multer instance `ocrRoutes.js` uses — 10MB limit, PDF/JPG/JPEG/PNG only) followed by `verifyMagicBytes()` — a file whose signature doesn't match its claimed mimetype is deleted and rejected with `400`, exactly like the OCR upload path. On success, the file is moved from `uploads/tmp` to a new permanent `uploads/access-requests/` directory (kept separate from `uploads/documents/`, the Document Library's own storage, which `requireUploader` deliberately still excludes View-Only from) and `{ fileLink, originalFilename }` is stored on the `accessRequests` entry itself — not inserted into the shared `documents` collection, since this attachment belongs to one access request, not the searchable registry. The upload is optional; omitting it still submits successfully.
- **Verification Performed (live, real accounts, no synthetic Mongo data):**
  - Real disposable View-Only account (`zz-test-viewonly-*`, created via `POST /signup`) submitted a real, valid PDF (correct `%PDF-` magic bytes) via `POST /api/access-requests` with a spoofed `email` field in the multipart body — server ignored it and used the session email (C1 protection intact), file persisted to `uploads/access-requests/`, response included the correct `fileLink`/`originalFilename`.
  - Confirmed the file's bytes are correct on disk (`ls`/`head` on the real path returned) and the owning account could `GET` it back (`200`, real PDF bytes returned).
  - A second, unrelated View-Only account attempting to `GET` the first account's file was correctly `403`'d.
  - An unauthenticated request for the same file was redirected (blocked).
  - A file with a `.pdf` extension/mimetype but the wrong first bytes (plain text, not `%PDF-`) was correctly rejected `400` ("Unsupported file type…") and not persisted.
  - The pre-existing duplicate-pending-request guard (`409`) still fires, and correctly deletes the just-uploaded temp file rather than leaving it orphaned in `uploads/tmp` (confirmed `0` files in `uploads/tmp` both before and after the rejected attempt).
  - All test accounts, the one real `accessRequests` document, and its uploaded file were deleted afterward — confirmed zero residue via a direct MongoDB read.

## Request Access form — "Reason for Access Upgrade" removal

- **Files Affected:** `cirl.js` (`POST /api/access-requests` no longer destructures/requires `reason`, no longer stores it on new entries); `views/view_only/viewonly_access_request.ejs` (field removed from the form, from `clearAccessForm()`, and from the required-fields check); `views/administrator/partnership_requests.ejs` (AR review modal's "Reason / Purpose" block and its `ar-v-reason` population line removed — left as a permanent "—" placeholder would have been dead UI directly caused by this change).
- **Verification Performed:** Live submission with no `reason` field sent at all succeeded (`200`) end-to-end. Direct MongoDB read of the new entry confirms no `reason` key is written going forward. Pre-existing `accessRequests` documents from before this change (ids 1–7) still legitimately carry their old `reason` field — expected and untouched, since this is a schemaless collection and no data migration was requested or needed; only new submissions are affected.

## Dashboard changes

- **Files Affected:** `views/view_only/viewonly_dashboard.ejs` only.
- **Verification Performed:** Live `GET /viewonly/dashboard` as a real View-Only account, response HTML grepped for: zero occurrences of "Expiring Soon" and "Quick Actions" (both widgets removed); zero occurrences of "by College"/"by Unit" (fully renamed); one occurrence each of "Partnerships by Institution" and "To Be a Partner with CSPC" (both present as intended). Page returned `200` with no template errors (also confirmed via a standalone `ejs.renderFile()` static check before the server was ever started).

## RBAC — View-Only Partnership Request submission (E3 reversal)

- **Product decision:** Two decisions were required and explicitly made by the user before implementation (not guessed): (1) whether to keep the 2026-07-19 E3 block or revert it — user chose **revert**; (2) how much of the originally-removed hidden-tab UI to restore — user chose **full restore**, including its client-side OCR auto-fill pipeline (Tesseract.js/pdf.js).
- **Root Cause / Recovery Method:** The original pre-E3 `views/view_only/viewonly_access_request.ejs` (tab switcher, Partnership Request panel, OCR drag-and-drop upload, `parseOcrText`/`applyOcrData` auto-fill, Tesseract.js/pdf.js CDN scripts) was recovered byte-for-byte from `git show :views/view_only/viewonly_access_request.ejs` — the file's state in the git index from before this session's E3 edit, still available there because the working-tree edits were never re-staged. The pre-E3 `POST /api/requests` handler (no View-Only block, only the still-current `Auth. Personnel` block) was recovered the same way from `git show :cirl.js`, confirming exactly what to remove from the current handler.
- **Files Affected:** `cirl.js` (removed the View-Only 403 block from `POST /api/requests`, added `app.use('/api/ocr', requireAuth, ...)` in place of `requireUploader` so View-Only's restored OCR upload works); `views/view_only/viewonly_access_request.ejs` (full tab/panel/OCR restore, layered with this task's own Access Request changes — no Reason field, new attachment input — on top); `test/requests.test.js` (new regression test asserting View-Only can submit; the comment block referencing the now-superseded E3 rationale was removed).
- **Scope note on the OCR gate change:** `/api/ocr` moved from `requireUploader` (Administrator/Auth. Personnel/potential_partner only) to `requireAuth` (any logged-in role) so View-Only's restored OCR upload works. This does **not** reopen View-Only's access to the Document Library itself (`GET /api/documents` and `GET /uploads/documents/:filename` are untouched, still `requireUploader`-gated, per the B1 fix) — every OCR job remains ownership-checked per-uploader (`ocrController.status`, from the G6 fix), so this only grants View-Only the ability to run OCR against their own uploads, not to see anyone else's.
- **Verification Performed (live):**
  - Real disposable View-Only account successfully submitted a Partnership Request via `POST /api/requests` (`200`, `status: "Pending"`).
  - Re-promoted a second disposable account to `Auth. Personnel` and confirmed `POST /api/requests` still correctly `403`'s for that role — the *other* pre-existing guard (never part of E3) is unaffected.
  - Static: `node -c cirl.js` clean; `ejs.renderFile()` clean on the restored template.
  - `npm test`: **27/27 passed** (26 pre-existing + 1 new View-Only-can-submit test in `test/requests.test.js`).
  - All test accounts and the one real `requests` document created during verification were deleted afterward (the partnership request was also swept automatically by the test suite's own `jesttest`-tagged `cleanupAll()` since its `notes` field carried the tag) — confirmed zero residue via a direct MongoDB read.

## Regression testing (whole task)

- `npm test`: **27/27 passed**, all 4 suites (`auth`, `partnerships`, `reports`, `requests`).
- Manually re-verified, live, that unrelated roles are unaffected: Administrator's AR review/approve flow still works end-to-end (tested via a real disposable Administrator account listing and viewing the AR entry with its attachment); `potential_partner`'s own `POST /api/requests` path (the C1/C2/E3-era fixes) untouched by any of these changes.
- No browser/Playwright automation is available in this environment (confirmed again this session) — all "live verification" above is real HTTP-request/response and direct-MongoDB-read verification against a single fresh server instance, not a rendered-browser check. Explicitly caveated here rather than claimed as browser-tested.
