# Administrator Role & Workflow Update — 2026-07-20

Second out-of-roadmap feature request the same day as the View-Only update above, covering the Administrator role and the workflows it touches (Partnership Requests, Document Requests, Notifications, Document Library, Calendar, User Management, Dashboard). Documented to the same standard: files changed, routes modified, UI changes, workflow changes, notification changes, Document Library integration, verification performed.

## Product decision: Access Requests removed entirely

Before implementing, the request to "Remove completely: Access Requests" was flagged for clarification rather than assumed, since it would delete substantial functionality built earlier the same session (View-Only's file-upload-enabled Access Upgrade Request panel, its ownership-scoped file serving, the admin AR review tab). The user confirmed: remove the whole feature, not just the legacy `/admin/access-requests` redirect stub. See the superseded-note added to the E3 entry above for the full cross-reference.

- **Removed from `cirl.js`:** the entire `accessRequests` block — `POST/GET /api/access-requests`, `GET /api/access-requests/mine`, `PATCH /api/access-requests/:id`, `POST /api/access-requests/:id/accept`, `GET /uploads/access-requests/:filename`, the `ACCESS_REQUEST_DOCS_DIR` setup, and the `/admin/access-requests` legacy redirect. `generateTempPassword()` itself is kept (still used by `POST /api/users`'s own temp-password flow) — only its access-request-approval call site went away.
- **Removed from `views/view_only/viewonly_access_request.ejs`:** the entire "Access Upgrade Request" panel and tab switcher (now only one panel — Partnership Request — remains, matching the same "single-panel-left → remove the tab UI" precedent as the original E3 fix). Page title/hero updated to reflect it's now Partnership-Request-only.
- **Updated `views/partials/sidebar_viewonly.ejs`:** the "Request Access" nav link and dropdown-menu link (same href, `/viewonly/request-access`) renamed to "Partnership Request" to match what the page now actually does.
- **Removed from `views/administrator/partnership_requests.ejs`:** the "Access Requests" tab/panel/stat-card, the `ar-modal` and `temp-password-modal` (the latter was only ever shown by AR approval — now dead), and all associated JS (`arData`, `loadAR`, `renderAR`, `openARDetail`, `resolveAR`, `quickAR`, `showTempPasswordModal`, `copyTempPassword`). `updateStats()`/`init()`/`switchSection()` updated to drop AR from their aggregates. The page's `<meta name="description">` and hero copy, which both still mentioned "Access Requests," were corrected too.
- **Deleted `views/administrator/requests.ejs`** (the legacy page file itself — confirmed nothing rendered it; its route, `GET /requests`, already just redirected to `/partnership-requests` since the 2026-07-19 D1 fix) **and removed that `GET /requests` route from `cirl.js`** — this closes the separate "Requests page" removal item.
- **Fixed a dead link surfaced by the `/requests` removal:** `views/partials/header.ejs`'s topbar notification-preview demo content had a hardcoded `href="/requests"` — repointed to `/partnership-requests` (the demo notification's placeholder text is untouched; only the now-404 link was fixed, since fixing that link is a direct consequence of this removal, not unrelated cleanup).
- **RBAC consequence (unavoidable, documented rather than silently accepted):** View-Only no longer has any self-service path to become Auth. Personnel. The only remaining path is an Administrator manually changing the role via User Management. No replacement upgrade flow was built — that would be new scope beyond what was requested.
- **Verification:** live, `GET /admin/access-requests`, `GET /requests`, and `POST /api/access-requests` all return `404`; `/viewonly/request-access` loads with zero "Access Upgrade Request"/`panel-access`/`f-reason` remnants and the Partnership Request panel intact; `/partnership-requests` loads with zero "Access Requests" text anywhere (including the meta tag, caught on a second pass after the first grep still found one match there). Historical `accessRequests` MongoDB documents were left untouched (no destructive collection drop) — only the code paths that read/wrote them were removed.

## 1. Partnership Requests — Supporting Documents, Document Library, Notifications, Export

- **Files Affected:** `cirl.js` (new `notifyUsers()` and `prLinkForRole()` helpers; new `POST /api/requests/:id/documents` and `POST /api/document-requests/:id/documents` routes; new `GET /api/reports/requests/pdf` and `/excel` routes; `POST /api/requests` and both PATCH decision routes updated); `services/documentLibraryService.js` (`archiveToDocumentLibrary` extended to optionally record `requestId`/`requestType`); `views/administrator/partnership_requests.ejs` (Supporting Documents section + upload control in both the PR and DR detail modals, Excel/PDF export buttons, deep-link auto-open support).
- **Reuse, not duplication:** the upload itself reuses `uploadMiddleware.js`'s multer instance + `verifyMagicBytes.js` — the exact same pairing `ocrRoutes.js` and the View-Only Access-Request upload (built earlier the same session) both already use. The file is stored **once**, via the Document Library's own `archiveToDocumentLibrary()` — not copied into a second, request-specific location — closing the "avoid duplicate uploads" requirement structurally rather than with a dedup check. Ownership (`uploadedByEmail`) is set to the **requester's** email, not the admin's, which is what makes the file "automatically appear in the requester's Document Library" — `GET /api/documents` already scopes by `uploadedByEmail`, so no separate "push to requester" step was needed. `requestId`/`requestType` are stored on the Document Library entry so the association back to the originating request is preserved; `uploadedAt` comes from the Document Library entry's own timestamp (also preserved, never overwritten).
- **Bug caught and fixed during this item's own verification:** the synthetic `documentType` passed to `archiveToDocumentLibrary` was the short code (`"MOA"`/`"MOU"`) already known from the request, but `shortDocType()` classifies by matching words like "agreement"/"understanding" — a bare short code matches neither, so every upload was silently filed as `type: "Other"`. Added `expandDocTypeLabel()` to expand the short code to a matching descriptive phrase first; re-verified live that a fresh upload now correctly lands as `type: "MOA"`.
- **Export:** `GET /api/reports/requests/pdf` and `/excel` reuse the existing `renderTablePdf()` helper and the same ExcelJS workbook pattern as the sibling partnerships/activity-log export routes — same `requirePersonnel` gating, same streaming-error handling already built into `renderTablePdf`.
- **Verification Performed (live, real accounts):**
  - Real `potential_partner` test account submitted a Partnership Request; both a real Administrator and a real Auth. Personnel account received a "New request submitted" notification with a working deep link (`/partnership-requests?open=pr&id=<id>`).
  - Real Administrator account uploaded a real PDF as a supporting document on that request — confirmed the file exists in the requester's own `GET /api/documents` response (correct `uploadedByEmail`, `requestId`, `requestType`, and — after the type-label fix — correct `type: "MOA"`), confirmed `requests.<id>.supportingDocuments` was updated, and confirmed the requester received a notification carrying a working `downloadLink`.
  - Same flow repeated for a Document Request (Auth. Personnel submits → Administrator notified → Administrator uploads a document → Auth. Personnel notified with a download link) — all confirmed live.
  - `GET /api/reports/requests/pdf` returned a real, valid PDF (`%PDF-` header, 1 page); `GET /api/reports/requests/excel` returned a real, valid `.xlsx` (`PK\x03\x04` zip signature).
  - RBAC: `potential_partner` and `Auth. Personnel` both correctly `302`-redirected (blocked) from `POST /api/requests/:id/documents` and from both export routes.
  - All test accounts, the one real `requests`/`documentrequests` document, the two real `documents` entries, and their uploaded files were deleted afterward — confirmed zero residue via direct MongoDB reads.

## 2. Notifications — new types, click-to-navigate, download

- **Files Affected:** `cirl.js` (all six notification-producing call sites — PR submit, PR decision, DR submit, DR decision, PR/DR document upload, calendar event creation — now go through the shared `notifyUsers()` helper and every one sets a `link`, closing the duplication that would otherwise come from adding this many new call sites); `views/administrator/notifications.ejs` and `views/potential_partner/partner_notifications.ejs` (click now navigates via a new `openNotif()`, plus a "Download" button renders when a notification carries a `downloadLink`).
- **New notification types added:** "New request submitted" (Partnership Request and Document Request — previously not notified to anyone), "Renewal initiated" (fires when `isRenewal` is set on a Partnership Request submission — see honest caveat below), "Documents uploaded" (the Supporting Documents feature above), "Additional documents requested" (see next point). "Request approved"/"Request rejected" already existed and were left as-is apart from adding `link`.
- **"Additional documents requested" — reused the existing `'Under Review'` status rather than adding a new field/workflow:** both PATCH decision routes already accepted `'Under Review'` as a valid status value, but nothing happened when it was set (previously silent). A "Request More Info" button was added next to Approve/Reject in both the PR and DR review modals, and setting status to `'Under Review'` now sends this notification. This is a deliberate minimal choice — the Administrator has no separate "request more docs" action distinct from a status change, so reusing the status value avoids inventing new request-schema fields for the same effect.
- **Honest caveat on "Renewal initiated":** `POST /api/requests` was extended to accept and persist `isRenewal`/`renewalPartnershipId` (fields the PATCH-approval logic already expected and acted on) and to fire this notification when set — but neither the real database nor the seed/backup data (`Database_backup/ciprms.requests.json`) contains a single renewal request, and no page in the app currently sends `isRenewal: true` in a `POST /api/requests` body. This was a pre-existing gap (not introduced by this change) — the notification now correctly fires *if* this path is ever used, but no "Initiate Renewal" UI was built, since that would be new scope beyond a notification-type requirement.
- **Deep-linking:** `views/administrator/partnership_requests.ejs` now reads `?open=pr&id=` / `?open=dr&id=` on load and auto-opens the matching detail modal (`openFromQuery()`). For roles without a per-request detail modal — `potential_partner` (`partner_requests.ejs`) and `Auth. Personnel` (`personnel_requests.ejs`) neither has one today — notifications link at the page level (`/partner/requests`, `/personnel/requests`) instead of inventing a new modal; this is a deliberate scope limit, not an oversight.
- **Bug fixed in passing:** `tagColor` in `notifications.ejs` only had a `requests` (plural) key, but every request-related notification's `module` value has always been `'request'` (singular) — a pre-existing mismatch that made every Partnership/Document Request notification badge render as `bg-undefined-subtle`. Added the singular key alongside the existing plural one. Left as a known, separately-noted limitation: the notifications page's pie chart still groups by exact `tag` string against a hardcoded `{Lifecycle, Calendar, Requests, Registry, Dashboard}` list that doesn't match real tags like "Partnership Request"/"Document Request" — pre-existing, out of scope for this pass, not fixed here.
- **Verification Performed:** see the live-verification bullets under sections 1 and 6 (Calendar) above/below — every notification type described here was actually triggered and observed with a correct `link` (and `downloadLink` where applicable) during this session's live testing, not just written and assumed correct.

## 3. Document Library integration

Covered structurally under section 1 above (the upload routes archive through the existing `archiveToDocumentLibrary()`, never a second storage path). No separate Document Library code changes were needed beyond the `requestId`/`requestType` metadata addition and the `expandDocTypeLabel()` fix.

## 4. Dashboard — Partnerships by Institution

- **Files Affected:** `cirl.js` (`GET /api/partnerships/stats` now also returns `byInstitution`, the top 8 institutions by partnership count — capped the same way the pre-existing "Top Partner Countries" widget already caps itself, since institution names (unlike the small fixed set of CSPC units) can have far more distinct values than a bar chart can usefully show); `views/administrator/admin_dashboard.ejs` (new "Partnerships by Institution" chart card, Row 2 reflowed from two `col-xl-6` cards to three `col-xl-4` cards so the existing "by Type & Status" and "by College / Unit" charts keep their same relative prominence).
- **Verification Performed:** live `GET /api/partnerships/stats` confirmed to return a real, correctly-capped `byInstitution` object against the actual `partnerships` collection; live `GET /dashboard` confirmed both "Partnerships by Institution" and "Partnerships by College / Unit" headings present, zero "Quick Actions" text, and the new `institution-chart` element rendering.
- **Quick Actions removal:** the entire "Quick Actions" card (Row 4, right column — Partnership Registry/Lifecycle Monitor/Calendar/Reports/Partnership Requests Hub links) was removed from `admin_dashboard.ejs`; the remaining "Expiring Partnerships" table was widened from `col-xl-8` to `col-xl-12` so no empty space was left in that row.

## 5. Calendar — recipient targeting on event creation

- **Files Affected:** `cirl.js` (new `resolveCalendarRecipients()` helper; `POST /api/calendarevents` now resolves `req.body.recipients` — a mix of role tokens (`all`, `Administrator`, `Auth. Personnel`, `potential_partner`, `View-Only`) and/or individual emails — into a concrete recipient list and notifies only those users via `notifyUsers()`); `views/administrator/calendar.ejs` (new multi-select "Notify Recipients" field, shown only in Add mode — editing an existing event never re-notifies, since recipients are only meaningful at creation time per the request — populated with role options plus a live-fetched list of individual users).
- **RBAC-aware individual-user list:** the individual-user options are populated from `GET /api/users`, which is Administrator-only. Auth. Personnel can also reach this page (`requirePersonnel` gates event creation) and will simply see an empty "Individual Users" group if the fetch is rejected — the role-based options still work for them. `/api/users` itself was **not** loosened; this preserves existing RBAC exactly as instructed.
- **Verification Performed:** live, an Administrator created an event targeting only `potential_partner` — the real `potential_partner` test account received the notification; a real Administrator and a real Auth. Personnel account did **not**. A second event targeting `all`, created by an Auth. Personnel account (regression-checking that role's pre-existing event-creation ability is unaffected), correctly notified every role including View-Only. View-Only was confirmed still blocked (`403`/redirect) from creating events at all.

## 6. User Management — filtering, tile removal

- **Files Affected:** `views/users.ejs` only.
- **Removed:** the four stat-card tiles (Total Users, Administrators, Auth. Personnel, View-Only) — the row was deleted outright (not just its contents), so "System Users" becomes the first content block with no leftover empty space.
- **Added:** an email-substring filter and a role-exact-match filter above the table, combinable (`renderUsers()` now applies both via `Array.filter`, matching the same client-side filter pattern already used on the Partnership Requests and Notifications pages rather than introducing a new one).
- **Verification Performed:** live `GET /users` confirmed zero occurrences of the four removed tile identifiers and the two new filter inputs present. The combined-filter logic itself was verified directly against a real `GET /api/users` response (23 real users at the time) using the exact same filter expression as the page's own `renderUsers()` — email-substring `"zz-test"` AND role `"Administrator"` correctly narrowed to exactly the one matching account.

## Regression testing (whole task)

- `npm test`: **27/27 passed** (same suite as the View-Only update above — no new tests were needed for this task's changes, since none of it touched code paths the existing suite exercises; regression was instead verified live per section, as detailed above).
- Static: `node -c cirl.js` clean; every touched `.ejs` template (`admin_dashboard`, `partnership_requests`, `notifications`, `calendar`, `users`, `viewonly_access_request`, `partner_notifications`, `sidebar_viewonly`, `header`) rendered cleanly via a standalone `ejs.renderFile()` pass before the server was ever started.
- Live regression confirmed for all four roles: Administrator (full PR/DR/export/calendar/user-management flow), Auth. Personnel (document request submission, PR/DR notifications, calendar event creation still works), `potential_partner` (Partnership Request submission, own notifications, blocked from admin-only routes), View-Only (blocked from calendar creation and all admin-only routes, unaffected otherwise).
- No JavaScript errors were observed in any server-side render or API response during live testing; no browser/Playwright automation is available in this environment (confirmed again), so this is HTTP/DB-level verification, not a rendered-browser console check — stated plainly rather than implied.
- All test accounts, requests, document requests, documents, calendar events, and uploaded files created during this verification pass were deleted afterward — confirmed zero residue via direct MongoDB counts on every touched collection.

# Administrator UI Update — Export Removal & Notifications Redesign — 2026-07-21

Third out-of-roadmap request, scoped narrowly to two pieces of the Administrator UI touched in the update immediately above: removing the Partnership Requests export feature, and redesigning the Notifications page's summary area. Documented to the same standard.

## 1. Partnership Requests — export functionality removed

- **Files Affected:** `views/administrator/partnership_requests.ejs` (removed the "PDF"/"Excel" export buttons — added the previous day — from the status quick-filter toolbar); `cirl.js` (removed the `GET /api/reports/requests/pdf` and `GET /api/reports/requests/excel` routes, and the `REQUEST_COLUMNS` column-definition constant that only those two routes used).
- **No separate frontend JS to remove:** the export buttons called `window.open(...)` directly inline — there was no dedicated `exportReport()`-style function or other JS backing them (unlike `reports.ejs`'s exports, which do have one), so removing the two `<button>` elements was the entire frontend change.
- **Backend routes removed, not retained:** confirmed via a full-repository grep that `/api/reports/requests/pdf` and `/api/reports/requests/excel` were referenced nowhere else — no other view, no test file. Both routes and their shared `REQUEST_COLUMNS` array (used only by the PDF route) were deleted outright. The routes' shared underlying helpers — `renderTablePdf()` and the `ExcelJS` import — are still used by the sibling `/api/reports/partnerships/*` and `/api/reports/activitylog/*` export routes and were left untouched.
- **Verification Performed (live):** `GET /partnership-requests` still loads (`200`) with zero remaining "PDF"/"Excel"/`reports/requests` references; `GET /api/reports/requests/pdf` and `/excel` both now `404`; the sibling `/api/reports/partnerships/pdf` and `/api/reports/activitylog/excel` routes still return `200` (regression-confirmed unaffected). The rest of the Partnership Requests page (search, status filters, PR/DR tabs, table, detail modals, Supporting Documents upload) was not touched and continues to work.

## 2. Notifications — Summary section replaced with stat tiles

- **Files Affected:** `views/administrator/notifications.ejs` only (this template is shared by Administrator's `/notifications` and Auth. Personnel's `/personnel/notifications` via `sidebarPartial` — the redesign therefore applies to both, which is an expected consequence of reusing the existing shared component rather than a scope overrun; `potential_partner`'s separate `partner_notifications.ejs` template was not touched).
- **Removed:** the entire "Summary" card — the ECharts pie chart (`chart-pie`), its `<script src=".../echarts.min.js">` tag, and the ~90 lines of supporting JS (`getChartColorsArray`, `initPieChart`, `updatePieChart`). Clicking a pie slice had been the *only* way to filter the list by module (Lifecycle/Calendar/Requests/Registry/Dashboard) — that capability is gone along with the chart, since it was Summary-exclusive and not exposed anywhere else; the `activeModule` state variable and its dead `setPill('module', …)` branch were removed as the direct, same-scope cleanup this implies.
- **Added:** four stat tiles (Total Notifications, Unread Notifications, Read Notifications, Today's Notifications) positioned above the notification list, in a new dedicated row, built from the exact same markup pattern as the dashboard's own stat cards (`admin_dashboard.ejs`'s `card card-animate` / `avatar-title rounded-circle` structure) rather than inventing a new tile style — matching "the design language used elsewhere in the system" literally. A new `updateSummaryTiles()` function computes all four counts from the real, already-loaded `allNotifs` array (no hardcoded values) and is called everywhere `updatePieChart()` used to be called (initial load, mark-read, mark-all-read), so the tiles stay live.
- **Preserved (not part of "Summary," kept for continuity):** the "Status Filter" (All/Unread/Read) pills — these are a functional control over the notification list, not a display-only summary element, so removing them would have broken existing filtering rather than just changing how it's presented. They were relocated into the Notification List card's own header (a second row, below the title/Mark-All-Read/settings-gear row) instead of being deleted along with the chart.
- **Bug fixed in passing, directly in the code being touched:** the "Today" date-grouping check the list already had (`n.time.startsWith('Today')`) could never actually match anything, since every notification's `time` field is written as `"Jul 21, 2026"`-style text, never the literal string `"Today"` — a pre-existing, silently-dead branch. The new `tile-today` count instead parses `n.time` as a real `Date` and compares `toDateString()` against today's date, so it's the first place in this file that correctly identifies "today's" notifications.
- **Verification Performed:**
  - Live: `GET /notifications` (Administrator) confirmed zero remaining `chart-pie`/`echarts` references, all four new tile elements present, the Status Filter pills and Mark-All-Read control still present. `GET /personnel/notifications` (Auth. Personnel, same shared template) confirmed the same, plus confirmed `potential_partner`'s separate `partner_notifications.ejs` page was untouched.
  - **Tile counts verified against real, non-hardcoded data**, not just visually: a real `potential_partner` test account submitted a real Partnership Request, generating one real, unread, same-day notification for a real Administrator test account. The exact script block from the live `notifications.ejs` file was extracted and executed in a Node harness (mocked DOM + `fetch`, real notification JSON from `GET /api/notifications`) — the page's own `loadNotifs()` → `render()` → `updateSummaryTiles()` chain produced `total=1, unread=1, read=0, today=1`, matching an independently-computed expected value calculated directly from the same API response. This confirms the actual shipped code computes the right numbers, not just that the markup looks plausible.
  - Notification actions re-confirmed working after the redesign: `PATCH /api/notifications/:id` (mark read) still succeeds and persists; the notification's `link` field (added the previous day) is untouched by this change.
  - `npm test`: **27/27 passed** — no test in the suite exercises the export routes or the notifications page, so this also serves as the regression check that removing them didn't collaterally break anything the suite covers.
  - **Cleanup note:** while verifying, found 45 leftover test notifications in the real `notifications` collection — a residue miss from the *previous day's* Administrator-workflow verification pass (real staff accounts like `santos@cspc.edu.ph` had accumulated genuine-looking but fake "ZZ Test…" notifications from that session's `notifyUsers()` broadcast testing, which had deleted its test requests/users but not the notifications those actions had fanned out to real accounts). Deleted all 45 now that they were found, and confirmed zero `ZZ Test`-pattern residue remains across `requests`, `notifications`, and `users`.

# Calendar — Multiple Recipients & Visibility Scoping — 2026-07-21

Fourth out-of-roadmap request the same week, focused entirely on the Administrator Calendar module. The multi-select recipient UI and per-recipient notification targeting already existed from the 2026-07-20 Administrator workflow update (`views/administrator/calendar.ejs`'s `#f-recipients`, `cirl.js`'s `resolveCalendarRecipients()`/`notifyUsers()`) — inspection confirmed individual users, multiple individual users, entire roles, and role+individual combinations were already selectable in one multi-select control, and "All Users" already existed. This pass closed the three real gaps found against this task's specific requirements: explicit duplicate-recipient prevention (previously an incidental side effect of a shared helper, not a guaranteed property of the resolution step itself), "All Users" notifying inactive/deactivated accounts, and — the substantial new piece — calendar **visibility** scoping, which never existed before (recipients only ever controlled who got notified, never who could see the event on the calendar itself).

## Files Affected

`cirl.js` only — `resolveCalendarRecipients()`, `GET /api/calendarevents`, `POST /api/calendarevents`. No frontend changes were needed: `views/administrator/calendar.ejs`'s multi-select UI already satisfied every UI requirement in this task as-is.

## Recipient resolution changes

- **Explicit de-duplication:** `resolveCalendarRecipients()` now returns `[...new Set(...)]` instead of a plain concatenated array. Previously, a user selected both via a role (e.g. "Auth. Personnel") and individually by their own email would appear twice in the resolved list; `notifyUsers()`'s own internal `Set` happened to absorb this before it could produce a duplicate notification, but that was incidental to a helper this function's callers don't control — now the guarantee lives at the point of resolution, which matters more now that the resolved list is also persisted for visibility (see below), where a duplicate email would have been harmless but sloppy.
- **"All Users" now resolves to active users only:** `db.collection('users').find({})` → `find({ status: 'Active' })`. A user with `status: 'Inactive'` can't log in to see a notification anyway, so including them was never meaningful — this is scoped narrowly to the "all" branch specifically (per the task's literal wording), not applied to explicit role or individual selections, which continue to honor exactly what the Administrator picked.

## Calendar visibility scoping (new)

- **Storage:** `POST /api/calendarevents` now conditionally stores a `recipientEmails` array on the event document — only when the Administrator picked a genuinely narrowed set of recipients (specific roles and/or specific individuals). Selecting "All Users," selecting nothing, or omitting `recipients` entirely all produce **no** `recipientEmails` field, marking the event as public/system-wide. This was the deliberate design choice that makes the feature backward-compatible: every event that existed before this change, and every future broadcast-style event, has no `recipientEmails` field and is therefore unaffected by the new filter.
- **Retrieval:** `GET /api/calendarevents` now branches on the requester's role. Administrator: unfiltered, sees every event (full oversight, exactly as required). Every other role — including Auth. Personnel, who can create events but is not exempted from this filter per the task's literal "Administrators can view all events" / "Other users only see events intended for them" wording — gets `{ $or: [{ recipientEmails: { $exists: false } }, { recipientEmails: user.email } ] }`: public events plus any event that names them specifically. Mongo's implicit array-membership semantics make `{ recipientEmails: user.email }` match "email is in the array" directly, no `$elemMatch`/`$in` needed.
- **Not changed:** `PATCH /api/calendarevents/:id` does not touch `recipientEmails` — editing an event's title/date/etc. never alters who it's visible to or re-triggers notifications, consistent with the existing "recipients only apply at creation time" design from the prior update.

## Verification Performed (live, real accounts, no synthetic Mongo data)

Seven real disposable accounts were created via `POST /signup` (Administrator, Auth. Personnel, two `potential_partner`, two `View-Only`, and one `View-Only` account subsequently set to `status: 'Inactive'`), against a single fresh server instance:

- **Combination + dedup:** an event created with `recipients: ["potential_partner", "Auth. Personnel", <partner1's own email>]` — partner1 being both a `potential_partner` (role match) and individually named — resolved to a recipient list containing partner1's email **exactly once** (confirmed by grepping the raw API response). Live notification check confirmed: both `potential_partner` accounts and the `Auth. Personnel` account received exactly one notification each; the two `View-Only` accounts and the creating Administrator (not selected) received none.
- **"All Users" + Active-only:** an event created with `recipients: ["all"]` produced no `recipientEmails` field (public) and notified every active account; the `Inactive` test account's own notification feed was directly queried by `targetEmail` and confirmed empty.
- **Visibility scoping:** an event created with `recipients: [<viewonly1's email>]` only was confirmed visible via `GET /api/calendarevents` to the Administrator (sees all) and to viewonly1 (the named recipient), and confirmed **absent** from the same endpoint's response for viewonly2 and partner1 (not recipients). The same event remained visible to viewonly1 after being edited via `PATCH` (confirming recipient scoping survives edits), and Auth. Personnel — despite being able to create events — was confirmed **not** to see this scoped event either, matching the literal "Administrators view all, others see only what's intended for them" requirement.
- **Backward compatibility:** compared the total event count visible to the Administrator (23, including all pre-existing real events) against the count visible to a `potential_partner` test account (22) — the difference was exactly the one newly-scoped test event; every pre-existing real event remained visible to a non-admin role, confirming "existing calendar functionality continues to work."
- **RBAC regression:** `View-Only` remained blocked (`302`) from `POST /api/calendarevents`, unchanged from before this pass.
- **Static:** `node -c cirl.js` clean; `ejs.renderFile()` on `calendar.ejs` clean.
- `npm test`: **27/27 passed.**
- No browser/Playwright automation is available in this environment (confirmed again) — verification above is real HTTP-request/response and direct-MongoDB-read against a single fresh server instance, explicitly stated as such rather than implied to be browser-tested.
- All seven test accounts, the three real calendar events, and all real notifications generated during this verification pass (37, mostly from the deliberately broad "All Users" test reaching every real active account in the system) were deleted afterward — confirmed zero `ZZ Test`-pattern residue across `calendarevents`, `notifications`, and `users`.

# Notifications — Click-to-View-Details (header bell dropdown + deep-link coverage) — 2026-07-21

Fifth out-of-roadmap request the same week: "make notifications when clicked — can also automatically view details." Inspection found this was **already true** for one specific path (Administrator's Partnership/Document Request notifications, built 2026-07-20 — `partnership-requests.ejs`'s `openFromQuery()`), but not anywhere else. Two distinct gaps were closed:

1. **The header topbar notification bell (`views/partials/header.ejs`) was still 100% static Velzon demo content on every page, for every role.** Only the unread-count badge was ever wired to real data (`GET /api/notifications/unread-count`); the dropdown list itself (`#notif-list`, plus the "Alerts" tab) had hardcoded fake items — "Ateneo MOA expires in 12 days," "REQ-2026-011 submitted by Dr. Santos" — linking to dead/wrong routes (`/lifecycle`, and the Alerts tab's "View Strategic Alerts" button was hardcoded to `/personnel/notifications` even for Administrators). This was flagged once before, in passing, during the 2026-07-20 Access-Requests removal (a single dead `/requests` href inside this same static block was repointed) — but the block's fundamentally-fake nature was left alone at the time as out of scope for that task. This pass replaces it outright with real data.
2. **Several notification `link` targets pointed at a list page with no way to jump to the specific item** — the click navigated, but "viewing details" still required manually finding the right row/request afterward.

## Files Affected

- `views/partials/header.ejs` — the bell dropdown's `#notif-list` and Alerts-tab content are now rendered from real data (`GET /api/notifications/mine`) instead of static markup; clicking an item marks it read (`PATCH /api/notifications/:id`) then navigates via its real `link`.
- `cirl.js` — `prLinkForRole()` (`potential_partner`/`View-Only` PR links), both `Auth. Personnel` Document Request notification `link`s, and the Calendar event-creation notification now all carry the target item's `id`; the Calendar notification specifically now resolves a **per-recipient-role** link (`/calendar`, `/personnel/calendar`, or `/partner/calendar`) since a single event can notify users across multiple roles, each with a different calendar page.
- `views/auth. personnel/personnel_requests.ejs` — new `openFromQuery()` reads `?open=dr&id=` and auto-opens the existing `request-view-modal` via `openRequestView(id)`. (The modal already existed here — contrary to a note in the section above this one, which said neither `potential_partner` nor `Auth. Personnel` had a per-request detail modal; that was no longer accurate for this page by the time of this pass, whether it was added after that note was written or the note was simply wrong. Corrected here rather than left to propagate.)
- `views/administrator/calendar.ejs` and `views/potential_partner/partner_calendar.ejs` — new `openFromQuery()`/`showEvent()` reads `?id=` and auto-opens the matching FullCalendar event's existing detail modal once FullCalendar's async events feed finishes loading (`loading: function(isLoading){ if(!isLoading) openFromQuery(); }`).
- `views/potential_partner/partner_requests.ejs` and `views/view_only/viewonly_access_request.ejs` — new `openFromQuery()` reads `?id=` and scrolls to + briefly highlights (`table-warning`, 3s) the matching row in the existing history table. Neither page has (or needed) a separate detail modal — deliberately not built, since both tables already display every field the notification could otherwise duplicate in a modal; this mirrors the "deliberate scope limit, not an oversight" reasoning already established in the section above for these same two pages.

## What was deliberately left unchanged

- **Lifecycle notifications (partnership expiring/expired) are untouched** — they were made invisible to everyone by design in the 2026-07-18 product decision documented earlier in this file (`OWN_SCOPE_ROLES`/`GET /api/notifications` comment: lifecycle alerts "carry no `targetEmail`... no longer visible to anyone via this route"). Since they never reach `/api/notifications/mine` either, there is nothing for this task's click-to-navigate work to act on; adding a `link` to a notification type nobody can see would be dead code. Not revisited here — that's a separate, already-decided product tradeoff.
- **View-Only has no calendar page at all** (`/calendar`, `/personnel/calendar`, `/partner/calendar` exist; no View-Only equivalent), even though View-Only is a selectable calendar-event recipient (2026-07-20 feature). A View-Only recipient still receives the real notification with a `link`, it just has nowhere in the app to click through to. This pre-existing gap was noted, not fixed — building a new View-Only calendar view is a materially larger feature than "make notifications clickable" and was left alone per "avoid unrelated refactoring."

## Verification Performed (live, real accounts, no synthetic Mongo data)

Four real disposable accounts (Administrator, Auth. Personnel, `potential_partner`, `View-Only`) were created via `POST /signup` against a single fresh server instance (a stale server process from a prior session was found holding port 3000 and killed first):

- **Header bell dropdown, real data:** the Administrator test account's real `GET /api/notifications/mine` response (one live notification generated by a real cross-role Partnership Request submission) was fed into the actual `<script>` block extracted from `header.ejs` via a Node harness (mocked `document`/`fetch`, no browser available in this environment). Confirmed the dropdown correctly rendered the real title and `data-notif-id`, in both the "All" tab and the unread-only "Alerts" tab.
- **Personnel Document Request deep-link:** a real Document Request submitted by the Auth. Personnel test account, marked `Fulfilled` by the Administrator test account, produced a real notification with `link: "/personnel/requests?open=dr&id=3"`. The real `<script>` block from `personnel_requests.ejs` was run in a Node harness against the real `GET /api/document-requests/mine` response for `?open=dr&id=3` — confirmed `openRequestView(3)` fired, correctly populating the modal with the real institution name and "Fulfilled" status badge.
- **Calendar deep-link, per-role links:** an event was created by the Administrator, targeting the Auth. Personnel test account by individual email. The resulting notification carried `link: "/personnel/calendar?id=22"` (not `/calendar` — confirming the per-recipient-role link resolution). The real `<script>` block from `calendar.ejs` was run in a Node harness with a mocked `FullCalendar.Calendar` (returning the real event data from `GET /api/calendarevents`) — confirmed the `loading(false)` callback correctly triggered `showView()` on event id 22, populating the modal with the real title ("ZZ Test Event") and location ("Test Hall").
- **potential_partner / View-Only row-highlight:** a real Partnership Request submitted by the `potential_partner` test account and approved by the Administrator produced `link: "/partner/requests?id=9"`; a Node harness against the real `<script>` block from `partner_requests.ejs` (with a minimal fake DOM supporting `querySelector` against real `data-req-id` attributes) confirmed `openFromQuery()` found row `id=9`, called `scrollIntoView()`, and applied the `table-warning` highlight class. The same Partnership Request flow repeated for the View-Only test account confirmed `link: "/viewonly/request-access?id=10"` — `viewonly_access_request.ejs` uses the byte-identical pattern (verified by direct code comparison rather than a second duplicate harness, given the parity).
- **Static:** `node -c cirl.js` clean; `ejs.renderFile()` clean on all six touched templates (`header.ejs`, `personnel_requests.ejs`, `calendar.ejs`, `partner_calendar.ejs`, `partner_requests.ejs`, `viewonly_access_request.ejs`).
- `npm test`: **27/27 passed** — no test in the suite exercises the notification-dropdown or deep-link paths, so this also serves as the regression check that nothing else broke.
- No browser/Playwright automation is available in this environment (confirmed again) — all "the right modal/row opened" claims above are backed by Node-harness execution of the actual shipped `<script>` content against real API responses (same technique used in the 2026-07-21 Notifications-tiles verification earlier in this file), not a rendered-browser check; stated plainly rather than implied.
- All four test accounts and the real Partnership Requests, Document Request, calendar event, and notifications generated during this verification pass were deleted afterward — confirmed zero `ZZ Test`-pattern residue across `requests`, `documentrequests`, `calendarevents`, `notifications`, and `users`.

# Potential Partner Role Update — 2026-07-23

Sixth request the same week, scoped entirely to the `potential_partner` role: dashboard reshaping (new "Top Departments" section, widget removal, a rename), auto-opening the Partnership Request form, adding Document Requests (previously Auth. Personnel-only), removing Upload & Extract from the Document Library, and removing Reports & Analytics outright. Administrator, Auth. Personnel, and View-Only were explicitly out of scope and are unaffected.

## Files Affected

- `views/potential_partner/partner_dashboard.ejs` — the "Insights" row (3 alert cards: My Renewal Status / Expansion Opportunity / My Request Progress, plus their backing `renderInsights()`/fetches of `/api/partnerships/mine` and `/api/requests/mine`) was replaced with a single new full-width **"Top Departments (CSPC Expansion Opportunities)"** card — a horizontal bar chart (`#dept-chart`) reusing the existing, real `stats.byUnit` breakdown (CSPC colleges/units, ranked by partnership count) that the old "by College/Unit" chart used to show. The "Partnerships by College / Unit" chart was renamed to **"Partnerships by Institution"** and its data source switched from `stats.byUnit` to `stats.byInstitution` (added to the stats API in the 2026-07-21 Administrator dashboard work, top 8 institutions by count) — renaming the heading without also swapping the data would have left it showing department names under an "Institution" label, which would have been a real mismatch, not a minimal-diff nicety. The "Expiring Partnerships" table and "Quick Actions" card (Row 4) were deleted outright, along with their dead `renderExpiringTable()`/`fmtDate()` functions; nothing followed Row 4, so no further reflow was needed to avoid empty space.
- `views/potential_partner/partner_requests.ejs` — `DOMContentLoaded` now auto-opens the request form on every visit: after `loadHistory()` resolves, if a request with `status === 'Draft'` exists, `editDraft(id)` resumes it (no duplicate draft created); otherwise `openNewRequestForm()` opens a blank one. This auto-open is skipped when the page was reached via a notification's `?id=` deep link (2026-07-21 feature) — that link already means "show me this specific past request," not "start a new one," so the two behaviors are mutually exclusive by construction (`if (...?id) openFromQuery(); else openFormOnEntry();`).
- `cirl.js` — new `requireRequester` middleware (Administrator, Auth. Personnel, or `potential_partner`; View-Only intentionally excluded) now gates the three self-service Document Request routes: `POST /api/document-requests`, `GET /api/document-requests/mine`, `DELETE /api/document-requests/:id` (previously `requirePersonnel`, which excluded `potential_partner`). The admin-only routes — `GET /api/document-requests` (full list) and `PATCH /api/document-requests/:id` (decision) — are untouched. A new route, `GET /partner/document-requests` (`requirePartner`), renders the new page below. The `GET /partner/reports` route was deleted.
- `views/potential_partner/partner_document_requests.ejs` (new file) — a `potential_partner`-facing Document Request page, adapted from `views/auth. personnel/personnel_requests.ejs` (same form, history table, detail/cancel modal, and API calls — `POST /api/document-requests`, `GET /api/document-requests/mine`, `DELETE /api/document-requests/:id`), since this codebase's convention is a parallel per-role view file rather than a single shared template for pages not already using `sidebarPartial`. Reused verbatim rather than rewritten, since none of the JS is role-specific — it only ever acts on the session's own email server-side.
- `views/partials/sidebar_partner.ejs` — added a "Document Requests" nav item (between Partnership Requests and Calendar); removed the "Reports & Analytics" nav item.
- `views/potential_partner/partner_documents.ejs` — removed the "Upload Document" button, the entire Upload modal (drop zone, OCR progress/result/error stages), and its backing JS (`startOcr`, `pollStatus`, `docOcrFail`, `docOcrSucceed`, and the modal's `hidden.bs.modal` reset listener). Viewing and downloading (`renderGrid`/`renderList`, both linking to each document's real `fileLink`) and the search/type-filter/grid-list-toggle controls are untouched — none of them depended on the upload modal.
- `views/potential_partner/partner_reports.ejs` — deleted. Confirmed via repo-wide search that only `cirl.js`, `partner_dashboard.ejs` (already being edited for other reasons above), and `sidebar_partner.ejs` referenced it before removal; its two data sources (`/api/requests/mine`, `/api/partnerships/mine`) are both shared, general-purpose endpoints with no partner-reports-specific backend to also clean up.

## RBAC

- `potential_partner` gained: submit/monitor/cancel own Document Requests (`requireRequester`-gated, ownership-checked server-side exactly like Auth. Personnel's existing requests — a partner can never see or act on another organization's document request).
- `potential_partner` lost: access to `/partner/reports` (route deleted, returns 404) and the client-side ability to trigger Document Library uploads/OCR (the button and modal no longer render; the underlying `/api/ocr/*` routes were left alone since Administrator/Auth. Personnel/View-Only still use them elsewhere).
- Administrator, Auth. Personnel, and View-Only RBAC and routes are byte-for-byte unchanged — `requireAdmin`/`requirePersonnel`/`requirePartner`/`requireViewOnly` were not modified; only a new, additive `requireRequester` middleware was introduced.

## Verification Performed (live, real accounts, no synthetic Mongo data)

A fresh server instance was started (no stale process was found holding port 3000 this time). Real disposable accounts were created via `POST /signup` for `potential_partner`, Administrator, Auth. Personnel, and View-Only:

- **RBAC boundaries:** `GET /partner/reports` → `404` (route removed). `GET /partner/document-requests` → `200`. `GET /partner/dashboard` and `GET /partner/requests` → `200` (unaffected). Admin-only `GET /users` and `GET /api/document-requests` from the partner account → `302` (still blocked). `PATCH /api/document-requests/:id` from the partner account → `302` (decision route still Administrator-only, even though the partner can now submit/view requests).
- **Document Requests, partner:** a real Document Request submitted by the `potential_partner` test account persisted with `requestedByEmail` set to that account's own email; `GET /api/document-requests/mine` returned exactly that one request, scoped correctly.
- **Dashboard, real data:** `GET /api/partnerships/stats` returned real, non-hardcoded `byUnit` (`{"CCS":8,"CIRL":7,"CILS":3,"CETE":4,"CNAS":6}`) and `byInstitution` (8 real institution names) values. A Node harness ran the actual `<script>` block from `partner_dashboard.ejs` (mocked `document`/`fetch`/`ApexCharts`, real API responses) and confirmed `dept-chart` rendered the `byUnit` categories sorted descending (`["CCS","CIRL","CNAS","CETE","CILS"]` / `[8,7,6,4,3]`) and `institution-chart` rendered the real `byInstitution` institution names — confirming the rename and data-source swap both took effect together, not just the heading text.
- **Partnership Requests auto-open/resume-draft:** a Node harness ran the real `<script>` block from `partner_requests.ejs` twice — once with an empty request history (confirmed `openNewRequestForm()` ran: form-title became "New Partnership Request", form shown) and once with one `Draft`-status request in the mocked history (confirmed `editDraft()` ran instead: form-title became "Edit Draft Request") — verifying the "resume draft, don't duplicate" requirement against the actual shipped logic, not just by reading the code.
- **Document Library:** fetched the real rendered `partner_documents.ejs` HTML — confirmed zero occurrences of the removed upload markup/functions, and that `renderGrid`/`renderList`/`fileLink` (view/download) logic is still present. `GET /api/documents/mine` still returns `200` for the partner account.
- **Regression — Administrator:** `/partnership-requests`, `/dashboard`, `/registry`, and the admin-only Document Request list/decision routes all still respond exactly as before.
- **Regression — Auth. Personnel:** `/personnel/requests` still `200`; submitting and monitoring a real Document Request as Auth. Personnel still works identically (the `requirePersonnel` → `requireRequester` swap on those three routes is additive, not a behavior change for the roles that already had access).
- **Regression — View-Only:** dashboard and `/viewonly/request-access` unaffected; `GET /api/document-requests/mine` correctly still `302`s for View-Only — confirming this role was deliberately excluded from `requireRequester`, not accidentally swept in.
- **Static:** `node -c cirl.js` clean; `ejs.renderFile()` clean on all five touched/new templates (`partner_dashboard.ejs`, `partner_requests.ejs`, `partner_document_requests.ejs`, `partner_documents.ejs`, `sidebar_partner.ejs`).
- `npm test`: **27/27 passed.**
- No browser/Playwright automation is available in this environment — all "the right chart/form/modal rendered" claims above are backed by Node-harness execution of the actual shipped `<script>` content against real, live API responses, not a rendered-browser screenshot; stated plainly rather than implied.
- All test accounts and the real Document Requests generated during verification were deleted afterward; a repo-wide check confirmed zero `zztest_`-prefixed users, and zero leftover `ZZ Test`-pattern documents across `requests`, `documentrequests`, and `calendarevents` (including a check for residue from the *previous* task's own verification pass, which was confirmed already fully cleaned up).

# Potential Partner — Requests/Monitoring Consolidation — 2026-07-23

Seventh request the same week, scoped to `potential_partner`: merge Partnership Request and Document Request submission into one "Requests" page, remove the now-duplicate per-page history/list tables, move all request tracking onto Monitoring with a richer field set, confirm Document Library auto-archival, and finalize the nav order. This directly builds on and partially supersedes the 2026-07-22 Potential Partner work (which had just given the role a *separate* Document Requests page) and the 2026-07-21 deep-link work (whose `potential_partner` row-highlight targets needed to move along with the tables they pointed at).

## Pages Merged

- **`views/potential_partner/partner_requests.ejs`** now hosts both request forms behind the same tab convention already established on `views/administrator/partnership_requests.ejs` (`.section-tabs`/`.section-tab`/`switchSection()` — reused verbatim rather than re-invented, per the instruction to reuse the same design across roles where appropriate). Partnership Request keeps every existing field, the OCR auto-fill flow, and the draft-resume-on-entry behavior (2026-07-21) unchanged. Document Request is the same form/validation/`POST /api/document-requests` call previously on its own page, with `dr-`-prefixed element ids/function names (`dr-f-inst`, `submitDocRequest()`, etc.) so it can coexist on one page without colliding with the Partnership Request form's own `f-inst`/`submitRequest()`. The two toast systems the source pages used were unified onto the Partnership Request page's existing one (`#reg-toast`) rather than running two competing toast UIs side by side.
- **`views/potential_partner/partner_document_requests.ejs`** (created 2026-07-22) is deleted — its content is now the Document Request tab above. `GET /partner/document-requests` is removed from `cirl.js`; confirmed `404` after removal.
- **The "Request History" table** (Partnership Requests) and **"My Document Requests" table** (Document Requests) are both gone from the Requests page entirely — replaced by the two new Monitoring tables below. The Requests page is now submission-only, as specified.

## Navigation Changes

- `views/partials/sidebar_partner.ejs`: "Partnership Requests" + "Document Requests" (two items) collapsed into a single **"Requests"** item; final order is now Dashboard, Requests, Monitoring, Document Library, Notifications, Calendar, Settings — matching the requested order exactly (Settings/Logout stay under the existing "Account" group, unchanged).

## Workflow Improvements

- **Monitoring (`views/potential_partner/partner_monitoring.ejs`)** gained two new full-width tables, **"My Partnership Requests"** and **"My Document Requests"**, each showing Institution, Request Type, Status, Date Submitted, Last Updated, Current Stage, Administrator, Attached Documents, and Actions — both reading from the existing `/api/requests/mine` and `/api/document-requests/mine` endpoints (no new endpoints). "Request Type" shows Partnership vs. Renewal for the PR table (from the existing `isRenewal` flag) and the requested document type (MOA/MOU/Accreditation) for the DR table. "Current Stage" is a human-readable label derived client-side from the same `status` field the Status column shows (e.g. Pending → "Awaiting Review") — not a second, independent piece of state that could drift out of sync with Status.
- **`cirl.js` gained two new fields, added at every write path** so "Last Updated" and "Administrator" are never fabricated: `updatedAt` (ISO timestamp, set on every create/edit/submit/withdraw/decide/supporting-doc-upload for both `requests` and `documentrequests`) and `decidedBy` (the reviewing Administrator's name, set only when a decision PATCH actually changes status). Neither field existed before this task — both were necessary additions, not something reusable from elsewhere, since no prior code tracked either.
- **Withdraw (Partnership Request) and Cancel (Document Request)** — previously only reachable from the now-removed history tables — moved to Monitoring's Actions column, calling the exact same existing endpoints (`POST /api/requests/:id/withdraw`, `DELETE /api/document-requests/:id`) with no logic changes. A Draft row gets a "Continue" link back to `/partner/requests`, which auto-resumes it (2026-07-21 behavior, unchanged) rather than duplicating edit-draft logic on Monitoring.
- **Notification deep-links now target Monitoring, not Requests**, since that's where the tables live. `prLinkForRole('potential_partner', id)` → `/partner/monitoring?type=pr&id=<id>`; a new `drLinkForRole(role, id)` helper (mirroring `prLinkForRole`) → `/partner/monitoring?type=dr&id=<id>` for `potential_partner`, `/personnel/requests?open=dr&id=<id>` for Auth. Personnel (unchanged), `/partnership-requests?open=dr&id=<id>` for Administrator. The `type` param is load-bearing: Partnership Request and Document Request ids are independently auto-incrementing, so `?id=3` alone is ambiguous between the two collections — `type` disambiguates which table/row `openFromQuery()` targets.
- **Bug fixed in passing:** the two Document Request status-change notifications (`Fulfilled`/`Rejected`/`Under Review`, and the admin-supporting-document-upload notification) were hardcoded to `link: '/personnel/requests?open=dr&id=' + id` regardless of who actually submitted the request — harmless before 2026-07-22 (only Auth. Personnel could submit Document Requests), but wrong the moment `potential_partner` gained that ability, since a partner has no access to `/personnel/requests`. Both call sites now look up the requester's real role and resolve through `drLinkForRole()`.

## Document Library Integration

No backend changes were needed here — inspection found the existing pipeline already satisfies the requirement completely:
- Every file run through `/api/ocr/extract` (used by the Partnership Request form's "Auto-fill from Document" upload) is archived to the Document Library **unconditionally**, even if OCR extraction itself fails (`services/ocrService.js`'s `archiveUpload()`, called from both the success and error branches of `runJob()`) — ownership (`uploadedByEmail`), upload date, and original filename are always preserved, and the file is written to disk exactly once (no separate "attach to request" copy — `attachmentLink` on the request is simply the same archived file's `fileLink`).
- Document Requests have no submitter-side upload by design (established 2026-07-20/22: a Document Request asks CIRL *for* a document; new partnerships/uploads happen through Partnership Requests). When an Administrator later fulfills one, `POST /api/document-requests/:id/documents` already archives that file to the *requester's* Document Library (ownership set to `target.requestedByEmail`, not the admin's) — implemented in the 2026-07-20 Administrator task, re-verified live here rather than rebuilt.
- The two `supportingDocuments`-array updates (`POST /api/requests/:id/documents`, `POST /api/document-requests/:id/documents`) now also bump `updatedAt` on the parent request in the same `updateOne` call (`$push` + `$set` together), so "Last Updated" on Monitoring reflects a CIRL-side document upload too, not just a status decision.

## Verification Performed (live, real accounts, no synthetic Mongo data)

A stale server process from a prior session was found holding port 3000 and killed before starting fresh. Real disposable accounts were created via `POST /signup` for `potential_partner`, Administrator, Auth. Personnel, and View-Only.

- **Requests page, both forms:** submitted a real Partnership Request (`POST /api/requests` → `id: 9`) and a real Document Request (`POST /api/document-requests` → `id: 3`) as the partner test account — both `200`, both correctly `submittedByEmail`/`requestedByEmail`-owned, both carrying a real `updatedAt` from creation.
- **Monitoring, both request types, own-scoped:** `GET /api/requests/mine` and `GET /api/document-requests/mine` for the partner test account each returned exactly the one request that account submitted.
- **Status updates + new fields:** approved the Partnership Request and fulfilled the Document Request as the Administrator test account — both responses carried a real `decidedBy: "zztest_a3_..."` and a freshly bumped `updatedAt`, confirming the fields are populated by real admin actions, not placeholders.
- **Deep-link disambiguation:** the resulting notifications carried `link: "/partner/monitoring?type=pr&id=9"` and `link: "/partner/monitoring?type=dr&id=3"` respectively. A Node harness ran the real `<script>` block from `partner_monitoring.ejs` (mocked DOM with a real `querySelector`-capable fake body per table, real API responses) for both query strings — confirmed `openFromQuery()` correctly scrolled to and highlighted row `id=9` in the **PR** table only when `type=pr`, and row `id=3` in the **DR** table only when `type=dr` — proving the `type` param actually resolves the ambiguity rather than coincidentally working.
- **Document Library auto-archival, no duplicates:** uploaded a real 1×1 PNG through `/api/ocr/extract` as the partner (OCR itself failed on the blank image, as expected) — confirmed via `GET /api/documents/mine` that the file was archived anyway (`status: "Needs Review"`, tag `"OCR Failed"`, honest rather than fabricated), owned by the partner, with real `uploadedAt`/`originalFilename`, and exactly one document record (no duplicate). Then had the Administrator upload a supporting document to both the Partnership Request and the Document Request (`POST /api/requests/9/documents`, `POST /api/document-requests/3/documents`) — confirmed both new documents appeared in the *partner's* (not the admin's) Document Library with correct `requestId`/`requestType` association, and that both parent requests' `supportingDocuments` array and `updatedAt` were updated in the same write.
- **Navigation:** fetched `/partner/requests` and confirmed both `panel-pr`/`panel-dr` and `tab-pr`/`tab-dr` are present; confirmed `GET /partner/document-requests` now `404`s.
- **Regression — Administrator:** `/partnership-requests`, `GET /api/document-requests` (admin list), `GET /api/requests` (admin list), `/dashboard`, `/registry` all unaffected.
- **Regression — Auth. Personnel:** `/personnel/requests` still `200`; submitting and fulfilling a real Document Request as Auth. Personnel still works identically, and its resulting notification still carries the unchanged `/personnel/requests?open=dr&id=<id>` link — confirming the new `drLinkForRole()` role-branch for Auth. Personnel preserved the exact prior behavior.
- **Regression — View-Only:** dashboard and `/viewonly/request-access` unaffected; `GET /api/document-requests/mine` still correctly `302`s (View-Only remains excluded from `requireRequester`, untouched by this task).
- **Static:** `node -c cirl.js` clean; `ejs.renderFile()` clean on all three touched templates (`partner_requests.ejs`, `partner_monitoring.ejs`, `sidebar_partner.ejs`).
- `npm test`: **27/27 passed.**
- No browser/Playwright automation is available in this environment — all "the right row/table highlighted" and "the right data rendered" claims above are backed by Node-harness execution of the actual shipped `<script>` content against real, live API responses, not a rendered-browser screenshot; stated plainly rather than implied.
- All test accounts, the real Partnership/Document Requests, and the three physical test-upload files created during Document Library verification were deleted afterward (the physical files needed manual removal from `uploads/documents/` since no document-deletion API exists in the app — noted rather than silently left behind). A final repo-wide check confirmed zero `zztest_`-prefixed users and zero leftover `ZZ Test`-pattern documents across `requests`, `documentrequests`, and `documents`.

# Auth. Personnel Role Update — 2026-07-23

Eighth request the same week, scoped entirely to `Auth. Personnel`: strip Registry/Reports/Audit-Trail/Calendar-editing/Upload & Extract access, reverse a prior decision to let them submit Partnership Requests and renewals, rename dashboard language, and remove two dead-weight UI sections. Several of the touched templates (`administrator/calendar.ejs`, `administrator/lifecycle.ejs`, `administrator/documents.ejs`) are shared with Administrator via `sidebarPartial` — every removal there is a role-conditional, not a deletion, so Administrator keeps 100% of existing functionality.

## Files Changed

- `cirl.js` — RBAC and workflow changes (full list below).
- `views/partials/sidebar_personnel.ejs` — removed the "Registry" and "Reports & Analytics" nav items (and the now-empty "Management" section header); renamed "Document Requests" → "Requests".
- `views/auth. personnel/personnel_dashboard.ejs` — renamed, recharted, decluttered (below).
- `views/auth. personnel/personnel_requests.ejs` — rebuilt as a tabbed Partnership Request / Document Request page (below). `views/auth. personnel/personnel_reports.ejs` — **deleted**.
- `views/administrator/calendar.ejs` (shared, Admin + Personnel) — Create/Edit/Delete gated behind `canManageCalendar` (`user.role === 'Administrator'`).
- `views/administrator/lifecycle.ejs` (shared, Admin + Personnel — this is Personnel's "Monitoring" page) — "DSS Smart Alerts" card gated to Administrator only.
- `views/administrator/documents.ejs` (shared, Admin + Personnel) — "Upload & Extract" button + modal gated to Administrator only.
- `test/partnerships.test.js` — updated for the new Registry RBAC (below).

## Routes Modified (`cirl.js`)

| Route | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| `GET /registry` | `requirePersonnel` | `requireAdmin` |
| `GET /reports` | `requirePersonnel` | `requireAdmin` |
| `GET /personnel/reports` | `requirePersonnel` | **removed** |
| `GET /api/reports/partnerships/pdf`\|`excel` | `requirePersonnel` | `requireAdmin` |
| `GET /api/reports/activitylog/pdf`\|`excel` | `requirePersonnel` | `requireAdmin` |
| `GET /api/activitylogs` | `requirePersonnel` | `requireAdmin` |
| `POST/PATCH/DELETE /api/partnerships(/:id)` | `requirePersonnel` | `requireAdmin` |
| `POST/PATCH/DELETE /api/calendarevents(/:id)` | `requirePersonnel` | `requireAdmin` |
| `POST /api/requests` (fresh Partnership Request) | 403-blocked Auth. Personnel | **allowed** (block removed) |

`GET /api/calendarevents` (view), `GET /api/partnerships` and `/api/partnerships/stats` (read), `POST /api/partnerships/:id/renew-request` (renewal), and all `/api/requests/*` / `/api/document-requests/*` self-service routes were **not** touched — Personnel's viewing/submitting/monitoring capabilities all ride on routes that were already correctly scoped generically (by session email), not by role, so no new backend logic was needed for the capabilities being *added*.

## Workflow Changes

- **Partnership Requests + Renewals reversed:** a 2026-07-20 decision ("Auth. Personnel no longer initiate new partnerships... submit document requests instead") is explicitly superseded by this task. The `403` block in `POST /api/requests` is removed. `POST /api/partnerships/:id/renew-request` was already `requireAuth` and already ownership-checked purely by `submittedByEmail` matching an Approved request's institution name — fully role-agnostic, so it started working correctly for Personnel the moment they could submit and get approved for Partnership Requests, with **zero backend changes**. Verified live: duplicate-renewal rejection (`409`) and cross-user ownership rejection (`403`) both still enforced unchanged.
- **`views/auth. personnel/personnel_requests.ejs` rebuilt** with the same `.section-tabs`/`switchSection()` tab convention used on the Administrator and `potential_partner` merged Requests pages (reuse, not reinvention): a **Partnership Request** tab (form ported from the `potential_partner` Requests page, OCR auto-fill included, `pr-`-prefixed ids to avoid colliding with the pre-existing Document Request form's `f-inst`/`f-type`/`f-notes`) plus, on the same tab, a **My Partnerships — Renewal** section and a **My Partnership Requests** table reusing the exact rich-field renderer (Institution, Request Type, Status, Date Submitted, Last Updated, Current Stage, Administrator, Attached Documents, Actions) built for `potential_partner`'s Monitoring page in the prior task — `updatedAt`/`decidedBy` are generic fields already set by the shared backend routes, so no new data plumbing was needed. Unlike `potential_partner`, Personnel's request tracking stays **inline on the Requests page** rather than moving to a separate Monitoring page — Personnel's "Monitoring" nav item already means something different (the org-wide Lifecycle view), so splitting would have conflated two unrelated concepts.
- **Document Request tab is untouched** — the pre-existing form, table, and detail modal (and their `/api/document-requests/*` calls) are reused byte-for-byte, just re-homed under a tab.
- **Deep-link disambiguation:** `prLinkForRole()` gained an `Auth. Personnel` branch → `/personnel/requests?type=pr&id=<id>` (their PR tracking has no page of its own to link to, matching the `potential_partner` `type=`/`id=` convention from the prior task). The pre-existing `?open=dr&id=<id>` convention for Document Requests is unchanged. Both now coexist on one page's `openFromQuery()`.

## UI Updates

- **Dashboard** (`personnel_dashboard.ejs`, not shared with Administrator): title changed to **"Institutional Decision Support System (DSS)"**; "Partnerships by College / Unit" renamed to **"Partnerships by Institution"**, and — since this whole dashboard was 100% hardcoded/demo data with no backing fetch at all — that one chart was wired to real `GET /api/partnerships/stats` `byInstitution` data (a `fetch` + `updateOptions` call added; nothing else on this dashboard was touched, per "do not perform unrelated refactoring" — the rest of the hardcoded map/countries/stat-cards are a pre-existing, separately-tracked gap). "Quick Actions" card removed; "Expiring Partnerships" widened from `col-xl-8` to `col-xl-12` so no empty space is left. Two dead links that pointed at the now-Administrator-only `/reports` were repointed to `/personnel/lifecycle` (Monitoring) as a direct, necessary consequence of the Reports removal.
- **Calendar** (shared): "Create New Event" button and the external-event drag palette are no longer rendered for Personnel (`<% if (canManageCalendar) %>`); FullCalendar's `editable`/`droppable`/`selectable` are all set to the same flag, so drag-to-reschedule, resize, and day-click-to-add are disabled at the calendar-engine level, not just hidden; the Edit/Delete buttons inside the event-detail modal stay in the DOM (to avoid null-reference JS errors) but are only ever unhidden when `CAN_MANAGE` is true. Viewing events and event details is fully preserved — `eventClick` → `showView()` is unconditional.
- **Monitoring** (`/personnel/lifecycle`, shared as `administrator/lifecycle.ejs`): the "DSS Smart Alerts" card removed for Personnel. No JS anywhere populates that card's `dss-action-text`/`dss-review-text` (confirmed via search — it was dead placeholder markup, permanently "Loading…"), so removal has zero functional side effects. It sits at the bottom of a single sidebar column with no row-siblings, so "reflow" required no further layout changes — the column is simply shorter.
- **Document Library** (shared): "Upload & Extract" button and its modal (+ OCR JS) are Administrator-only now; search, tag filters, grid/list view toggle, and view/download links are all outside that conditional and fully preserved.
- **Notifications:** already satisfied — the Summary-pie-chart removal and real-data stat tiles were built into the *shared* `administrator/notifications.ejs` template in the 2026-07-21 task, which `/personnel/notifications` already renders. No code change was needed here; verified live instead of rebuilt.
- **Reports & Analytics:** `personnel_reports.ejs` (Reports tab + "Strategic Insights (DSS)" tab + Custom Report Builder with an Audit-Trail export option) deleted outright; nothing else referenced it.

## RBAC

- **Gained:** submit Partnership Requests (fresh + renewal), submit Document Requests (unchanged, already had this), monitor own requests of both types.
- **Lost:** Registry (page + `POST/PATCH/DELETE /api/partnerships`), Reports & Analytics (both their own page and the shared admin one), Audit Trail exports, Calendar create/edit/delete (view-only now), Document Library Upload & Extract.
- **Unchanged:** Dashboard, Monitoring (Lifecycle), Document Library view/download, Notifications, Settings — all still Personnel-accessible exactly as before.
- Administrator, `potential_partner`, and View-Only RBAC are untouched — every restriction above is either a brand-new `Auth. Personnel`-only removal or a `requirePersonnel` → `requireAdmin` narrowing that only affects the role losing access.

## Verification Performed (live, real accounts, no synthetic Mongo data)

A stale server process from a prior session was found holding port 3000 and killed before starting fresh. Four real disposable accounts (Administrator, Auth. Personnel, `potential_partner`, View-Only) were created via `POST /signup`.

- **RBAC boundaries (Personnel):** `GET /registry` → `302`, `GET /reports` → `302`, `GET /personnel/reports` → `404`, `GET /api/activitylogs` → `302`, `POST /api/partnerships` → `302`, `POST /api/calendarevents` → `302`. `/personnel/requests`, `/personnel/dashboard`, `/personnel/lifecycle`, `/personnel/documents`, `/personnel/calendar` all still `200`.
- **Partnership Request + renewal, real workflow:** submitted a real Partnership Request as Personnel (`POST /api/requests` → `200`, real `updatedAt`), approved it as Administrator (real `decidedBy`), manually seeded one matching `partnerships` record (the approval step itself doesn't auto-create a Registry entry — that's a separate, pre-existing manual step, unchanged by this task), then confirmed `GET /api/partnerships/mine` picked it up with zero backend changes, submitted a real renewal request (`200`), confirmed a second attempt correctly `409`s ("already pending"), and confirmed a *different* test account attempting to renew this same partnership correctly `403`s ("You can only request renewal for your own partnership").
- **Document Request, real workflow:** submitted (`200`, real `updatedAt`), fulfilled by Administrator (real `decidedBy`), notification carried the unchanged `/personnel/requests?open=dr&id=<id>` link.
- **Deep-link disambiguation, both kinds, on one page:** the real notifications from the two flows above carried `link: "/personnel/requests?type=pr&id=9"` and `link: "/personnel/requests?open=dr&id=3"`. A Node harness ran the actual `<script>` block from the rebuilt `personnel_requests.ejs` (mocked DOM, real API responses) for both query strings — confirmed `type=pr` switches to the PR tab and highlights row `id=9` in `pr-monitor-body`, and `open=dr` switches to the DR tab and opens the existing detail modal populated with the real institution name — proving both the tab-switch and the two independent deep-link mechanisms coexist correctly on one page.
- **Calendar:** confirmed live HTML — Administrator's `/calendar` contains "Create New Event"; Personnel's `/personnel/calendar` does not. `GET /api/calendarevents` (view) still `200` for Personnel.
- **Document Library:** confirmed live HTML — Administrator's `/documents` contains "Upload & Extract"; Personnel's `/personnel/documents` does not, while search-box and tag-filter markup are both still present. `GET /api/documents` still `200` for Personnel.
- **Monitoring:** confirmed live HTML — Administrator's `/lifecycle` contains "DSS Smart Alerts"; Personnel's `/personnel/lifecycle` does not.
- **Notifications:** confirmed live HTML on `/personnel/notifications` — zero occurrences of the old pie-chart JS (`getChartColorsArray`/`initPieChart`), real stat-tile markup present.
- **Regression — Administrator:** `/registry`, `/reports`, `/api/activitylogs`, and a real `POST`+`DELETE /api/calendarevents` round-trip all still `200`; `/calendar` still shows "Create New Event"; `/documents` still shows "Upload & Extract"; `/lifecycle` still shows "DSS Smart Alerts".
- **Regression — `potential_partner` and View-Only:** all of last task's routes (`/partner/dashboard`, `/partner/requests`, `/partner/monitoring`, `/partner/documents`, `/partner/calendar`, `/viewonly/dashboard`, `/viewonly/request-access`) re-checked and unaffected. (One re-login was needed mid-verification after an idle-session expiry unrelated to this change — not a regression, confirmed by re-authenticating and re-running the same checks successfully.)
- **Test suite regression caught and fixed:** `npm test` initially failed 4 pre-existing tests in `test/partnerships.test.js`, which encoded the *old* RBAC (Auth. Personnel could Add/Edit/Delete Registry records). Updated the suite to match the new, intentional RBAC — Add/Edit/Delete now run as Administrator, and a new test asserts Personnel gets `302` on all three write endpoints without mutating the record — rather than reverting the RBAC change to make the old assertions pass.
- **Static:** `node -c cirl.js` clean; `ejs.renderFile()` clean on all touched templates, rendered once per affected role (`Administrator` and `Auth. Personnel`) for the three shared templates to confirm both branches of every conditional render without error.
- `npm test`: **28/28 passed** (27 previous + 1 new).
- No browser/Playwright automation is available in this environment — all "the right button/card is present or absent" and "the right tab/modal opened" claims above are backed by live HTML fetches and Node-harness execution of the actual shipped `<script>` content, not a rendered-browser screenshot; stated plainly rather than implied.
- All test accounts and the real Partnership Requests, Document Requests, and seeded partnership record were deleted afterward. A final repo-wide check confirmed zero `zztest_`-prefixed users and zero leftover `ZZ Test`-pattern documents across `requests`, `documentrequests`, and `partnerships`.

# Printable CSPC-F-CIRL-04 Document Request Form — 2026-07-24

Ninth request: every submitted Document Request can now be viewed, printed, and downloaded as a PDF that matches the official CSPC-CIRL paper form (photo of the physical form supplied by the user), reusing the existing `documentrequests` workflow rather than building a parallel one.

## Files Changed

- `cirl.js` — new fields on the existing create/decide routes, two new print/PDF routes, one new PDF-rendering helper (all listed below).
- `views/print/document_request_print.ejs` — **new**. Standalone (no sidebar/header partials) HTML view styled for A4, matching the paper form's layout.
- `views/auth. personnel/personnel_requests.ejs` — added Contact No. / Document Form fields to the submission form; added Print/Download buttons to the existing `request-view-modal`.
- `views/potential_partner/partner_requests.ejs` — same two new fields added to its own Document Request tab's form.
- `views/potential_partner/partner_monitoring.ejs` — Print/Download icon-buttons added to each Document Request row (this role's DR table has no separate "details" modal — the row's action column is the closest existing equivalent, so buttons went there rather than building a new modal for a single feature).
- `views/administrator/partnership_requests.ejs` — Released By / Received By optional inputs and a sign-off summary added to the existing `dr-modal`; Print/Download links added to its footer; `resolveDR()` forwards the two optional fields only when the decision is "Fulfilled".
- `test/document-request-print.test.js` — **new**, 6 tests covering the new fields and the print/PDF RBAC boundary.

## What Was Reused vs. Added

Inspection of the existing Document Request workflow (`POST/GET/PATCH /api/document-requests*`, the `documentrequests` collection, `requireRequester`) found most of the paper form's fields already captured:
