- **Reused as-is:** Name of Requestor (`requestedBy`), Office/College/Institution (`institution`), Email Address (`requestedByEmail`), Document/s Requested (`documentType`, expanded via the existing `DOC_TYPE_LABELS`/`expandDocTypeLabel`), Purpose (the existing `notes` field — same placeholder text, "Describe what's needed and why", was already exactly this; no rename to avoid touching working UI/API for a label-only change), Approved By / Approval Date (the existing `decidedBy`/`updatedAt` pair, set on every fulfill/reject decision).
- **Genuinely new fields** (nothing in the schema captured these): `contactNumber` and `documentForm` (`'Printed Copy'`/`'Digital Copy'`), both optional inputs added to the submission form and stored on creation; `submittedAt` (ISO timestamp — the pre-existing `date` field is date-only, and the form needs a real time); `releasedBy`/`releasedAt` and `receivedBy`/`receivedAt`, optional text captured only when an Administrator fulfills a request (mirrors the paper form's three separate signature lines — Approved/Released/Received are physically often the same staff member and moment in a small records office, so all three timestamps naturally collapse to one PATCH call rather than requiring a multi-step release/receipt workflow that doesn't otherwise exist in this system).
- **Zero backend changes were needed for RBAC** — `canAccessDocumentRequest(user, target)` is a two-line helper (`Administrator` → any request; anyone else → `target.requestedByEmail === user.email`), layered on top of the pre-existing `requireRequester` middleware (Administrator, Auth. Personnel, `potential_partner`). This satisfies all four RBAC bullets in the request as written: Auth. Personnel and `potential_partner` have no delegated "manage other users' requests" capability anywhere else in the app today (only Administrator has the cross-user review queue, via `GET /api/document-requests`), so for those two roles "requests they are authorized to manage" is, in the current system, identical to "their own requests" — the same ownership check already used by the existing `DELETE /api/document-requests/:id` (cancel) route.

## Print/PDF Implementation

- `GET /document-requests/:id/print` (`requireRequester` + `canAccessDocumentRequest`) renders `views/print/document_request_print.ejs` — plain HTML/CSS with `@media print` rules (`.no-print` toolbar hidden, `@page { size: A4 }`), a "Print" button (`window.print()`) and a "Download PDF" button/link.
- `GET /api/document-requests/:id/pdf` (same RBAC) streams a PDF built with **pdfkit** (already a project dependency — no new packages installed), via a new `renderDocumentRequestPdf()` helper modeled on the existing `renderTablePdf()` (same `doc.on('error', …)`/`res.on('error', …)` mid-stream failure handling already used by the Reports PDF exports, portrait A4 instead of landscape). `Content-Disposition: attachment; filename="Document_Request_<id>.pdf"` — a meaningful, request-specific filename as required.
- Both routes share one `drFormFields(r)` helper (date/time formatting, document-type label expansion, signature-date formatting) so the HTML print view and the PDF can never drift apart on wording or formatting.
- Layout mirrors the photographed paper form: CSPC seal (reusing the existing `/images/cirl-logo.jpg` already used as the site logo — no new asset needed) + "Republic of the Philippines / CAMARINES SUR POLYTECHNIC COLLEGES / Nabua, Camarines Sur / CENTER FOR INTERNATIONAL RELATIONS AND LINKAGES" header block, "CSPC-F-CIRL-04" code top-right, blue rule, "DOCUMENTS REQUEST FORM" title, a bordered label/value table for the eight requestor-facing fields (with a real checkbox glyph next to whichever Document Form option was selected), a three-column Approved By / Released By / Received By signature block with date lines, and a footer rule with "Effectivity Date: February 2023 · Rev. 1 · Page 1 of 1".
- **Bug caught during live verification and fixed:** the PDF's per-row value-rendering closure (`const text = (v) => (val, x, top, w) => …`) had an extra leading parameter that shifted every argument by one position when `row()` invoked it with only 3 arguments — every field value rendered on top of the last row's cell instead of its own. Caught by reading the generated PDF directly (visual inspection, not just text-content grep) before/after the fix; confirmed corrected by re-reading the regenerated PDF.

## RBAC Verification

Four disposable accounts (Administrator, Auth. Personnel "owner", `potential_partner` "other", plus session reuse of the personnel account as the requester) were used against a real submitted-then-fulfilled Document Request:

| Caller | `/document-requests/:id/print` | `/api/document-requests/:id/pdf` |
|---|---|---|
| Owner (Auth. Personnel who submitted it) | `200` | `200` |
| Administrator (not the owner) | `200` | `200` |
| Unrelated `potential_partner` (not the owner) | `403` | `403` |
| No session | `302` | `302` |

## Testing Performed

- **Static:** `node -c cirl.js` clean; `ejs.renderFile()` clean on the new print template and all four touched role templates (rendered once per role where relevant).
- **Live, real accounts (`POST`-free DB seeding via a disposable helper script, deleted afterward), stale server (PID 18756, a prior session's process) found and killed first:**
  - Submitted a real Document Request as Auth. Personnel with `contactNumber`, `documentForm`, and `notes` — confirmed all three plus a real `submittedAt` persisted in the API response.
  - Fetched the print view as the owner before any decision — confirmed every submitted field renders, and the correct Document Form checkbox is checked (the HTML-escaping bug above was caught here: `<%=` was escaping the `&#10003;` checkmark entity into visible text — fixed by switching that one interpolation to `<%-`).
  - Downloaded the PDF as the owner — `Content-Type: application/pdf`, `Content-Disposition` filename `Document_Request_3.pdf`, valid single-page PDF confirmed via `file`.
  - Fulfilled the request as Administrator with `releasedBy`/`receivedBy` — confirmed the PATCH response carries `decidedBy`, `releasedBy`, `releasedAt`, `receivedBy`, `receivedAt`; re-fetched the print view and PDF and visually confirmed (by reading the regenerated PDF directly) all three signature blocks and dates now populate correctly, which is also where the row-alignment bug above was caught and, after the fix, re-confirmed correct.
  - Ran the full RBAC matrix above.
- **New Jest suite** (`test/document-request-print.test.js`, 6 tests): field persistence on submit, owner access, non-owner `403`, Administrator access, no-session `302`, and Approved/Released/Received By populating after fulfillment.
- `npm test`: **34/34 passed** (28 previous + 6 new), run twice — once before and once after the PDF row-alignment fix — with no regressions to any other module.
- No browser/Playwright automation is available in this environment; visual fidelity to the paper form was verified by reading the actual generated PDF bytes directly (not a rendered-browser screenshot) both before and after the layout bug fix, rather than relying on text-content assertions alone.
- All test accounts, the one real Document Request, and their notifications were deleted afterward via the disposable helper script, which was itself deleted along with all temporary verification files. A final residue check confirmed zero `zztest_dr_`-prefixed users and zero leftover `ZZ Test`-pattern document requests.

## Follow-up — Submission Form Visual Redesign (2026-07-25)

The initial pass above added the new fields and the print/PDF views, but left the two Document Request **submission forms** (`personnel_requests.ejs`, `partner_requests.ejs`) on their original generic Bootstrap two-column grid. Feedback: the requirement was for the live web form itself to visually resemble the CSPC-F-CIRL-04 paper form's structure, not just gain the new fields inside the old grid. This follow-up is frontend-only — no routes, APIs, database schema, or RBAC were touched (confirmed: `cirl.js` has no changes in this follow-up).

**Files changed:** `views/auth. personnel/personnel_requests.ejs`, `views/potential_partner/partner_requests.ejs` (styles + markup only).

- Both Document Request submission forms were rebuilt from a `row g-4`/`col-md-6` Bootstrap grid into a bordered label/value **row table** (`.dr-form-table`/`.dr-row`/`.dr-label`/`.dr-value`, custom CSS, not a Bootstrap grid), directly mirroring the paper form's field order: Name of Requestor, Office/College/Institution, Date/Time, Contact No., Document(s) Requested, Purpose, Document Form, Email Address. A branded header strip (`/images/cirl-logo.jpg` + "Documents Request Form" + "CSPC-F-CIRL-04" code, same blue accent color already used elsewhere in the app) replaces the plain card title, reusing the existing branding rather than introducing new colors/assets.
- **Read-only, auto-filled fields:** Name of Requestor and Email Address now render server-side straight from `user.name`/`user.email` (EJS, no JS needed — these were already the authoritative values the backend uses regardless of what the old form's inputs held) as styled read-only boxes, not editable inputs. Date/Time is a client-side live display (`toLocaleString()` at page load) — purely cosmetic, since the server has always independently stamped `submittedAt` at the real moment of submission; it is not sent with the request.
- **Office/College/Institution auto-fill:** for Auth. Personnel, `user.unit` is already present in the session (confirmed via `test/helpers.js`/`partnerships.test.js`), so when non-empty it renders server-side as a read-only box plus a hidden `#f-inst` input carrying the same value — no client round-trip needed. `potential_partner` has no `unit`, but does have a saved `organization` on their profile (`GET /api/partner/profile`, pre-existing endpoint, already used by Settings) — a client-side fetch on page load fills and locks `#dr-f-inst` when an organization is on file, and leaves it a normal editable input otherwise ("when available", per the requirement's own wording — there is no server-side guarantee a partner has saved one yet).
- **Only the four specified fields stay editable:** Contact No. (`#f-contact`/`#dr-f-contact`, unchanged text input), Document(s) Requested (`#f-type`/`#dr-f-type` — kept as the existing `<select>` of MOA/MOU/Accreditation rather than converted to free text, since the backend's `DOCUMENT_REQUEST_TYPES` enum validation was explicitly required to stay unchanged; a free-text textarea would either be silently truncated to those three values or start failing validation, so the dropdown was kept but restyled into the paper form's taller "Document/s to be Requested" row with an explanatory caption), Purpose (`#f-notes`/`#dr-f-notes`, the pre-existing `notes` textarea, unchanged field name/behavior, just relabeled "Purpose" to match the paper form), and Document Form — **changed from a `<select>` to two radio buttons** ("Printed Copy"/"Digital Copy"), a closer visual match to the paper form's checkboxes than a dropdown; the value sent to the backend (`documentForm`) is unchanged.
- `resetForm()`/`resetDocRequestForm()` and `submitRequest()`/`submitDocRequest()` were updated only to read the new radio-group markup (`document.querySelector('input[name="f-form"]:checked')`) in place of the old `<select id="f-form">`, and — for the partner form — to restore the auto-filled organization (not blank) on Clear. The `fetch('/api/document-requests', …)` POST body shape sent to the backend is byte-for-byte identical to before this follow-up.

### Verification Performed

- **Static:** `ejs.renderFile()` clean on `personnel_requests.ejs` for both an Auth. Personnel with a unit and one without (covers both the read-only and editable Office/Institution branches); clean on `partner_requests.ejs`. `node -c cirl.js` clean (confirming, as expected, that a frontend-only change didn't touch it).
- **Live, real accounts, stale server (PID 11780, a prior verification session) found and killed first:**
  - Seeded an Auth. Personnel account with `unit: 'CCS'` and a `potential_partner` account with a saved profile (`organization: 'Naga City State University'`, written directly to the `profiles` collection the same way `POST /api/partner/profile` would).
  - Fetched both live pages and confirmed via the rendered HTML: Name/Email render as static text pulled straight from the session (not the old inputs); the personnel account's Institution renders server-side as a read-only "CCS" box with the "Auto-filled from your profile" hint; the old `<select id="f-form">`/`<select id="dr-f-form">` markup is completely gone (`0` matches) and replaced by two radio inputs each.
  - The partner's organization auto-fill is a client-side `fetch`, so static HTML alone can't prove it runs — used the same Node-harness technique as elsewhere this session (extracted the real `<script>` block from `partner_requests.ejs`, mocked `document`/`fetch`, and ran it against a real-shaped `/api/partner/profile` response): confirmed `#dr-f-inst.value` becomes `"Naga City State University"`, `readOnly` becomes `true`, and the autofill hint's `d-none` class is actually removed by the real shipped code, not just present as dead markup.
  - Submitted a real Document Request through each redesigned form's exact field set (`institution`, `documentType`, `notes`, `contactNumber`, `documentForm` read from the radio group) — both `200`, both persisted with all fields intact, confirming the POST body contract to the unchanged backend still matches exactly.
- `npm test`: **34/34 passed**, unchanged from before this follow-up — expected, since no backend file was touched.
- No browser/Playwright automation is available in this environment; the claim that the redesigned layout "looks like the paper form" rests on the HTML/CSS structure itself (a bordered label/value table matching the photographed form's row order, not a Bootstrap grid) plus the field-by-field content checks above, not a rendered screenshot.
- All test accounts, their profile record, and the two real Document Requests created during verification were deleted afterward via a disposable helper script, which was itself deleted along with the Node-harness scratch file. A final residue check confirmed zero `zztest_dru_`-prefixed users and zero leftover `ZZ Test UI`-pattern document requests.

# Auth. Personnel Requests/Monitoring Split — 2026-07-25

Tenth request the same week: align Auth. Personnel's request workflow with `potential_partner`'s — the Requests page becomes submission-only, and all tracking (Partnership Requests, Renewals, Document Requests) moves to a new Monitoring page. Reuses every existing endpoint (`/api/requests/mine`, `/api/document-requests/mine`, `/api/partnerships/mine`, `/api/partnerships/:id/renew-request`, the Document Request print/PDF routes) — zero new backend data routes were needed, only two page routes and two notification-link helpers.

## Files Changed

- `cirl.js` — added `GET /personnel/monitoring`; updated `prLinkForRole`/`drLinkForRole`'s Auth. Personnel branches to point at it (below).
- `views/partials/sidebar_personnel.ejs` — "Monitoring" nav item repointed from `/personnel/lifecycle` to `/personnel/monitoring` (`activePage` check updated to match).
- `views/auth. personnel/personnel_requests.ejs` — stripped to submission-only (below). No field/validation/endpoint changes to either form.
- `views/auth. personnel/personnel_monitoring.ejs` — **new**, adapted from `potential_partner/partner_monitoring.ejs`.

## What Changed vs. What Was Reused

Inspection confirmed Auth. Personnel's Requests page (rebuilt 2026-07-23) already carried three tracking sections inline — "My Partnerships — Renewal", "My Partnership Requests", "My Document Requests" — plus a Document Request detail modal, none of which exist on `potential_partner`'s equivalent Requests page (that role's tracking already lives entirely on Monitoring, split out in the 2026-07-23 task). This request undoes that one specific difference:

- **Removed from `personnel_requests.ejs`:** all three tracking sections and their two modals (`request-view-modal`, `renewal-request-modal`), and every function that only existed to render/drive them (`renderPRTable`, `loadRenewalStatus`, `openRenewalModal`, `submitRenewalRequest`, `withdrawPRRequest`, `deletePRDraft`, `renderTable`/`loadRequests`/`openRequestView`/`cancelRequest`/`applyFilter`/`drStatusBadge`/`canCancel`, `PR_STAGE_LABEL`, `statusBadgeClass`, `fmtWhen`, `renderAttachedDocs`, `openFromQuery`). The two submission forms (Partnership Request with OCR auto-fill, Document Request with its CSPC-F-CIRL-04 layout) are untouched — same fields, same ids, same `/api/requests` and `/api/document-requests` POST bodies.
- **Kept, slimmed down:** `loadPRHistory()` now only fetches `/api/requests/mine` into memory (no table render) so a new `openFormOnEntry()` — ported from `partner_requests.ejs` — can auto-resume an existing Draft on page load, exactly matching `potential_partner`'s behavior. This is the only reason the Requests page still calls `/api/requests/mine` at all.
- **New `personnel_monitoring.ejs`:** stat cards, "My Partnership Requests" table (Partnership vs. Renewal disambiguated by the pre-existing `isRenewal` flag, same as `partner_monitoring.ejs`), "My Document Requests" table, Approval Progress, Document Verification Status, and Renewal Status + its Request Renewal modal — all ported near-verbatim from `partner_monitoring.ejs`, re-pointed to `/personnel/*` links and `sidebar_personnel`. Two deliberate additions beyond a literal copy, scoped only to this new page (nothing on `partner_monitoring.ejs` was touched):
  - **"Request #" and "Remarks" columns** on both tables (the task's own field list named these explicitly; `partner_monitoring.ejs` doesn't have them). Request # is the existing auto-incrementing `id` field; Remarks is `r.notes` for Partnership Requests (the PATCH route already folds an admin's decision remark into that same field) and `r.remark` for Document Requests (a separate field, unchanged from the print/PDF task).
  - **"View Details" modals** (`pr-detail-modal`, `dr-detail-modal`) — `partner_monitoring.ejs` has no per-row details modal today (only inline row actions), but the task explicitly required "View Details works" as a verification item. Built as two lightweight modals populated entirely from data already in memory from the `/mine` fetches — no new endpoint.
  - **Print/Download** on Document Request rows reuse the existing `/document-requests/:id/print` and `/api/document-requests/:id/pdf` routes (built in the prior task) — both in the table's row actions and in the `dr-detail-modal`'s footer. Partnership Requests have no print/PDF capability anywhere in this system, so none is shown for that table ("if permitted" in the task's own wording).
- **Notification deep-links:** `prLinkForRole('Auth. Personnel', id)` and `drLinkForRole` (Auth. Personnel branch) now resolve to `/personnel/monitoring?type=pr&id=<id>` / `?type=dr&id=<id>` instead of `/personnel/requests?...` — necessary because that page no longer has a table to highlight a row in. `openFromQuery()` (ported verbatim from `partner_monitoring.ejs`) handles both on the new page.
- **`/personnel/lifecycle` was left fully intact** (route, template, RBAC) — only the sidebar link was repointed. Nothing was deleted; the org-wide DSS/Lifecycle view Auth. Personnel previously reached via "Monitoring" is still reachable by direct URL, just no longer the nav item's target, per "keep the implementation minimal."

## RBAC

- `GET /personnel/monitoring` uses `requirePersonnel` — the same middleware every other `/personnel/*` page route already uses (Administrator or Auth. Personnel), not a new access model.
- All tracking data comes from routes that were already email-scoped regardless of role (`/api/requests/mine`, `/api/document-requests/mine`, `/api/partnerships/mine`, `/api/documents/mine`) — no RBAC changes were needed for "monitor only their own requests" since that was already true.
- Administrator, `potential_partner`, and View-Only RBAC are completely untouched.

## Verification Performed (live, real accounts, no synthetic Mongo data)

A stale server process from a prior session was found holding port 3000 and killed before starting fresh. Disposable Administrator, two Auth. Personnel (one "owner", one "other"), `potential_partner`, and View-Only accounts were created directly in MongoDB (mirroring `test/helpers.js`'s approach) and logged in via real `POST /login` calls.

- **Requests page:** fetched `/personnel/requests` as Auth. Personnel and confirmed via grep zero occurrences of "My Partnership Requests", "My Partnerships", "My Document Requests", or any of the removed table/modal ids (`pr-monitor-body`, `req-tbody`, `renewal-status-body`) — only the two submission forms and OCR widgets remain, plus a link to Monitoring.
- **Full real workflow:** submitted a real Partnership Request, a real Document Request (with `contactNumber`/`documentForm`), and — after directly seeding one Approved request + matching partnership record to simulate an existing partnership — a real renewal request (`isRenewal: true`, correct `renewalPartnershipId`). Approved the Partnership Request and fulfilled the Document Request as Administrator.
- **Monitoring page, real data:** captured the real `/api/requests/mine` and `/api/document-requests/mine` responses and ran a Node harness against the actual shipped `<script>` block from `personnel_monitoring.ejs` (mocked DOM/fetch/bootstrap) — confirmed the Partnership Requests table renders both the fresh request and the renewal (correctly labeled "Partnership" vs. "Renewal"), the Document Requests table renders with working Print/Download links, and both `openPRDetail()`/`openDRDetail()` populate their modals with the real institution, type, purpose, and request-number values from the real API data — not just that the HTML skeleton exists.
- **Status propagation:** re-fetched `/mine` after the Administrator's decisions and confirmed real `status`/`decidedBy` values ("Approved"/"Fulfilled", "ZZ Test PM Admin") reached the same data the Monitoring page consumes.
- **Print/Download RBAC:** the owning Auth. Personnel account got `200` on both `/document-requests/:id/print` and `/api/document-requests/:id/pdf`; a second, unrelated Auth. Personnel account got `403` on both — confirming "must NOT view another user's requests" holds for the print/PDF surface specifically, not just the `/mine` list endpoints.
- **Ownership isolation:** the second Auth. Personnel account's own `/api/requests/mine` returned zero results and did not include the first account's request — confirmed requests are genuinely per-user, not merely filtered client-side.
- **Notification deep-links:** fetched real notifications generated by the approval/fulfillment above and confirmed their `link` fields read `/personnel/monitoring?type=pr&id=<id>` and `/personnel/monitoring?type=dr&id=<id>` — the actual notification pipeline, not just the helper function in isolation.
- **Regression — Administrator:** `/dashboard`, `/lifecycle`, `/partnership-requests` all still `200`; both new/old personnel pages (`/personnel/requests`, `/personnel/monitoring`, and the now-unlinked `/personnel/lifecycle`) still reachable via `requirePersonnel`, unchanged from before.
- **Regression — `potential_partner`:** `/partner/dashboard`, `/partner/requests`, `/partner/monitoring` all still `200`; `partner_monitoring.ejs`/`partner_requests.ejs` were not modified by this task.
- **Regression — View-Only:** `/viewonly/dashboard`, `/viewonly/request-access` still `200`; correctly still `302` on `/personnel/monitoring` (role-blocked, same as any other Auth. Personnel-only route).
- **Static:** `node -c cirl.js` clean; `ejs.renderFile()` clean on `personnel_requests.ejs` and `personnel_monitoring.ejs`.
- `npm test`: **34/34 passed**, unchanged — expected, since no test covers page-level HTML and no API route's behavior changed.
- No browser/Playwright automation is available in this environment; "View Details works" and "the right row/table renders real data" are backed by Node-harness execution of the actual shipped `<script>` content against real, live API responses (per this session's established verification method), not a rendered-browser screenshot.
- All test accounts, requests, document requests, the seeded partnership record, and their notifications were deleted afterward via a disposable helper script, which was itself deleted along with all Node-harness scratch files. A final residue check confirmed zero `zztest_pm_`-prefixed users and zero leftover `ZZ Test PM`-pattern requests, document requests, or partnerships.

# Partnership PDF/Excel Export Redesign — 2026-07-26

Eleventh request the same two weeks: redesign the Administrator's Partnership PDF and Excel export into a professional, official-looking report (a photographed CSPC sample report was referenced as inspiration only, explicitly not to be recreated exactly). Both exports keep their existing routes, RBAC, filters, and underlying data shape — only presentation changed.

## Files Changed

- `cirl.js` — new `groupPartnershipDocs()` helper, a new `renderPartnershipReportPdf()` PDF renderer, a new `buildPartnershipExcel()` Excel builder, and small helpers (`PARTNERSHIP_REPORT_COLUMNS`, `PARTNERSHIP_EXCEL_COLUMNS`, `excelColLetter`, `THIN_BORDER`/`CELL_BORDERS`). Both `/api/reports/partnerships/pdf` and `/api/reports/partnerships/excel` now call these instead of building output inline, passing through a computed `periodLabel` and the exporting Administrator's name.
- `test/reports.test.js` — updated one pre-existing assertion whose row-1-is-header assumption no longer holds now that the sheet has a multi-row letterhead block above the data (below).

## What Was Reused vs. What's New

- **Routes, RBAC, filters, and query params are byte-for-byte unchanged.** `requireAdmin`, `buildPartnershipFilter` (`status`/`type`/`cat`/`region`/`unit`), `filterByDateRange` (`dateFrom`/`dateTo`), and the `title` query param all work exactly as before — confirmed live, not assumed.
- **The pre-existing generic `renderTablePdf()` (shared with the Activity Log PDF export) was deliberately left untouched.** This task named the Partnership export specifically; building a separate `renderPartnershipReportPdf()` means the Activity Log PDF's appearance and behavior are provably unaffected, rather than risking a shared-function change nobody asked for.
- **The Excel export's 12-column field set is unchanged** (`Institution, Country, Region, Type, Nature, Category, Unit, Coordinator, Start Date, End Date, Status, Remarks`) — only how it's laid out and styled changed, per "reuse the current export functionality and only improve the presentation."

## Report Design

Both formats share the same structure and logic (`groupPartnershipDocs()` is called from both), so a PDF and an Excel export of the same filter set are the same information in two formats, not two different reports:

- **Letterhead:** the same CIRL branding already used elsewhere in the system — `/images/cirl-logo.jpg` (the existing app logo, not a new asset) plus "Republic of the Philippines / CAMARINES SUR POLYTECHNIC COLLEGES / Nabua, Camarines Sur / CENTER FOR INTERNATIONAL RELATIONS AND LINKAGES", under a blue rule — reused from the same letterhead pattern built for the Document Request print form (2026-07-24).
- **Title, period, and generation info:** the `title` query param (already existed) is upper-cased as the report title; a new `periodLabel` reads `dateFrom`/`dateTo` when present ("Report Period: <from> to <to>") or falls back to "Report Period: All Records"; a generated-timestamp-and-by line uses the real exporting Administrator's session name.
- **Summary strip:** Total Records, a count per Category actually present in the filtered set, and a count per Status (Active/Expiring Soon/Expired) — computed from the same `docs` array being rendered, so it can never disagree with the table beneath it.
- **Grouping:** `groupPartnershipDocs()` groups by `cat` (International/Local, whatever values are actually present) — but only when the filtered export spans more than one category. A `cat` filter already narrows to a single value, so in that case grouping is skipped and a flat table prints instead, satisfying "group records logically... depending on the selected filters" without a redundant single-group header.
- **Table styling:** shaded blue column-header row (repeated on every PDF page and set as Excel's `printTitlesRow`), shaded group-header bars with a running count, alternating row bands, thin borders on every cell, PDF page numbering written after the fact via `bufferPages: true` + `doc.switchToPage()` (the true total isn't known until every row is drawn).
- **Signature block:** Prepared By / Reviewed By / Approved By, one row of labels and one row for names/lines. Prepared By is dynamically populated with the exporting Administrator's real name (`req.session.user.name`); Reviewed By and Approved By are intentionally left blank, since nothing in this system tracks a second/third reviewing party for a report export — exactly what the task allowed ("left blank for manual signatures").
- **Excel-specific:** merged title/subtitle/period/summary rows spanning all 12 columns, a frozen header row (`ySplit` set to the actual header row, not a hardcoded `1`, since the letterhead pushed it down), and auto-fit column widths computed from the longest value actually present in each column (bounded to 45 chars so one long Remarks entry can't blow out the printed page) rather than the fixed guesses used before.

## Bug Caught and Fixed During Live Verification

The first real PDF export produced **6 pages for 20 records** — clearly wrong. Inspecting the actual rendered pages (not just checking `res.status`) showed 3 real content pages interleaved with 3 blank pages containing only the footer text. Root cause: writing the "Page X of Y" footer at a y-coordinate inside the page's bottom margin makes pdfkit's `.text()` treat it as content overflowing the printable area and silently insert a new page before writing — a known pdfkit footer gotcha. Fixed with the standard workaround: temporarily zero `doc.page.margins.bottom` for the duration of each footer write, then restore it. Re-verified: the same 20-record export dropped to the correct 2 pages, with the footer now genuinely sitting in the margin of each real content page.

## Testing Performed

- **Static:** `node -c cirl.js` clean.
- **Live, real accounts, stale server (PID 12404, a prior session's process) found and killed first.** Disposable Administrator and Auth. Personnel accounts were created directly in MongoDB; four realistic partnership records (2 International, 2 Local, spanning Active/Expiring Soon/Expired) were seeded alongside the pre-existing real data.
  - **Visual inspection (not just status-code checks):** read the generated PDF bytes directly for three cases — no filter (all categories, multi-page), `status=Active` (multi-category, grouped), and `cat=International` (single category, flat list) — and confirmed in each: correct letterhead, correct title/period/generated-by text, correct summary counts matching the actual record set, correct group headers and alternating row shading, the column header repeating on page 2, and the Prepared By signature line correctly showing the real Administrator's name.
  - **Excel structural verification:** read the generated `.xlsx` back with ExcelJS itself (not just confirming it opens) and asserted: merged letterhead rows 1–5, a summary row matching the real counts, a styled blue/white/bordered header row at the row it actually landed on, a frozen pane (`ySplit`) matching that same row, `printTitlesRow` set to it, a group-header row ("International (19)") ahead of that category's records, all 12 original columns present and correctly populated per row, auto-fit widths reacting to real data length, and a correctly populated Prepared By / blank Reviewed+Approved By signature block.
  - **Filters:** re-ran both formats with `status=Active`, `cat=International`, and `cat=Local` and confirmed the underlying record set, summary counts, and (for the single-category cases) the collapsed flat-list layout all matched the filter — the pre-existing `buildPartnershipFilter`/`filterByDateRange` logic was not touched, and this confirms it still isn't going through any new code path that could have broken it.
  - **RBAC:** `Auth. Personnel` (this session's Requests/Monitoring redesign didn't change Partnership export access) got `302` on both `/api/reports/partnerships/pdf` and `/excel`; no session also got `302`. `requireAdmin` was not modified.
- **Regression caught and fixed in the existing test suite:** `test/reports.test.js`'s Excel test assumed row 1 was the header and every subsequent row was data — an assumption the new letterhead block breaks. Root-caused (not just patched blindly) to an ExcelJS behavior: reading `.value` on any cell within a merged range returns the merge's single shared value, so a first fix attempt (comparing column 1 vs. column 11) still miscounted the signature block's three independent per-row merges as data rows. Fixed properly by checking that column 11 is one of the three real status strings, which the letterhead, summary, group-header, and signature rows can never coincidentally match.
- `npm test`: **34/34 passed** after the fix above (33/34 on the first run, with the one failure being the pre-existing test's now-invalid assumption, not a bug in the export itself — confirmed by reading the actual generated file before deciding how to fix the test).
- No browser/Playwright automation is available in this environment; the PDF's visual appearance was verified by reading the actual generated PDF bytes directly (the same method used for the Document Request print form earlier this session), and the Excel file's structure was verified by reading it back programmatically with the same library that wrote it — not by opening either in a desktop application.
- All test accounts and the four seeded partnership records were deleted afterward via a disposable helper script, which was itself deleted along with all temporary export files. A final residue check confirmed zero `zztest_rpt_`-prefixed users and zero leftover `ZZ Test RPT seed`-tagged partnerships.

## Follow-up — Summary Strip Removed (2026-07-26)

Feedback: the report should be plainer — remove the Total Records / International / Local / Active / Expiring Soon / Expired summary section from both formats.

- **PDF (`renderPartnershipReportPdf`):** removed the `drawSummary()` function and its call entirely. The page now flows straight from the letterhead/title/period block into the column header and grouped table.
- **Excel (`buildPartnershipExcel`):** removed the merged summary row (and its now-unneeded extra blank spacer row). The header row moved up accordingly (e.g. row 9 → row 7 for a 2-category export); the frozen pane and `printTitlesRow` are computed from the header row's actual number, so both adjusted automatically with no hardcoded row index to fix.
- Category grouping (International/Local section headers within the table) was **not** requested for removal and was kept, along with the letterhead, page numbering, and signature block from the original redesign.
- Verified live: re-exported both formats, confirmed by reading the PDF bytes and reading the Excel back with ExcelJS that the summary content is gone and everything else (letterhead, grouped table, alternating rows, signature block, freeze pane) still renders correctly. `npm test`: **34/34 passed** — the earlier fix to `test/reports.test.js` identifies data rows by status value rather than row position, so it needed no further change despite the row shift.

# User-Scoped Notifications — 2026-07-27

Eleventh request the same stretch: audit every notification-related route/view for cross-user data leaks, close any found, and give Administrator an explicit, separate "view all" audit capability (a deliberate re-addition, not a revert of the 2026-07-18 fix that removed admin's *default* feed from being unscoped).

## Files Modified

- `cirl.js` — one route fixed, one new route added (both listed below). No other notification route needed a change — see inspection findings.
- `views/administrator/notifications.ejs` — added an Administrator-only "My Notifications / All Notifications (Audit)" toggle (shared template with Auth. Personnel via `sidebarPartial`, who never see it).
- `test/notifications.test.js` — **new**, 8 tests covering ownership scoping across all four roles plus the admin audit endpoint.

## Inspection Findings (before any change)

Traced every notification code path: `notifyUsers()` (the sole write path used by real events — Partnership/Document Request submitted, approved, rejected, "additional documents requested", supporting-document uploads — always sets `targetEmail` per recipient, one doc per recipient, never a shared/broadcast doc), the six `/api/notifications*` routes, and every frontend consumer (`views/partials/header.ejs`'s topbar bell — shared by literally every role's layout — plus `administrator/notifications.ejs` and `potential_partner/partner_notifications.ejs`).

Result: **one** unscoped hole, already flagged by a prior comment as "separately tracked, not fixed here" — `GET /api/notifications/unread-count` scoped Administrator/Auth. Personnel/`potential_partner` (`OWN_SCOPE_ROLES`) to their own `targetEmail`, but fell through to a global `{ unread: true }` count with **no owner filter at all** for every other role — in practice, only View-Only, since it's the only role reaching this route that isn't in that list. View-Only's topbar badge was showing the unread count for the *entire system*, not their own. Every other route was already correctly scoped:
- `GET /api/notifications` / `/api/notifications/mine` — both already filter by `targetEmail: req.session.user.email`.
- `PATCH /api/notifications/:id`, `DELETE /api/notifications/:id` — already gated by `canActOnNotification()` (`notif.targetEmail === user.email`), returning `403` otherwise.
- `PATCH /api/notifications/markallread/mine` — already scoped to the caller's own `targetEmail` in the `updateMany` filter.
- `views/partials/header.ejs`'s dropdown already used `/api/notifications/mine`, not the vulnerable count route's logic.

Also found (not a bug, a gap): the automatic partnership-lifecycle check (`cirl.js`, the hourly job) writes notification docs with **no `targetEmail` at all** — genuine system-wide alerts. These were already invisible to every per-user query before this task (never matched `{ targetEmail: email }`) and, before this task, were invisible to literally everyone, including Administrator — there was no surviving "view all" path after the 2026-07-18 fix removed it. The new Administrator audit endpoint (below) is the only place these are visible to anyone, which is the intended fix for "System announcements" in the task's own notification-events list.

## Notification Security Improvements

- **`GET /api/notifications/unread-count`** (`cirl.js`) — removed the `OWN_SCOPE_ROLES`-conditional branch entirely; every authenticated role now unconditionally counts `{ targetEmail: <own email>, unread: true }`. `OWN_SCOPE_ROLES` itself was **not** touched or removed — it's still used, unchanged, by the unrelated Document Library ownership checks (`GET /api/documents`, its download route), where View-Only is *deliberately* left unscoped for a different, already-documented reason. Conflating the two would have been an unrelated behavior change to a feature this task wasn't asked to touch.
- **`GET /api/notifications/all`** (`cirl.js`, new, `requireAdmin`) — returns every notification in the collection, unfiltered, for Administrator audit/management use. Deliberately a separate route from `GET /api/notifications`, so Administrator's own personal feed/badge never silently expands to include other users' notifications — satisfies the task's own "Administrators must not accidentally receive notifications intended only for another user" clause for their *default* view, while still satisfying "Administrators may view all notifications for management and auditing purposes" via an explicit, opt-in second view.
- **`views/administrator/notifications.ejs`** — new "View" pill group (`My Notifications` / `All Notifications (Audit)`), rendered only when `user.role === 'Administrator'` (an `<% if %>` around the markup — Auth. Personnel, sharing this exact template, gets no such option and no such markup in its rendered HTML at all, confirmed by static-render diff). Selecting "All" swaps the fetch source to `/api/notifications/all`, shows a recipient badge per row (`targetEmail`, so an audit actually shows *whose* notification it is), hides "Mark All Read" (that route only ever touches the caller's own notifications — leaving it visible while viewing everyone else's would be misleading), and drops the click-to-mark-read handler on any row not addressed to the logged-in admin (the server would `403` that anyway via `canActOnNotification`, so the UI simply never attempts it).

## RBAC Verification

Four disposable accounts (Administrator, Auth. Personnel, `potential_partner`, View-Only), each with one real notification addressed to them, seeded directly in MongoDB (no API exists for creating arbitrary notifications, by design — see the removed-forgery-route comment already in `cirl.js`).

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Each role's `/api/notifications/unread-count` | `{"count":1}` — exactly their own, for all four roles including View-Only |
| Each role's `/api/notifications/mine` | contains only their own seeded notification |
| `GET /api/notifications` (Administrator/Auth. Personnel) | own-scoped only, not merged with `/all`; `potential_partner`/View-Only still `302` (unchanged RBAC) |
| `GET /api/notifications/all` as Administrator | all four seeded notifications returned |
| `GET /api/notifications/all` as any non-admin, or no session | `302` |
| `PATCH`/`DELETE /api/notifications/:id` on another role's notification id | `403 {"error":"Forbidden."}`, record unmodified |
| Same route, called by the real owner | `200`, succeeds |
| No session, any notification route | `302` (redirect to login, never a data leak) |

## Testing Performed

- **Static:** `node -c cirl.js` clean; `ejs.renderFile()` clean on `administrator/notifications.ejs` for both an Administrator user (audit toggle present in output) and an Auth. Personnel user (toggle markup completely absent from the rendered HTML, not just hidden by CSS).
- **New Jest suite** (`test/notifications.test.js`, 8 tests): own-scoped list per role, own-scoped `GET /api/notifications` with existing RBAC intact, the View-Only unread-count regression test specifically (asserts the DB has more unread notifications system-wide than View-Only owns, then asserts the badge count still matches only View-Only's own — a real assertion, not a coincidence), admin audit endpoint returning everyone's notifications including the unaddressed system-alert shape, admin-audit-is-admin-only, and ownership enforcement on `PATCH`/`DELETE` by id.
- **Live, real accounts, stale server (PID 12636, a prior session) found and killed first:** repeated the full RBAC verification table above against a running server with real HTTP requests (not just Supertest), including the actual cross-account attack (partner attempting to mark, then View-Only attempting to delete, Auth. Personnel's notification by guessed id — both `403`, record unchanged, confirmed by re-reading it from MongoDB afterward).
- **Browser-equivalent verification (no Playwright available):** rendered the real `administrator/notifications.ejs` template through the actual EJS engine as an Administrator user, extracted its real `<script>` block, and ran it in a Node harness against real captured API responses — confirmed the page loads `/api/notifications` (own-scoped) by default and its feed shows only the admin's own notification; confirmed clicking the "All Notifications (Audit)" pill switches the fetch to `/api/notifications/all`, the feed then renders all four seeded notifications with recipient-email badges, and the "Mark All Read" button hides — proving the actual shipped client code, not just the backend route, behaves correctly.
- `npm test`: **42/42 passed** (34 previous + 8 new), no regressions to any other module.
- All four test accounts and their seeded notifications were deleted afterward via a disposable helper script, which was itself deleted along with all Node-harness scratch files. A final residue check confirmed zero `zztest_notif_`-prefixed users and zero leftover `ZZ Test notif`-titled notifications.

# Request Workflow + Document Library Improvements — 2026-07-28

Twelfth request the same stretch: give Auth. Personnel and `potential_partner` an automatic personal copy of every request they submit, let Administrator preview/download every uploaded attachment directly from a request's details, declutter the Document Requests review buttons, and turn the Document Library's folder sidebar (previously 100% hardcoded/non-functional placeholder markup) into a real, working, per-owner folder system for all three roles that already use the library.

## Files Modified

- `services/documentLibraryService.js` — added `archiveRequestRecordToLibrary()`, exported alongside the existing `archiveToDocumentLibrary`/`updateDocument`.
- `cirl.js` — hooked the new archival helper into three submission routes; added `GET/POST/PATCH /api/document-folders*` and `PATCH /api/documents/:id/organize` (all new, ownership-checked, `requireUploader`-gated).
- `views/administrator/partnership_requests.ejs` — Document Request action-button simplification; Preview/Download buttons for both request types' attachments.
- `views/administrator/documents.ejs` (shared with Auth. Personnel) — replaced the fake hardcoded "Folders" sidebar and dead Archive/Edit buttons with real, working folder management.
- `views/potential_partner/partner_documents.ejs` — same real folder system added (this file had no folder UI at all before, fake or otherwise).
- `test/document-library-workflow.test.js` — **new**, 10 tests.

## Request Workflow Improvements

### Auto-copy to the submitter's own Document Library (Auth. Personnel & `potential_partner`)

Inspection found the OCR-upload path (`archiveToDocumentLibrary`, used when a Partnership Request's optional "Auto-fill from Document" upload is used) already archives that file to the Document Library, owned by the submitter — this part was already working and needed no change. The gap was **every submission with no attached file** — the normal case for Document Requests (which have no upload step at all) and for Partnership Requests submitted without OCR. For those, nothing ever reached the submitter's library.

- New `archiveRequestRecordToLibrary(db, meta)` inserts a lightweight metadata-only entry into the same `documents` collection the library already reads from — no physical file operation, since there may be no file. For Document Requests, `fileLink` points at the existing `/document-requests/:id/print` page (built in an earlier task) so the library entry is still something real and openable, not a fabricated placeholder. For Partnership Requests without an attachment, there is no equivalent print page, so `fileLink` is left empty and the library UI already handles a missing link gracefully (Preview/Download simply have nothing to point at).
- Hooked in at the three points a request actually becomes "submitted": `POST /api/requests` (fresh, non-draft submission), `POST /api/requests/:id/submit` (a saved Draft being submitted), and `POST /api/document-requests` (always submitted immediately — no draft state exists for this type). Each call is gated to `req.session.user.role` being `'Auth. Personnel'` or `'potential_partner'` — Administrator submitting a request (rare, but `requireRequester`/`requireAuth` both permit it) does **not** get an auto-copy, matching the task's literal scope.
- **Duplicate-avoidance:** for Partnership Requests, the archival call is skipped whenever `attachmentLink` is already set, since the OCR path already created a library entry for that exact submission — creating a second one would violate "reuse existing... avoid duplicate files." Document Requests never have this competing path, so their copy is unconditional.

### Administrator: preview and download every uploaded attachment

The request-details modals already listed uploaded files as bare links; there was no clear preview-vs-download distinction. Both the Partnership Request modal (submitter's OCR attachment + Administrator-uploaded supporting documents) and the Document Request modal (supporting documents) now render two explicit actions per file: **Preview** (`target="_blank"`, same same-origin static file the app already serves from `/uploads/documents/`) and **Download** (identical link with the HTML `download` attribute, forcing a save rather than an inline open) — both reuse the exact `fileLink` values already present, no new storage or route needed.

### Administrator fulfillment uploads → requester's Document Library

Confirmed already fully implemented (built in an earlier task) for both `POST /api/requests/:id/documents` and `POST /api/document-requests/:id/documents` — both call `archiveToDocumentLibrary` with `uploadedByEmail` set to the *requester's* email, not the admin's. Re-verified live with a real multipart upload rather than assumed from reading the code (see Verification below); no change was needed here.

## Admin Requests Page — Document Requests Button Cleanup

- Table row: removed the quick Fulfill/Reject icon buttons that sat beside "Review" — a single "Review"/"View" button now opens the same modal, where Fulfill/Reject already live. The now-fully-unused `quickDR()` function was removed along with its two callers.
- Modal footer (`dr-modal-footer`): removed the "Print" button entirely (the print *page* itself, `/document-requests/:id/print`, is untouched and still used elsewhere); removed "Request More Info" (the `Under Review` transition); shrank "Download PDF" from a labeled button to a small icon-only button (`btn-sm btn-icon`, tooltip via `title`). Reject and Fulfill are unchanged. The Partnership Request modal's own footer (a separate, untouched workflow with its own "Request More Info"/Reject/Approve) was intentionally left alone — the task scoped this cleanup to Document Requests only.

## Document Library — Real Folders (Administrator, Auth. Personnel, `potential_partner`)

Inspection found the Administrator/Auth. Personnel Document Library's "Folders" sidebar was **entirely fake** — hardcoded folder names ("Research", "Student Exchange", etc.) that just filtered by the existing `tags` array, a "New" button with no click handler, and per-document "Edit"/"Archive" buttons with no click handlers at all. The `potential_partner` library had no folder concept whatsoever, real or fake.

- **Backend** — new `documentfolders` collection: `{ id, name, ownerEmail, archived, createdAt }`. `GET/POST/PATCH /api/document-folders*` and `PATCH /api/documents/:id/organize` (move to folder and/or archive) are all gated by `requireUploader` (the same three roles — Administrator, Auth. Personnel, `potential_partner` — the library already supports) plus an explicit ownership check (`folder.ownerEmail`/`document.uploadedByEmail` must equal the session email), returning `403` otherwise. These are deliberately new, separate routes — the pre-existing `PATCH /api/documents/:id` (OCR metadata correction, Administrator/Auth. Personnel-only, no ownership check) was **not** touched, so its RBAC and behavior are exactly as before.
- **Frontend** — both `administrator/documents.ejs` and `partner_documents.ejs` got the identical, real folder system: a sidebar list (All Documents / Unfiled / each active folder / Archived) backed by `/api/document-folders/mine`; "New Folder" and per-folder Rename/Archive using `prompt()`-based input (consistent with this codebase's existing lightweight-interaction convention — `confirm()` dialogs are already used everywhere else for destructive actions, so this isn't a new pattern); a per-document "Move to folder" `<select>` in both grid and list views wired to the new organize route; and a working Archive/Unarchive toggle (previously a dead button) also wired to it. Administrator/Auth. Personnel additionally keep a working "Rename" (title) button, reusing the pre-existing `PATCH /api/documents/:id` route/RBAC unchanged — `potential_partner` doesn't get this button, matching that route's existing role restriction exactly (not a new limitation introduced by this task).
- Type Filter and Tag Filter pills (pre-existing, real, working features) were left completely untouched; folder filtering composes with them (a folder view still respects the active type/tag/search filters).

## Verification Performed (live, real accounts, no synthetic Mongo data)

A stale server process from a prior session was found holding port 3000 and killed before starting fresh. Disposable Administrator, Auth. Personnel, and two `potential_partner` accounts ("owner" and "other") were created directly in MongoDB.

- **Auto-copy, both roles, both request types:** submitted a real Partnership Request and a real Document Request as both the Auth. Personnel and the `potential_partner` accounts (4 submissions total) — confirmed via `GET /api/documents/mine` that all four produced a real library entry with the correct `requestType`/`requestId`, and that the Document Request entries' `fileLink` was exactly `/document-requests/<id>/print`.
- **Role scoping:** submitted a Partnership Request as Administrator — confirmed **no** library entry was created for it.
- **Duplicate-avoidance:** submitted a Partnership Request with a (fake) `attachmentLink` already set — confirmed zero additional library entries were created for that submission.
- **Cross-user isolation:** the "other" `potential_partner` account's `/api/documents/mine` returned zero of the owner's or personnel's documents.
- **Fulfillment upload regression, with a real file:** uploaded a real PDF via `POST /api/document-requests/:id/documents` as Administrator — confirmed via the requester's own `/api/documents/mine` that the resulting entry is owned by the *requester* (`uploadedByEmail`), not the admin, and that the same request's `supportingDocuments` (visible to Administrator in the request details) carries the real `fileLink`.
- **Folders, real CRUD:** created a real folder as the `potential_partner` account, confirmed it appears in that account's `/api/document-folders/mine` and **not** in the "other" account's; renamed it; archived it; confirmed the "other" account gets `403` attempting to rename or archive it.
- **Move to folder:** moved a real document into the new folder via `PATCH /api/documents/:id/organize`, confirmed `folderId` persisted; confirmed the "other" account gets `403` attempting to move or archive a document it doesn't own.
- **Browser-equivalent verification (no Playwright available):** rendered the real `partner_documents.ejs` template through the actual EJS engine, extracted its real `<script>` block, and ran it in a Node harness against the real API responses captured above — confirmed the folder sidebar renders the real folder name (not a placeholder), the document grid shows the moved document when that folder is selected, and correctly excludes it from the "Unfiled" view — proving the actual shipped client-side filtering logic, not just the backend routes, works correctly.
- **Admin Requests page, live HTML:** fetched `/partnership-requests` as Administrator and confirmed via grep: zero occurrences of `quickDR` or a literal `>Print<` button in the Document Request section; the "Download PDF" button carries `title="Download PDF"` (icon-only); the surviving `Request More Info` match is on the **Partnership Request** modal only (a different, intentionally-untouched workflow) — confirmed by locating the exact line and reading its surrounding code.
- **Static:** `node -c cirl.js` clean; `ejs.renderFile()` clean on `administrator/partnership_requests.ejs`, `administrator/documents.ejs` (both Administrator and Auth. Personnel), and `partner_documents.ejs`.
- **New Jest suite** (`test/document-library-workflow.test.js`, 10 tests): auto-copy for both roles and both request types, role-scoping exclusion (Administrator), duplicate-avoidance (attachment already present), folder CRUD with cross-user `403`s, document-organize (move + archive) with cross-user `403`s, and RBAC/session gating on the new routes.
- `npm test`: **52/52 passed** (42 previous + 10 new), no regressions to any other module.
- All test accounts, their requests/document requests, library entries, folders, and the one physical test-upload file were deleted afterward via a disposable helper script, which was itself deleted along with all Node-harness scratch files. A final residue check confirmed zero `zztest_dl_`-prefixed users and zero leftover `ZZ Test DL`-pattern records across `requests`, `documentrequests`, `documents`, and `documentfolders`.

# OCR → Intelligent Document Processing (IDP) Upgrade — 2026-07-28

Upgraded the existing OCR pipeline (upload → extract text → suggest form values → attach) into a richer Intelligent Document Processing workflow, per the task's ten-section spec: automatic multi-field form-filling, document-type classification, per-field confidence scoring, expiration/renewal-status extraction, full-text-searchable scanned documents, duplicate-document detection, an auto-generated summary, pre-OCR image-quality checks, and a redesigned OCR result panel — all layered onto the existing `/api/ocr/extract` + `/api/ocr/status/:jobId` job pipeline and the existing Document Library storage, with **no new routes, no RBAC changes, and no change to the existing text-extraction call sites**.

## Files Modified

- `services/extractionService.js` — extended (not replaced): 4 new document-type classification patterns (Research/Student Exchange/Faculty Exchange/Training Agreement), new country/region/category/nature/responsible-unit inference, richer date-label recognition + remaining-duration/expiration-label calculation, per-field confidence scores, an auto-generated summary sentence, and search-keyword extraction. `extractFields()`'s existing return keys are all still present, unchanged — this is a pure superset.
- `services/imageQualityService.js` — **new**. Sharp-based, best-effort pre-OCR image quality analysis (resolution, brightness, blur, edge-cropping).
- `services/documentLibraryService.js` — added `findPossibleDuplicates()`; extended `archiveToDocumentLibrary()` to persist the new IDP fields (`ocrText`, `searchKeywords`, `summary`, `country`/`category`/`region`/`nature`/`unit`, `fieldConfidence`, `expiration`, `imageQualityWarnings`, `possibleDuplicateIds`) onto the same `documents` collection record — additive fields only, every existing field untouched.
- `services/ocrService.js` — wired image-quality analysis and duplicate detection into the existing job pipeline (`processImage`/`processPdf`/`runJob`); both are wrapped so a failure in either never fails the OCR job itself.
- `assets/js/pages/idp-ocr-widget.js` — **new**, shared client-side renderer (`window.IDP`) used by all three OCR-enabled forms, so the enriched result panel (summary, confidence badges, doc-type override, expiration/renewal line, duplicate-warning alert, keyword chips, image-quality warnings, auto-filled-field highlighting) is implemented once, not tripled.
- `views/administrator/registry.ejs` + `assets/js/pages/registry-gridjs.init.js` (Add Partnership OCR panel), `views/auth. personnel/personnel_requests.ejs`, `views/potential_partner/partner_requests.ejs` — each wired in the shared widget script, an IDP result container, and extended their existing `applyOcrToForm()`/`applyPROcrToForm()` to also auto-fill Country/Region/Category/Nature/Unit (previously only Institution/Type/Dates were auto-filled) with the "only apply on explicit click, then highlight what was just filled" behavior already established by the existing Apply button — no auto-fill ever happens without the user's own click.
- `views/administrator/documents.ejs` (shared Admin/Auth. Personnel Document Library) + `views/potential_partner/partner_documents.ejs` — extended the existing client-side search filter to also match `ocrText`/`searchKeywords`, so scanned documents are findable by words that only ever appear inside the file.
- `test/idp-extraction.test.js` — **new**, 24 tests.
- `test/idp-duplicate-detection.test.js` — **new**, 5 tests.

## Section-by-Section Notes

1. **Automatic form filling** — `extractCountry`/`inferCategory`/`inferRegion`/`inferNature`/`inferUnit` added to `extractionService.js`, all following the file's existing "only populate when the text actually contains a real signal" philosophy (no guessing). Country detection picks the single non-Philippines country mentioned, or the one nearest the partner's name when several appear, or `Philippines`/domestic when only that's mentioned. Category/Region are then derived from the detected country. Nature is scored the same way `classifyDocumentType` already scores document type. Responsible Unit is deliberately conservative — only a literal acronym mention (`CCS`, `CIRL`, etc.), no guessed department-name synonyms, hence its low confidence ceiling. Values are only written into a form field on an explicit "Apply suggested values" click (pre-existing behavior, unchanged) — never overwriting a field silently — and every field actually written gets the new `.ocr-autofilled` highlight class (auto-clears on the user's next edit).
2. **Document type classification** — 4 new patterns added (Research/Student Exchange/Faculty Exchange/Training Agreement) alongside the 5 already there. The enriched result panel adds a `<select>` pre-set to the detected type (or the classifier's existing `null`→"Other" fallback) that the user can freely override before applying — satisfies "if confidence is low, allow the user to change it manually" without touching the classifier's own conservative "don't guess" return value.
3. **Confidence scoring** — every inferred field now has a paired `<field>Confidence` (0-100). The widget's `confidenceBadge()` colors it green (95-100), blue (80-94), yellow (60-79), or red (<60), exactly the thresholds specified.
4. **Expiration/renewal extraction** — `extractDates()` now recognizes more label variants (Effective Date, Signing Date, Date Signed, Valid Until, Expiration/Expiry Date) and computes `remainingDays`/`expirationLabel` using the same 90-day "Expiring Soon" window as the existing hourly lifecycle check (`LIFECYCLE_EXPIRING_WINDOW_DAYS` in `cirl.js`, mirrored as a local constant with a comment cross-referencing it, not imported — `extractionService.js` has no existing dependency on `cirl.js` and this task didn't warrant creating one for a single constant). Renewal reminders and expiry-monitoring dashboard stats themselves are **not new code** — once a partnership is saved to the Registry with the extracted start/end dates, the pre-existing `recomputePartnershipStatuses()` hourly job already picks it up; the OCR panel's "Renewal Monitoring" line says this explicitly so the user understands what happens after saving.
5. **Searchable scanned documents** — the full OCR text and a list of extracted search keywords (institution/partner/country/type/nature/unit plus the top recurring proper-noun-like phrases in the document, e.g. "Kyushu", "CHED") are now persisted on the archived document record and included in both Document Library search filters.
6. **Duplicate detection** — `findPossibleDuplicates()` scores candidate matches on institution/partner name (primary), document type, and overlapping validity dates, with matching filename as a secondary-only signal (matches the spec's own ranking — filename alone never crosses the match threshold). Never blocks the upload: the file is archived exactly as before, and the OCR result additionally carries a `duplicateWarning`. The result panel's "Cancel" button reuses the **existing** `PATCH /api/documents/:id/organize` route (already ownership-checked) to archive the new entry rather than deleting it outright — recoverable, no new route.
7. **OCR summary** — `buildSummary()` produces the one-paragraph template sentence described in the task (institution/type/partner/dates/expiration status), shown above the extracted fields in the result panel.
8. **Image quality analysis** — `imageQualityService.js` checks resolution, brightness, and blur (via a Laplacian-style edge-variance kernel) using `sharp`, all cheap and reliable from pixel stats. **Scope decision, disclosed rather than hidden:** true geometric rotation/skew detection was deliberately *not* implemented — it would require either a second Tesseract OSD pass (extra latency + a new network-dependent failure mode fetching orientation data) or real computer-vision code, neither justified for a "warn but always allow continuing" check. The pipeline still auto-corrects EXIF-based rotation exactly as it already did. The "possible crop" check is a heuristic (edge-strip variance), not a certainty, and is labeled `severity: 'info'` rather than `'warning'` for that reason.
9. **Document Library integration** — everything above is persisted onto the same `documents` collection record `archiveToDocumentLibrary()` already creates; no duplicate physical file, no new collection.
10. **UI** — the shared `idp-ocr-widget.js` renders all of the above (Summary, Document Type + override, Confidence Scores, Expiration/Renewal Monitoring line, Duplicate Warning with Continue/Cancel/View Existing, Search Keywords chips, Image Quality warnings) into one panel per form, identically across all three OCR entry points.

## RBAC

No new routes were added, so no new RBAC surface exists. Every enriched field rides along inside the existing, already-scoped `/api/ocr/status/:jobId` (ownership-checked against `job.uploadedByEmail`), `/api/documents`, and `/api/documents/mine` responses. The one new interaction (duplicate "Cancel") reuses the existing `PATCH /api/documents/:id/organize` route's existing ownership check unchanged.

## Testing Performed

- **Static:** `node -c` clean on all four modified/new backend JS files; `ejs.renderFile()` clean on all 31 view files (three pre-existing false positives from missing unrelated mock variables, confirmed unrelated to this change); `node -c` clean on `idp-ocr-widget.js` and `registry-gridjs.init.js`.
- **New Jest suite** (`test/idp-extraction.test.js`, 24 tests, pure unit — no DB/server): international MOA classification/country/region/category/nature/dates/summary/keywords; domestic MOU → Philippines/Local/Student Exchange/Expiring-Soon; already-expired Letter of Intent; all 4 new document-type classifications; conservative null/0-confidence fallback when nothing matches; conservative unit-code matching. Real scanned sample documents (MOA/MOU/LOI/Partnership Proposal/Research Agreement, as the task asked to test) were not available in this environment, so these use synthetic OCR-like text blocks standing in for actual scans — an intentional, disclosed limitation, not a shortcut taken silently.
- **New Jest suite** (`test/idp-duplicate-detection.test.js`, 5 tests): institution+type match flagged with matches returned; unrelated institution not flagged; filename-only match (secondary signal) correctly *not* flagged; empty/null-extraction edge cases.
- **Live end-to-end verification, real running server, real Tesseract OCR** (stale server from a prior session found on port 3000 and killed first): generated a synthetic but genuinely legible MOA image (sharp+SVG, real black-on-white text, not a fixture file) and ran it through the actual `/api/ocr/extract` → poll `/api/ocr/status/:jobId` flow as a disposable Auth. Personnel account. Confirmed in the real API response: 95% OCR confidence, `documentType` "Memorandum of Agreement (MOA)" at 99% confidence, correct institution/partner, `country: "Japan"` (90%), `category: "International"`, `region: "Asia"`, `nature: "Research"`, correct start/end dates, `expirationLabel: "Active"` with a sane `remainingDays`, a coherent `summary` sentence, populated `searchKeywords`, an `imageQuality` object with metrics and a (correct, for this tightly-cropped synthetic image) "possible-crop" warning, and `duplicateWarning: { found: false }`. Confirmed via direct MongoDB read that the archived `documents` record persisted every one of the new fields. Confirmed via `GET /api/documents/mine` that the owner sees `ocrText`/`searchKeywords`/`fieldConfidence` in the API response (not just in the DB). Uploaded the **same** document a second time and confirmed `duplicateWarning.found: true` with the correct match/reason, and that the upload was still archived (not blocked) as the spec requires. Exercised the "Cancel" flow for real (`PATCH /api/documents/:id/organize` with `{archived:true}`) and confirmed it succeeded for the owner. Confirmed a different `potential_partner` account got `403` attempting the same organize call on someone else's document, and that `/api/documents/mine` correctly excluded it — cross-role RBAC unaffected by this change (also incidentally reconfirmed the pre-existing, unrelated `OWN_SCOPE_ROLES` behavior that even Administrator's `/api/documents` is own-scoped, not global — expected, not a regression).
- `npm test`: **81/81 passed** (52 previous + 29 new), no regressions to any other module.
- All disposable accounts, archived test documents (DB records and their physical uploaded files), and Node-harness scratch scripts were deleted afterward. A final residue check confirmed zero `zztest_idp_`-prefixed users and zero leftover `ZZ Test IDP`-titled documents.

# IDP UX Upgrade: Automatic Form-Filling, Keyword-Panel Removal — 2026-07-29

The prior IDP task (above) required an explicit "Apply suggested values" click before OCR-extracted values reached the Partnership Request form. This task removes that click: **both Partnership Request forms** (Auth. Personnel's and `potential_partner`'s) now auto-fill immediately when OCR succeeds. Scope is deliberately limited to the two Partnership Request pages — the task's own goal/verification sections name only "the Partnership Request form" throughout, so the Registry "Add Partnership" OCR panel (a different, Administrator-only workflow that directly creates a live partnership record rather than a request) was intentionally left on its existing manual-click flow, unchanged.

## Files Modified

- `assets/js/pages/idp-ocr-widget.js` (shared renderer, unchanged public contract otherwise): removed the Search Keywords panel from `renderResult()`; `markFilled(id, confidence)` gained a second, optional `confidence` parameter — auto-fades the "just filled" highlight after 4s even without user interaction ("briefly", per the spec), and adds a persistent low-confidence border + inline warning (`<70%`) that only clears once the user actually edits the field; new `mapDocumentType(detectedType, validOptions)` helper, shared by both forms instead of tripling the mapping logic.
- `views/auth. personnel/personnel_requests.ejs` — `prOcrSucceed()` now calls `applyPROcrToForm()` immediately (no click required); `applyPROcrToForm()` passes each field's confidence into `markFilled()`, uses `IDP.mapDocumentType()` instead of the old regex chain, scrolls to the first auto-filled field, and no longer needs a manual trigger. The "Apply" button remains as an optional "Re-apply suggested values" (idempotent, useful if a user wants to revert their own edits back to the OCR suggestions). Added a small `pr-f-type-warn` element under Agreement Type. Fixed a real date-conversion bug (see below).
- `views/potential_partner/partner_requests.ejs` — identical set of changes (`ocrSucceed()`, `applyOcrToForm()`, `f-type-warn`, same date-conversion fix).

## Behavior Changes

- **Auto-fill, no click required:** `prOcrSucceed(result)` / `ocrSucceed(result)` (the functions the OCR poll loop already calls when a job finishes) now call the page's own apply function directly. Verified via a Node harness that calls **only** `prOcrSucceed`/`ocrSucceed` — never `applyPROcrToForm`/`applyOcrToForm` — and asserts every field ends up populated anyway.
- **Highlight every auto-filled field, briefly:** unchanged `.ocr-autofilled` pulse class, now additionally auto-removed after 4 seconds via `setTimeout` (previously it only cleared when the user edited that specific field, which could leave months-old highlighting styling if a field was never touched — not actually reachable in practice since the page reloads per session, but "briefly" as specified is now literally true).
- **Low-confidence marking (<70%):** any field `markFilled()` is called with a confidence below 70 gets a persistent red-tinted border (`.ocr-low-confidence`) plus a small inline warning naming the exact percentage, distinct from the "just filled" pulse — both clear together the moment the user edits that field. Fields extraction couldn't find at all are simply never filled (already-existing `set()` behavior — a value of `null`/`undefined`/`''` is a no-op, never a placeholder like "Unknown").
- **Agreement Type mapping, made honest:** the old per-page logic was `/agreement/i.test(r.documentType)` → force MOA. Since the earlier IDP task added `documentType` values like "Research Agreement" and "Student Exchange Agreement" (both contain the substring "agreement"), that regex would have **silently mis-mapped** them to MOA — a real bug, never previously exercised because no test covered a document type outside the original 3. The new shared `IDP.mapDocumentType()` only maps on a specific, unambiguous alias (`memorandum of agreement`/`\bmoa\b` → MOA, etc.) and returns `null` for anything else, so the caller leaves the dropdown blank and shows `"Detected \"<type>\" — please select the closest Agreement Type manually."` instead of guessing — exactly the spec's own example (`Agreement Type = "Agreement"` → map if possible, else blank + warn).
- **Scroll to first auto-filled field:** each apply function now calls `.scrollIntoView({behavior:'smooth', block:'center'})` on the first field it actually set (Institution, in practice, since it's both first in call order and first in the visible form).
- **Search Keywords removed from display, kept in the backend:** deleted the keyword-chip-rendering block from the shared widget entirely (it's rendered by one function used by all three OCR panels, so removing it there removes it everywhere — Registry's panel never had a divergent "keep keywords here" requirement either). `services/extractionService.js`'s `buildSearchKeywords()` and `documentLibraryService.js`'s persistence of `searchKeywords` on the archived document (used by Document Library search) are completely untouched.
- **All five "keep" panels verified still present:** Document Summary, Detected Document Type (+ override select), Confidence Scores, Expiration/Renewal Monitoring, Duplicate Detection, Image Quality Warnings — none of `renderResult()`'s other sections were touched.

## Bug Found and Fixed During Verification: date-conversion timezone bug

Building the auto-fill Node harness (below) surfaced a real, pre-existing bug: `prOcrIsoDate()`/`ocrIsoDate()` converted an extracted date string to the `<input type="date">` field's required `YYYY-MM-DD` format via `new Date(str).toISOString().slice(0,10)`. `new Date('March 3, 2026')` parses as **local midnight**; `.toISOString()` converts to UTC — in a UTC+ timezone, that rolls the date back to March 2. This wasn't caught by the prior task's verification because that task only checked the raw backend string values (`"March 3, 2026"`), never the client-side ISO conversion actually written into the date input. Fixed in both files by reading the parsed `Date`'s local `getFullYear()/getMonth()/getDate()` instead of going through UTC at all. **Not fixed** in `assets/js/pages/registry-gridjs.init.js` or `views/view_only/viewonly_access_request.ejs`, which contain the identical bug in the identical pattern — out of this task's stated scope (Partnership Request forms only), flagged here rather than silently left for someone to rediscover.

## RBAC

No routes changed, no new routes added — this task is entirely client-side rendering/UX logic layered on the same `/api/ocr/extract` + `/api/ocr/status/:jobId` responses the existing, unchanged RBAC already gates.

## Testing Performed

- **Static:** `node -c` clean on `idp-ocr-widget.js`; `ejs.renderFile()` clean on all 31 view files (same 3 pre-existing, unrelated false positives as every prior check in this doc).
- `npm test`: **81/81 passed**, unchanged from before this task (purely frontend change, no backend test surface touched).
- **New Node harness** (extracts the real inline `<script>` block from both `personnel_requests.ejs` and `partner_requests.ejs` via regex, evaluates it with a minimal hand-built DOM mock — `getElementById`/`classList`/`insertAdjacentElement`/`scrollIntoView`/event listeners — and calls the real, unmodified `prOcrSucceed`/`ocrSucceed` functions), run against **real OCR results from the real running server**:
  - Uploaded a synthetic-but-legible MOA **image** (sharp+SVG, real Tesseract OCR) as a disposable Auth. Personnel account, and a synthetic MOU **PDF** (pdfkit, real embedded text layer — exercises the text-layer fast path, not Tesseract) as a disposable `potential_partner` account — covering both document-input types the task's verification list asks for.
  - For both: called **only** `prOcrSucceed(result)` / `ocrSucceed(result)` (the OCR-poll success callback) — never the apply functions directly — and confirmed Institution, Country, Category, Region, Nature, Agreement Type (correctly mapped), Start Date, End Date (both correct after the timezone fix), and Purpose (folded into Additional Details) were all already populated afterward, with zero manual steps.
  - Confirmed the Institution field carried the `ocr-autofilled` highlight class immediately, and that `scrollIntoView` was called on it.
  - Confirmed the rendered result-panel HTML contains **no** occurrence of "Search Keywords" for either result, despite both real API responses having a non-empty `searchKeywords` array — while `Document Type`, `Confidence Scores`, `Expiration / Renewal Monitoring`, and the summary sentence text all render correctly.
  - Manually overwrote the auto-filled Institution field and fired an `input` event — confirmed the new value stuck and the highlight cleared, proving fields stay genuinely editable.
  - Fed a variant result with `documentType: "Research Agreement"` (deliberately not one of MOA/MOU/LOI/JVA) through `prOcrSucceed` and confirmed the Agreement Type dropdown was left blank (not force-mapped to MOA, the pre-fix behavior) and the new warning text named the detected type.
  - Widget-level checks in the same harness: `IDP.mapDocumentType('Research Agreement', [...])` and `('Student Exchange Agreement', [...])` both return `null` (would have returned `'MOA'` under the old regex); `IDP.markFilled(id, 45)` produces both the highlight and the low-confidence warning with the correct percentage text; `IDP.markFilled(id, 92)` produces the highlight only, no warning.
  - 35/35 harness assertions passed.
