# Administrator Reports & Analytics — Follow-Up Fix: Compare Against Didn't Inherit the Report Type / Status Fix — 2026-08-26

Immediately after the audit above, `computeComparisonReport()` was still not covered by the `REPORT_TYPE_IMPLIED_STATUS` fix — it was only added to `computeCustomReportData()`. This was caught not by another API-level curl check (which had already passed) but by actually **driving the real, live-rendered `<script>` block** end-to-end against the real running server: extracted the exact JS served to the browser at `/reports`, ran it in a minimal Node DOM/fetch harness, and called the real `previewCustomReport()` → `generateCustomReport()` → `openCompareSelect()` → `generateComparison()` → `exportComparison()` functions in the same sequence a real click-through would — the same technique used for full click-through verification earlier in this project.

## Bug Found

With Report Type = "Active Partnerships" and Status left at "All": Preview correctly showed **17** records (all Active, per the fix above). Opening **Compare** and comparing against Country = Japan showed Group A ("Original Report") = **28** — every partnership regardless of status, not the 17 the admin was just looking at. The "original, preserved report" requirement (Round 2) was silently broken the moment Compare was opened, because `computeComparisonReport()` never received or understood `reportType` at all — it only ever looked at the separate, unset `status` param.

## Fix

- `cirl.js`: hoisted `REPORT_TYPE_IMPLIED_STATUS` out of `computeCustomReportData()` to module scope so both report engines share one definition (avoids the two ever drifting out of sync again). `computeComparisonReport()` now reads `query.reportType` and computes `effectiveStatusQ = statusQ || REPORT_TYPE_IMPLIED_STATUS[reportType] || ''`, used for: the base pre-filter that produces Group A (the preserved original report), the three `fetchOverrideGroup()` calls that build Group B for every dimension except `status` itself (which intentionally overrides status on purpose), and the exported `filters['Status Filter']` display value.
- `views/administrator/reports.ejs`: `buildComparisonQueryString()` now forwards `reportType` (from `cr-type`) alongside the other builder filters when opening Compare.

## Tests Performed

- Two new tests added to `test/reports.test.js`'s `Compare Against` suite: Group A's total in a Compare query now equals Preview's `totalRecords` for the same status-flavored Report Type; an explicit Status filter still overrides the Report Type's implied status inside Compare, matching Preview's own precedence rule. **35/35 tests in this file, 144/144 full suite (serial)**, zero regressions.
- **Live, full click-through re-verification** using the same driven-script harness: re-fetched the live page after the fix, re-ran the exact same sequence — Group A now correctly reads **17** (matching Preview) and Group B (Japan) correctly reads **3** (previously 4 — it too had been including a non-Active Japan record, since Group B's base status was inherited from the same broken `statusQ`). Confirmed at the file level too: the exported Comparison Excel's "Group A Records" sheet contains exactly 17 rows, all status `Active`, zero leakage.
- All disposable test data cleaned up; residue check confirmed zero `zztest`-prefixed documents remained.

## Final Status

Fixed and verified, including via an actual driven run of the real client-side script (not just its backend API in isolation) — the class of bug this fix closes (a UI workflow producing internally-inconsistent numbers across two views of "the same report") is exactly the kind that curl-only API testing can miss when the disconnect is in how two pages of a multi-step workflow pass parameters to each other, rather than in either endpoint alone.

# Administrator Reports & Analytics — Custom Report Builder Output-Correctness Audit — 2026-08-26

Full data-flow audit requested: Custom Report Builder filters → effective values → backend query → returned records → Preview/PDF/Excel/Compare/Compare Against output, verifying actual record IDs against the real `partnerships` collection (not just trusting a 200 response). Every filter (Report Title, Report Type, Date Range, Status, Institution, Country, Region, Agreement Type, Nature, Category, Unit) was tested individually and in combination, cross-checked against independent direct-MongoDB queries.

## Root Causes Found

Two real, silent output-correctness bugs, both in code shared by every consumer (Preview, PDF, Excel, Compare, Compare Against) — so each was a single fix closing all downstream paths at once, not four separate PDF/Excel/Preview/Compare bugs:

1. **Country filter was case-sensitive.** Country is the Custom Report Builder's one free-text filter besides Institution (Region/Unit/Category/Agreement Type are all fixed dropdowns already matching the DB's exact casing, so they were never exposed to this). `filter.country = country` did a literal exact-string match; typing `japan` instead of `Japan` — or any casing that didn't byte-for-byte match how the record was originally entered — silently returned **zero results** instead of the real matching partnerships. Confirmed against real data: the collection already contains admin-entered values with inconsistent casing (`'sadi kaabay'`, `'sadtoa'`, `'san nicolas'` alongside properly-cased countries), so this was a live, reachable data-quality interaction, not a theoretical edge case. Institution's own free-text filter was already correctly case-insensitive (`$regex ... $options:'i'`) — Country was the one filter that diverged from that pattern.
2. **Date Range's `dateFrom` boundary was parsed in a different timezone than everything it was compared against.** `filterByDateRange` built `from = new Date(dateFrom)` from a bare `"YYYY-MM-DD"` string — per the ECMAScript spec, a bare ISO date-only string is parsed as **UTC** midnight. But `to` had `'T23:59:59'` appended (making it a date-*time* form, parsed as **local** time), and every partnership's own date field (e.g. `"Apr 12, 2026"`) is a non-ISO format, also always parsed as **local** time. The server runs in `Asia/Manila` (UTC+8, confirmed via `Intl.DateTimeFormat().resolvedOptions().timeZone`), so `dateFrom`'s UTC interpretation was 8 hours ahead of the local-time interpretation everything else used — silently **excluding any record dated exactly on the `dateFrom` boundary day**. Confirmed live: a record with `end: "Jan 1, 2026"` was excluded by `dateFrom=2026-01-01`, which should be an inclusive boundary.

## Filters Audited — Individually and in Combination

Every filter was verified against real `partnerships` data by comparing actual returned record **IDs** (not counts, not labels) against an independent direct-MongoDB re-implementation of the same query, run in a separate one-off script with no shared code path to the app:

| Filter | Verified correct? | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Report Title | ✅ | Passthrough only; no filtering logic to audit |
| Report Type | ✅ (already fixed in the previous session) | `REPORT_TYPE_IMPLIED_STATUS` correctly implies a status for Active/Inactive/Expired Partnerships/Expiring Soon; an explicit Status filter always wins |
| Status | ✅ | Recomputed live via the single authoritative `computeStatusFromEnd()` — never a second implementation |
| Institution | ✅ | Case-insensitive partial match, already correct |
| Country | ❌ → ✅ fixed | Was case-sensitive exact match (bug #1 above) |
| Region | ✅ | Fixed-dropdown exact match |
| Agreement Type | ✅ | Fixed-dropdown exact match, MOA/MOU whitelist |
| Nature | ✅ | Fixed-dropdown exact match |
| Category | ✅ | Fixed-dropdown exact match |
| Unit | ✅ | Fixed-dropdown exact match |
| Date Range | ❌ → ✅ fixed | `dateFrom` boundary timezone mismatch (bug #2 above) |

Combinations independently cross-verified against real data (exact record IDs matched a from-scratch re-query in every case): Status+Institution (15), Status+Region (8), Institution+Country (4), Institution+Agreement Type (11), Country+Region consistent (4) and contradictory (0, correctly empty rather than silently ignoring one filter), Report Type+Date Range, Report Type+Institution, Report Type+Country, and a 6-filter "kitchen sink" (Report Type+Country+Region+Category+Unit+Agreement Type → 3 records, IDs matched exactly). No filter was found to silently override, cancel, or leak past another — all composition is a straightforward AND across one MongoDB filter object plus sequential in-memory filters, with no shared/colliding variable names between filters.

## Preview / PDF / Excel Parity

All three routes call the exact same `computeCustomReportData()` with the exact same `req.query` — architecturally guaranteed to return identical records for identical filters, and verified directly: for `reportType=Active Partnerships&country=Philippines`, Preview returned 6 institutions, the Excel export's first sheet contained the exact same 6 institution names, and the PDF's extracted text contained all 6 names — not just matching counts.

## Compare / Compare Against

Verified with the exact scenario from the task ("Active Partnerships + Country = Philippines, Compare Against Country = Japan"): Group A returned exactly the same 6 record IDs as the original Preview, and Group B (Japan) correctly inherited the Active-status filter rather than dropping it. Extended to the 6-filter kitchen-sink case: Group A matched Preview's 3 IDs exactly, and overriding only the Unit dimension in Group B correctly preserved all 5 other original filters (proven with a purpose-built second test record so the assertion couldn't pass vacuously on an empty group). Every `Compare By` dimension option (country, institution, unit, region, agreement type, nature, category, year, status) was individually verified against independent DB counts.

## Fix

- `cirl.js`:
  - `filterByDateRange()`: `from` now parses `dateFrom + 'T00:00:00'` instead of the bare date string, making both boundaries — and their comparison against record dates — consistently local-time. One shared function, fixes Preview/PDF/Excel/Compare/Compare Against simultaneously.
  - New shared helper `buildExactCaseInsensitiveMatch(value)` (`{ $regex: '^...$', $options: 'i' }`) — applied to Country's filter construction in both `computeCustomReportData()` and `computeComparisonReport()`, and to the Compare Against dimension-override branch for `country`, so this can never diverge between the three call sites the way the Report Type / Status logic briefly did in the previous session.
- No changes to any UI button, route, response shape, PDF/Excel template, or the Round 2 comparison workflow — both fixes are purely internal to the two shared data-computation functions.

## Tests Performed

- **New tests added to `test/reports.test.js`**: Country matches regardless of casing (proper/lower/upper all return identical record IDs); Country still requires an exact match, not a partial substring; `dateFrom`/`dateTo` each individually include a record dated exactly on the boundary; a record the day before the `dateFrom` boundary is correctly excluded; a same-day range is inclusive; a 6-filter AND combination returns the correct records and a contradictory combination correctly zeroes out; Preview/PDF/Excel return the identical record set (by name, not just count); Compare Against preserves every one of 6 original filters in Group A and correctly overrides only the intended dimension in Group B (verified against a purpose-built second record, not a vacuous empty-array pass).
- **`test/reports.test.js`: 44/44 passed.**
- **Full suite (serial): 153/153 passed**, zero regressions.
- **Live verification, real running server, real 29-record dataset**, cross-checked against independent one-off MongoDB queries for every filter and combination listed above — not just re-reading the app's own API response.
- **Driven-script verification**: re-extracted the live `<script>` served at `/reports` after the fix and ran the real `previewCustomReport()` function with a lowercase `"philippines"` typed into the Country field and a boundary date typed into Date From/To — both produced the correct record counts end-to-end through the actual client code, not just the backend endpoint in isolation.
- All disposable test users/partnerships deleted afterward; residue check confirmed zero `zztest`- and `Jest`-prefixed documents remained in either collection.

## Final Status

Fixed and verified against real data at the record-ID level, not just HTTP status or record counts. The Custom Report Builder's output now accurately reflects every selected filter, individually and in combination, consistently across Preview, PDF, Excel, Compare, and Compare Against. No other output-correctness inconsistency was found across the audited filter set. No existing button, UI behavior, route, or the Round 2 template/comparison implementation was redesigned or altered — both fixes are internal corrections to the shared filter-construction logic.

# Administrator Reports & Analytics — "Reports" Tab Full Functionalization Audit — 2026-08-26

Scope: specifically the **Reports** tab (`content-rep` / `tab-rep`) of Administrator → Reports & Analytics — the quick-export report cards, their live stat counts, and the Custom Report Builder/Preview/Compare workflow already hardened in the two prior sessions. Audit Trail and Strategic Insights (DSS) tabs were explicitly out of scope and untouched.

## Root Cause Found: report cards' live counts could disagree with what clicking the card's own export button produced

`/api/partnerships/stats` — which feeds the "17 records" / "10 records" / "1 records" live counts shown directly on the Active/Expired/Expiring Soon quick-export cards — computed those counts from each partnership's **stored** `status` field. That field is only refreshed by the hourly lifecycle job (`recomputePartnershipStatuses`, `LIFECYCLE_CHECK_INTERVAL_MS`, hourly by default) or at server startup. Every actual report export, by contrast, always recomputes status live from the record's own end date via `computeStatusFromEnd()` (the single authoritative implementation used throughout the Reports engine). This meant the number displayed on a card and the number actually produced by clicking that same card's PDF/Excel button could disagree for up to an hour after a status transition — a "second status implementation" in every sense that mattered even though it called the same field name, and exactly the class of divergence the previous session's `REPORT_TYPE_IMPLIED_STATUS` work was written to prevent.

Reproduced deliberately: forced a real record's stored `status` to a garbage value (`'DeliberatelyWrongStaleStatus'`) while its true end-date-derived status was Active. Before the fix, `/api/partnerships/stats`'s `active` count would have silently excluded it (filtering on the corrupted stored value); after the fix, `/api/partnerships/stats` and the Reports engine's own "Active Partnerships" total agreed exactly, both correctly counting the record as Active regardless of what was sitting in its stored field.

## Fix

- `cirl.js`, `GET /api/partnerships/stats`: now recomputes each record's status in-memory via `computeStatusFromEnd()` before counting — the identical pattern already used in `computeCustomReportData()` — rather than trusting the stored field. Pure read-side fix (does not write the recomputed status back to the database, so it cannot race with or duplicate the lifecycle job's own persistence).
- This endpoint is also consumed by the Administrator Dashboard, Personnel Dashboard, and Partner Dashboard (confirmed via a repo-wide search) — none of those pages' code was touched; they simply now receive a more accurate, non-stale count from the one shared endpoint that was the actual root cause of the Reports tab's specific card/export disagreement, per the task's own "shared backend function" exception.

## Full Reports-Tab Verification (real running server, real data, driven through the actual live client script)

- **Every quick-export card** (Partnership Summary, Expiring Agreements, Active Partnerships List, Expired Partnerships List, Expiring Soon List, Mid-Year Output, Yearly Output, Compliance/Audit Report) driven via the real `exportReport()` function extracted from the live-rendered page — all 9 PDF/Excel URLs returned HTTP 200 with valid file magic bytes (`%PDF` / `PK` ZIP signature), no crashes.
- **Live stat counts verified against the fix**: `loadDssInsights()` driven for real — `stat-active-count`/`stat-expiring-count`/`stat-expired-count` populated `17 records` / `1 records` / `10 records`, exactly matching the Reports engine's own Active/Expiring/Expired totals (17+1+10=28, the full real dataset).
- **Card-to-export parity, byte-level**: the Active Partnerships List card's displayed count and its actual Excel export's row count were independently verified to be exactly equal (17 = 17).
- **Full builder round-trip re-confirmed after the fix**: Preview (Active Partnerships + Country=Philippines → 6) → Compare → Compare Against Country=Japan → Group A stayed 6 (matching Preview exactly), Group B correctly read 3 (Active+Japan) → both comparison export URLs opened with every original filter (`reportType`, `country`) still present alongside the override.

## Cards Reviewed but Intentionally Not Changed

- **Mid-Year Output Report** and **Yearly Output Report**: their card descriptions ("first half of the academic year," "full academic/calendar year") imply date-scoped filtering, but their export calls pass no date range at all — they currently produce the same unfiltered report as "Partnership Summary Report." This is not a *correctness* bug (no crash, no wrong data for what is actually requested) but the cards are not doing what their own description promises. Implementing real date-scoping here would require choosing an academic-year cutoff (e.g., Philippine SY start month) that was not specified anywhere in the existing code or given by the user — inventing one risked being confidently wrong on a report explicitly described as suitable for **CHED submission**. Left unchanged and flagged here rather than guessed at.
- **Compliance / Audit Report**: produces the same complete, correctly-templated report as Summary, just under a compliance-oriented title — reasonable as-is (the "compliance" framing is about audience, not a distinct data shape), not changed.

## Tests Performed

- **New tests added to `test/reports.test.js`**: `/api/partnerships/stats` ignores a deliberately-corrupted stored status and matches the Reports engine's own Active/Expired totals exactly; the Active Partnerships List card's displayed count and its actual export's row count are proven equal.
- **`test/reports.test.js`: 46/46 passed. Full suite (serial): 155/155 passed**, zero regressions.
- All disposable test data (users, partnerships, and the deliberately-corrupted status field) cleaned up and restored; residue check confirmed zero `zztest`/`Jest`-prefixed documents remained, and the corrupted record's stored status was confirmed restored to its correct real value (`Expired`).

## Final Status

The Reports tab's report cards, their live counts, and the Custom Report Builder/Preview/PDF/Excel/Compare/Compare Against workflow are now fully consistent and verified against real data end-to-end, including through the actual live client script (not just curl). One documented, intentionally-unimplemented gap remains (Mid-Year/Yearly date-scoping) pending the user specifying the intended academic-year cutoff — everything else audited in this pass is confirmed correct and unchanged where it was already correct. No button, route, or existing UI behavior was redesigned; the Dashboard/Personnel/Partner pages that also call the one shared endpoint fixed here were not modified and are unaffected apart from now receiving more accurate data.

# Administrator Reports & Analytics — "Something went wrong" Error Banner Bug — 2026-08-26

Reported live by the actual Administrator (Raven Ferrer): generating an "Inactive Partnerships" report (Status filter also explicitly set to Inactive, date range 2023-01-26 to 2026-08-26) showed a "Something went wrong." error banner **directly above** a fully, correctly rendered report (title, meta, filter badges, summary cards, and all 10 real records) — the error and the successful content were visible at the same time.

## Root Cause

A stale-response race condition in the Preview modal's client-side JS, made worse by a real completeness gap in its own error-handling function:

- `showRpmError()` hid the loading spinner and showed the error banner, but — unlike `showRpmLoading()`, which correctly hides every content view — it never hid `rpm-view-preview` / `rpm-view-compare-select` / `rpm-view-compare-result` or their footers. So *if* an error handler ever fired after a content view was already visible, both stayed on screen simultaneously.
- `previewCustomReport()` (and `generateComparison()`, same pattern) had no way to tell an old, still-in-flight request apart from a newer one. If the admin re-triggered a preview before an earlier request had finished — a slow connection, a double-click, or (as very plausibly happened live here, given this session's own server was being restarted repeatedly for verification work at the time) a request that failed because the server was mid-restart while a follow-up request succeeded against the freshly-started server — the OLDER request's `.catch()` could fire *after* the NEWER request's `.then()` had already rendered the report and hidden the error/loading state, re-showing the error banner on top of an already-correct, fully-rendered report.

The report data itself was never wrong — this was purely a client-side display-ordering bug between two independent async operations racing each other.

## Fix

`views/administrator/reports.ejs`:
- `showRpmError()` now also hides every content view and footer, mirroring `showRpmLoading()` exactly — an error can never again be visible at the same time as a rendered report.
- Added a simple request-sequence guard to both `previewCustomReport()` (`_previewRequestSeq`) and `generateComparison()` (`_comparisonRequestSeq`): each call captures the current sequence number, increments the shared counter, and its `.then()`/`.catch()` handlers check the counter is still current before touching the UI — a response from a superseded, older request is silently ignored instead of being allowed to overwrite what a newer request already rendered.

## Tests Performed

- **Reproduction**: built a Node harness that evaluates the real, live-served `<script>` and fires two overlapping `previewCustomReport()` calls where the first (older) request is deliberately slower and fails, while the second (newer) request is faster and succeeds — exactly the "slow failing request that resolves after a fast successful one" shape. Run against the pre-fix code, this reliably reproduced the exact bug (error banner AND preview content both visible). Run against the fixed code, only the successful preview is shown and the stale error is silently ignored — confirming both that the fix works and that the reproduction is real (not a false positive).
- **Live re-verification of the exact reported query** (Report Type = Inactive Partnerships, Status = Inactive, Date range 2023-01-26 to 2026-08-26) against the real 28-record dataset via the live-served script: renders cleanly with the correct 10 records and no error banner.
- **Full suite**: `npm test` (serial) — **155/155 passed**, zero regressions (this was a pure frontend fix; no backend route or computation changed).
- Disposable test admin account cleaned up afterward; residue check confirmed zero `zztest`-prefixed users remained.

## Final Status

Fixed and verified both by directly reproducing the race condition and by re-running the admin's exact reported filter combination live. No backend logic, route, or report computation was touched — this was purely a client-side error/success display-ordering bug in the Preview/Compare modal's shared state-management functions.

---

# Administrator Reports & Analytics — "Reports" Tab: Real Browser Visual/Responsive Audit — 2026-08-26

Requested: make the Reports tab's UI responsive, consistent, and well aligned. Every prior audit of this page in this project was done via curl (API-level) or a hand-mocked Node DOM harness — neither ever actually renders real CSS. This pass used a **real, actual browser** for the first time: Playwright/Puppeteer are not installed in this environment, so a minimal CDP (Chrome DevTools Protocol) driver was written from scratch using Node's native `WebSocket`/`fetch` (no npm install needed) to drive a real headless Chrome instance (isolated profile, separate debugging port, never touching the user's own open Chrome windows) and capture real screenshots at desktop (1440px), tablet (768px), and mobile (375px) widths, plus dark mode. This surfaced one severe, previously-undetected bug that no amount of API or mocked-DOM testing could ever have caught, since it's purely a real-CSS-cascade issue.

## Bug 1 (severe): the Preview/Compare modal's error banner was permanently unhideable — this is the true root cause of the "Something went wrong" report from earlier this session

`<div id="rpm-error" class="alert alert-danger d-flex align-items-center gap-2" style="display:none;">` combined a Bootstrap **display utility class** (`d-flex`) with an inline `style.display` toggle on the *same element*. Bootstrap's `.d-*` display utilities are compiled with `!important` by design. A CSS `!important` rule in a stylesheet always beats a plain (non-`!important`) inline style, so `element.style.display = 'none'` was setting the inline attribute correctly (confirmed: `el.style.display` read back as `"none"`) while the browser's actual **computed** style stayed `display: flex` (confirmed via `getComputedStyle` — `display: flex`, `visibility: visible`, a real non-zero bounding rect) — the element was **always visibly rendered**, permanently, every single time the modal was open, regardless of success or failure. It was never actually possible to hide it via the existing JS.

This fully explains the admin's original "Something went wrong" report from earlier in this session far more completely than the request-sequence-guard fix applied at the time: that fix was a real, additional robustness improvement for genuine overlapping-request races, but it never touched this deeper markup bug, and could not have — no amount of JS state-management logic can override a CSS `!important` rule. The banner was there, showing the default fallback text ("Something went wrong."), on top of every correctly-rendered report, the entire time — confirmed by finding it present in literally every modal screenshot taken during this session's real-browser testing, until fixed.

**Why 3+ prior driven-script "verifications" of this exact modal never caught it**: every earlier verification in this project used a hand-built Node object (`{ style: { display: '' }, ... }`) standing in for `document.getElementById(...)`, not a real DOM element in a real browser — those mocks have no CSS cascade at all, so `style.display = 'none'` always "worked" in the mock regardless of what real Bootstrap CSS would do. This is a hard limitation of that technique that real-browser testing does not share.

**Fix**: moved the `d-flex align-items-center gap-2` layout classes off the element being toggled and onto a new inner wrapper div, leaving `#rpm-error` itself free of any conflicting display-utility class:
```html
<div id="rpm-error" class="alert alert-danger" style="display:none;">
  <div class="d-flex align-items-center gap-2"> ... </div>
</div>
```
Verified via `getComputedStyle` after the fix: `display: none`, zero-size bounding rect — genuinely hidden. Re-screenshotted the full Preview → Compare → Compare Against flow at all three widths and in dark mode: the banner never appears again during normal, successful use. Grepped every other element in the file that's toggled via `.style.display` in JS (`rpm-loading`, `rpm-view-preview`, `rpm-view-compare-select`, `rpm-view-compare-result`, the three `rpm-footer-*` divs, `content-rep`/`content-audit`/`content-insights`, `audit-empty`) — none of the others carry a conflicting `d-*` display-utility class in their own class list, confirming this was an isolated, one-element bug, not a systemic pattern.

## Bug 2: 7 of the 8 quick-export report cards rendered their icon as a wide, full-card-width colored bar instead of a small icon badge

Root cause: Velzon's `.avatar-title` class is `width:100%; height:100%;` by design, meant to fill a fixed-size `.avatar-sm`/`.avatar-xs` wrapper (confirmed against the exact convention used throughout `admin_dashboard.ejs`: `<div class="avatar-sm flex-shrink-0"><span class="avatar-title ...">`). Only the first report card ("Partnership Summary Report") had any wrapper at all (`<div class="flex-shrink-0">`, missing the sizing class but incidentally shrink-wrapping small enough to look right); the other 7 cards had no wrapper div whatsoever, so `.avatar-title`'s `width:100%`/`height:100%` resolved against the full-width flex row instead, rendering as a wide bar. **Fix**: added `<div class="avatar-sm flex-shrink-0">` around every card's icon `<span>`, matching the app-wide convention exactly. All 8 report cards now render identical, correctly-sized circular icon badges.

## Bug 3: Custom Report Builder header title+badge overlapped/cramped on narrow screens

`<div class="card-header border-0 d-flex align-items-center">` (title + "Administrator Analytics" badge) had no `flex-wrap`, so on mobile widths where "Custom Report Builder" wraps to two lines, the badge stayed pinned to the same row, overlapping the second line. The Audit Trail card-header just below it already handles the identical title+extra-content scenario correctly with `d-flex align-items-center flex-wrap gap-2`. **Fix**: added the same `flex-wrap gap-2` to the Custom Report Builder's header, matching the already-established, working pattern elsewhere in this same file. Verified via a cropped screenshot: title and badge now stack cleanly with proper spacing instead of overlapping.

## Bug 4: filter-badge labels illegible in dark mode

The "Applied Filters" badges in Preview (`reportType: Active Partnerships`, `category: All`, etc.) render their label portion with a hardcoded `<strong class="text-dark">`. Against the `bg-secondary-subtle` badge background's dark-mode-recolored (dark) variant, hardcoded `text-dark` produced near-invisible, illegible label text — confirmed via a zoomed-in real dark-mode screenshot showing the labels reduced to a barely-visible smudge, while the value portion (using theme-aware `text-secondary`) remained clearly legible. **Fix**: replaced `text-dark` with Bootstrap 5.3's `text-secondary-emphasis` (confirmed present in the bundled `bootstrap.min.css`, defined via the same `--vz-secondary-text-emphasis` CSS custom property architecture used throughout this Velzon build) — the utility class specifically designed to pair with `-subtle` backgrounds and guarantee contrast in both themes. Re-verified in both dark mode (labels now clearly legible, matching the value's blue tone) and light mode (unchanged, still correctly legible) — two call sites fixed identically (Preview's filter badges and Compare-select's inherited-filter badges).

## Bug 5 (cosmetic): "1 records" instead of "1 record"

The Expiring Soon List card's live count used a hardcoded `' records'` suffix regardless of the number. Fixed with a simple singular/plural check (`count === 1 ? '' : 's'`) applied to all three live-count cards (Active/Expired/Expiring Soon).

## What was reviewed and found already correct (not changed)

- Desktop (1440px), tablet (768px), and mobile (375px) grid behavior for the report cards row and the Custom Report Builder's filter grid — both already use `col-xl-3 col-md-6`, collapsing correctly to 2-per-row on tablet and 1-per-row on mobile.
- The Preview modal's 10-column records table — wrapped in `.table-responsive`, correctly scrolls horizontally within its own container on mobile rather than breaking the page layout (verified via a real scrolled screenshot).
- The 3-column Compare Result layout (`col-lg-4`) — correctly stacks to full-width single-column cards below the `lg` breakpoint on both tablet and mobile, verified via real screenshots at both sizes; button footer wraps cleanly at every width.
- The Compare-select (Compare By/Compare Against) view's 2-column form — clean and well-aligned at all three widths.

## Tests Performed

- **Real headless Chrome, CDP-driven** (no Playwright/Puppeteer available; wrote a from-scratch minimal CDP client using Node's native `WebSocket`): logged in as a disposable Administrator, screenshotted the Reports tab's card grid and Custom Report Builder at 1440/768/375px before and after each fix; opened the real Preview modal via the real `previewCustomReport()` function, screenshotted Preview/Compare-select/Compare-result at all three widths in both light and dark mode; used `getComputedStyle`/`getBoundingClientRect` (not just `.style.display`) to definitively confirm the error banner's true rendered state before and after the fix; used Network/Console domain instrumentation to rule out an actual duplicate-request race as the proximate cause of a confusing intermediate observation (traced to stale browser tabs left open by an earlier debugging step in this same investigation — not a real application bug, corrected by explicitly closing every CDP target after each script run going forward).
- **Full suite**: `npm test` (serial) — **155/155 passed**, zero regressions (all changes this pass were HTML/CSS/class-list only, in `views/administrator/reports.ejs`; no backend route or computation touched).
- Disposable test admin account and the isolated headless Chrome profile both cleaned up afterward; residue check confirmed zero `zztest`-prefixed users remained.

## Final Status

The Reports tab is now visually consistent (uniform icon sizing across every report card, correct dark-mode contrast on filter badges) and responsive (verified, not assumed, at desktop/tablet/mobile via real browser screenshots) across the report cards, Custom Report Builder, Preview, Compare, and Compare Against. The permanently-visible error banner — the true root cause behind the earlier "Something went wrong" report — is fixed and confirmed via computed style, not just inline style. No button, route, backend computation, or the existing Round 2 template/comparison implementation was altered; every change this pass was a targeted HTML/CSS correction.

# RBAC Role Migration: View-Only → Staff — 2026-08-27

## Summary

Full role migration replacing the `View-Only` role with `Staff` across the database, backend RBAC middleware, every API route, every page route, all shared/role-specific views, and the automated test suite. This was explicitly **not** a label rename: View-Only previously had access to only two pages (its own dashboard and a Partnership Request submission form). Staff now has restricted-but-real access to the same major module set Administrator has — Dashboard, Calendar, Monitoring, Requests, Partnership Registry, Document Library, Reports & Analytics (Reports/Audit Trail/Strategic Insights), User Management, Notifications, and Settings — with every destructive/security-sensitive action still enforced Administrator-only at the server, not merely hidden in the UI.

## Architecture decision: reuse, not a parallel system

The existing codebase already had a proven pattern for "a non-Administrator role reusing Administrator's own page templates": Auth. Personnel's `/personnel/*` routes render `administrator/calendar.ejs`, `administrator/lifecycle.ejs`, `administrator/notifications.ejs`, and `administrator/documents.ejs` unmodified, swapping only a `sidebarPartial` local passed into each template's `<%- include('../partials/' + (sidebarPartial || 'sidebar')) %>` line. Staff's entire `/staff/*` route set (new) follows this exact same convention — extended to the three modules Personnel never had (Registry, Reports, User Management) by adding the same conditional include to `views/administrator/reports.ejs`, `views/administrator/partnership_requests.ejs`, and `views/users.ejs` (the only shared templates that didn't already support it). Staff's Dashboard, Settings, and Partnership Request submission pages are new, standalone files under `views/staff/`, mirroring Auth. Personnel's own equivalents (`personnel_dashboard.ejs`, `personnel_settings.ejs`) rather than reusing Administrator's (which carries admin-exclusive content like the Google Calendar org-wide integration tab).

## New/changed backend RBAC (`cirl.js`)

- New `requireStaffAccess` middleware (Administrator OR Staff) — mirrors `requirePersonnel`'s dual-role convention. Gates every new `/staff/*` page route and every API route Staff needed **read** access to.
- `requireViewOnly` and its three `/viewonly/*` routes **removed** — replaced by `requireStaffAccess` and the `/staff/*` route set below.
- `requireUploader`, `OWN_SCOPE_ROLES`, `SELF_UPLOAD_ONLY_ROLES` — added `'Staff'` (Document Library: own-scoped upload/view, matching Auth. Personnel/potential_partner's treatment exactly).
- `requireRequester` — **deliberately not** widened to include Staff. Staff keeps the same conservative Document-Request-submission exclusion View-Only had; Staff's own Partnership Request submission flow (`/staff/request-access`) uses `POST /api/requests`, which was already `requireAuth` (open to any logged-in role) and needed no change.
- `VALID_USER_ROLES`, signup's default role, the Google-login auto-create default role, `CALENDAR_RECIPIENT_ROLES`, `homeForRole()`'s fallback, and the request-notification deep-link helpers (`prLinkForRole`) — all updated from `'View-Only'` to `'Staff'`.
- **Widened to `requireStaffAccess`** (read-only for Staff; every mutation route on the same resource stays `requireAdmin`-only, unchanged): `GET /api/users`, `GET /api/document-requests`, and all nine `GET /api/reports/*` routes (custom preview/pdf/excel, comparison preview/pdf/excel, dimension-values, activitylog pdf/excel).
- **`GET /api/activitylogs` (Audit Trail) — the most privacy-sensitive change.** Previously `requireAdmin`-only and always unfiltered. Now `requireStaffAccess`, with a new `activityLogFilterFor(user)` helper: Administrator gets `{}` (everyone, unchanged); Staff gets `{ email: user.email }`. The same filter is reused by the Activity Log PDF and Excel export routes so all three Audit Trail data sources (JSON/PDF/Excel) can never drift out of sync. `logActivity()` now stamps a new `email` field on every newly-inserted entry to make this filter possible — **entries logged before this change have no `email` field and are left exactly as they were**; this is a legitimate historical record, not migrated or backfilled (58 such entries exist in the live database as of this migration, all with `role: "View-Only"` intact).
- **`GET /api/requests` and `GET /api/document-requests` — a scope-tightening fix discovered mid-implementation.** Both were unscoped (`find({})`) `requireAuth`/`requireAdmin` routes whose only frontend consumer was Administrator's own `partnership_requests.ejs` review page. Granting Staff read access to that same shared page would otherwise have exposed **every other user's submitted request content** (institution proposals, review notes, decision history) to Staff. Fixed by scoping both routes: Administrator sees everything (unchanged); every other role (Staff included) sees only requests they themselves submitted (`submittedByEmail`/`requestedByEmail` matching their own session email) — the same filter `/api/requests/mine` already used, just applied to the shared route too. Verified no other page in the app relied on the previously-unscoped behavior before making this change.

## New Staff-facing surface

- **Routes**: `/staff` → `/staff/dashboard`, `/staff/calendar`, `/staff/lifecycle` (Monitoring), `/staff/requests`, `/staff/request-access`, `/staff/registry`, `/staff/documents`, `/staff/reports`, `/staff/users`, `/staff/notifications`, `/staff/settings` — all `requireStaffAccess`.
- **New views**: `views/partials/sidebar_staff.ejs` (full module sidebar, modeled on the Administrator sidebar with `/staff/*` links); `views/staff/staff_dashboard.ejs`, `views/staff/staff_settings.ejs`, `views/staff/staff_request_access.ejs` (the last one ported verbatim from the removed `views/view_only/viewonly_access_request.ejs`, preserving the exact working OCR-assisted Partnership Request submission flow View-Only had).
- **New API**: `GET/POST /api/staff/profile`, `POST /api/staff/password` — mirror the existing Personnel profile-store routes exactly, keyed to their own `profiles` collection document (`role: 'staff'`).
- **Removed**: `views/view_only/` (both files), `views/partials/sidebar_viewonly.ejs`.

## UI-level restriction (defense in depth — the backend enforces these regardless)

Every shared template Staff now reaches was audited for Administrator-exclusive mutation controls and had them wrapped in a role check, matching the pre-existing `canManageCalendar` pattern already used in `calendar.ejs`:

- `calendar.ejs` — already correctly gated (`canManageCalendar = role === 'Administrator'`); no change needed. Confirmed live: Staff sees the calendar and its events, with no Add/Edit/Delete controls rendered, and a direct `POST /api/calendarevents` call from the browser console returns the `requireAdmin` redirect, not a success.
- `registry.ejs` + `registry-gridjs.init.js` — Add Partnership button and per-row Edit/Approve/Renew/Delete actions now hidden for Staff via a new `CAN_MANAGE_REGISTRY` flag; the read-only "View" detail modal remains available.
- `lifecycle.ejs` + `lifecycle-gridjs.init.js` — Renew/Edit/Delete dropdown items hidden for Staff via a new `CAN_MANAGE_LIFECYCLE` flag; View/Remind remain available.
- `partnership_requests.ejs` — Review/Approve/Reject/Request-More-Info and the Administrator document-upload widget (both Partnership and Document Request detail modals) hidden for Staff via a new `IS_ADMIN` flag; Staff sees a read-only "View" mode plus a new "+ New Partnership Request" button (visible to Staff only) linking to `/staff/request-access`.
- `users.ejs` — Add User button and per-row Edit/Deactivate hidden for Staff (replaced with a "View only" label); the Role Permissions Matrix table's header and every row's fourth column relabeled from View-Only to Staff, with values re-audited to match the real gating above (see table below).

## Updated Role Permissions Matrix (`views/users.ejs`)

| Feature | Administrator | Auth. Personnel | Partner | Staff |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Dashboard | Full | Full | Full | Full |
| Calendar | Full | View Only | View Only | View Only |
| Monitoring | Full | Own Only | Own Only | View Only |
| Registry | Full | No | No | View Only |
| Document Library | Own + Review Attachments | Own Only | Own Only | Own Only |
| User Management | Full | No | No | View Only |
| Partnership Requests | Full (Review/Approve/Reject) | Submit Only | Submit Only | Submit + Own View |
| Document Requests | Full (Review/Fulfill/Reject) | Submit Only | Submit Only | No |
| Notifications | Own Only | Own Only | Own Only | Own Only |
| Reports & Analytics | Full | No | No | View + Own Audit Trail |
| Settings | Full | Full | Full | Full |

## Database migration

Live database `users` collection: 7 accounts with `role: 'View-Only'` migrated to `role: 'Staff'` via a targeted `updateMany({ role: 'View-Only' }, { $set: { role: 'Staff' } })` — only the `role` field touched, every other field (name, email, unit, login, status, password) verified byte-identical before/after. Zero users deleted. Confirmed 0 remaining `View-Only` users post-migration.

**Left unchanged, deliberately** (historical record, not live/authoritative data): 58 `activitylogs` entries with `role: "View-Only"` predating this migration; `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-07-partnership-document-link.md` (a dated planning document); `Database_backup/*.json` (point-in-time export snapshots).

## Tests

- `test/helpers.js` — `createTestUser()`'s default role changed to `'Staff'`.
- `test/auth.test.js`, `test/requests.test.js`, `test/partnerships.test.js`, `test/notifications.test.js`, `test/google-calendar.test.js`, `test/document-library-workflow.test.js` — every `View-Only` identifier/role string renamed to Staff; three tests whose asserted status code was about to become wrong under the new permissions (Document Library folder routes; Reports RBAC ×3) were rewritten to test the correct new behavior (Staff gets 200, not 302) rather than just renamed.
- `test/reports.test.js` — added a dedicated `describe('Audit Trail: Staff sees only their own records (privacy boundary)')` block: two separate Staff accounts each generate a real, distinguishable logged action; each Staff account's `GET /api/activitylogs` (and PDF/Excel exports) is asserted to contain only their own record and none of the other's or Administrator's, with `res.body.every(l => l.email === thisStaffUser.email)` proving it's a genuine server-side filter, not a coincidence; Administrator's own call is asserted to see both. Also added: Auth. Personnel/potential_partner remain fully blocked from the Audit Trail.
- `test/auth.test.js` — added: Staff can reach the read-only `GET /api/users`; Staff cannot `POST /api/users` (still `requireAdmin`); Staff cannot `POST /api/partnerships` (still `requireAdmin`).
- **Full suite: 180/180 passing** (up from 155 at the last recorded baseline — 25 net new/rewritten tests), zero regressions across Administrator, Auth. Personnel, and potential_partner behavior.

## Live verification (real headless Chrome, CDP-driven)

Logged in as disposable Administrator/Staff(x2)/Auth. Personnel/Partner accounts and drove the real running app:
- Staff: every one of the 11 listed module pages loads correctly with the correct title and the new `sidebar_staff` navigation; Calendar shows 0 visible Add-Event controls and a direct `fetch('/api/calendarevents', {method:'POST'})` call returns an opaque redirect (server-enforced rejection, not merely a hidden button); Registry shows 6/6 rows with only the View action (0 Edit, 0 Delete buttons) and the header's Add Partnership button absent; User Management shows 27/27 real users with "View only" in the Actions column on every row, and the migrated former-View-Only accounts correctly display a "Staff" badge; Requests shows a prominent "+ New Partnership Request" button and an empty, correctly own-scoped request list (0 of the several real historical requests in the database belong to this fresh test account); Reports/Audit Trail/Strategic Insights tabs all load.
- Direct unauthorized URL access: Staff hitting `/dashboard`, `/admin/settings`, `/registry`, `/users`, `/reports`, `/notifications`, `/documents`, `/personnel/dashboard`, and `/partner/dashboard` is redirected to `/staff/dashboard` every time (302, not a crash or partial render).
- Administrator: unaffected — `/dashboard`, `/registry` (Add Partnership button still present), `/users` (Add User still present) all behave exactly as before; Administrator's own `GET /api/activitylogs` call returns every historical record (2395, including legacy `View-Only`-role entries) — confirming the Staff-scoping change never touches the Administrator path.
- Auth. Personnel: unaffected — still lands on `/personnel/dashboard`, still redirected away from `/reports`.
- Partner: unaffected — still lands on `/partner/dashboard`.
- Logout works correctly for the migrated Staff role, returning to `/`.
- All disposable test accounts and the isolated headless Chrome profile cleaned up afterward; residue check confirmed 0 remaining `zztest`-prefixed users.

## Known limitations

- Staff's Dashboard and DSS insight cards reuse the same pre-existing hardcoded/demo data (fake "Expiring Partnerships" table rows, fake top-partner-country list) that Auth. Personnel's own dashboard has always shown — this is pre-existing behavior copied verbatim from `personnel_dashboard.ejs`, not something this migration introduced or was asked to fix.
- Staff cannot submit Document Requests (only Partnership Requests) — a deliberate, conservative choice preserving View-Only's exact prior capability rather than expanding scope beyond what was explicitly requested.

## Administrator/Staff Full-Parity RBAC Revision — 2026-08-27

A follow-up to the View-Only → Staff migration above. The original migration gave Staff its own restricted, mostly-read-only slice of each module. This revision reverses that scope decision on explicit request: **Administrator and Staff now share full authority over almost every module**, using the exact same underlying templates, routes, and APIs — not parallel Staff-only implementations — with two deliberately preserved exceptions (Calendar, Admin Settings) and one deliberately preserved security boundary inside User Management (Administrator accounts).

Earlier the same day, a separate, narrower request had already gone the other way — removing Staff's ability to *submit* new Partnership/Document Requests (the "+ New Partnership Request" button and `staff_request_access.ejs` were deleted, and the submission-side routes were locked to `requireRequester`, excluding Staff). That change is **not reversed** by this revision: Staff still never submits a request. What changed here is the *reviewer* side — Staff now reviews/approves/rejects every request (not just their own), matching Administrator exactly.

### Permission matrix (final)

| Module | Administrator | Staff |
|---|---|---|
| Dashboard | Full | Full — same template |
| Monitoring (Lifecycle) | Full | Full — same template, same CRUD |
| Requests (Partnership + Document) | Manage all | Manage all — same template, same CRUD |
| Partnership Registry | Full CRUD | Full CRUD — same template |
| Document Library | Own + Review Attachments | Own + Review Attachments (own-scoped browsing, full reviewer attachment access) |
| Reports & Analytics | Full | Full, **except** Audit Trail = own records only |
| Notifications | Own-scoped | Own-scoped + Partnership/Document Request reviewer broadcasts |
| User Management | Full | Full, **except** cannot touch Administrator accounts or grant the Administrator role |
| Calendar | Full (create/edit/delete) | View only (unchanged) |
| Admin Settings (Integrations) | Full | None (unchanged — Staff keeps its own restricted Settings page) |

### Shared-implementation architecture

No new Staff-only page was created for any of the modules above except where one already existed for a genuinely different reason (`staff_settings.ejs` — restricted account settings, no Integrations tab). Every `/staff/*` route added or reused in the original migration already rendered the Administrator template with `sidebarPartial: 'sidebar_staff'`; this revision widened the *backend* gating and role-conditional flags behind those same templates rather than forking new views:

- **Dashboard**: `staff_dashboard.ejs` (a full duplicate that had drifted onto disk outside this session's control) was deleted outright. `GET /staff/dashboard` now renders `administrator/admin_dashboard.ejs` directly, exactly like every other shared module. `admin_dashboard.ejs` gained a `sidebarPartial` conditional include and an `isStaffRole` flag that redirects its `/lifecycle`, `/reports`, and `/registry` quick-action links to their `/staff/*` equivalents.
- **Monitoring / Registry**: `CAN_MANAGE_LIFECYCLE` (`lifecycle.ejs`) and `CAN_MANAGE_REGISTRY` (`registry.ejs`) both changed from `role === 'Administrator'` to `['Administrator','Staff'].includes(role)`. Backing routes `POST/PATCH/DELETE /api/partnerships` changed from `requireAdmin` to `requireStaffAccess`.
- **Requests**: `partnership_requests.ejs`'s `IS_ADMIN` flag (kept under that name for a minimal diff) now evaluates true for Staff too, exposing the same Approve/Reject controls and document-upload widget. Backing routes widened to `requireStaffAccess`: `PATCH /api/requests/:id`, `POST /api/requests/:id/documents`, `PATCH /api/document-requests/:id`, `POST /api/document-requests/:id/documents`. `GET /api/requests` and `GET /api/document-requests` now return every record to a new `REQUEST_REVIEWER_ROLES = ['Administrator','Staff']` set (previously Administrator-only). The Document Request print/PDF routes (`/document-requests/:id/print`, `/api/document-requests/:id/pdf`) moved from `requireRequester` to `requireAuth` + widened `canAccessDocumentRequest()` (now `REQUEST_REVIEWER_ROLES.includes(role) || own`).
- **Document Library**: no route changes were needed — `GET /api/documents` was already own-scoped identically for every role in `OWN_SCOPE_ROLES` including Administrator, so Staff was already at full functional parity there. The one real gap: `GET /uploads/documents/:filename` (the file-serving route used to open a *requester's* attachment during review) exempted only Administrator from its ownership check via `SELF_UPLOAD_ONLY_ROLES`. Staff was removed from `SELF_UPLOAD_ONLY_ROLES`, giving it the same reviewer exemption.
- **Reports & Analytics**: already fully shared and fully widened to `requireStaffAccess` in the original migration (Custom Report Builder, comparisons, PDF/Excel export, DSS/Strategic Insights, dimension-values). No changes needed here beyond re-verifying the Audit Trail exception still holds (it does — see below).
- **Notifications**: `administrator/notifications.ejs` (now also Staff's page) calls `GET /api/notifications`, which was `requirePersonnel`-gated (Administrator + Auth. Personnel only) — **a real, previously-latent bug**: Staff's Notifications page has been silently broken (its one data-loading call always redirected) ever since `/staff/notifications` was created in the original migration, because nothing had exercised it end-to-end until this revision's Playwright pass caught it. Fixed by opening the route to `requireAuth`, safe because the query was already strictly scoped to `targetEmail: req.session.user.email` regardless of role. Separately, the Partnership/Document Request "new submission" notification broadcast (previously `db.collection('users').find({role:'Administrator'})`) now targets `REQUEST_REVIEWER_ROLES`, so Staff is notified of new submissions exactly like Administrator. A second small pre-existing gap fixed in passing: `CALENDAR_LINK_BY_ROLE` had no `'Staff'` entry, so a Staff recipient's calendar-event notification linked to the Administrator-only `/calendar` page instead of `/staff/calendar`.
- **User Management**: `GET/POST/PATCH/DELETE /api/users` all widened to `requireStaffAccess`. Three explicit privilege-escalation guards were added (not just UI hiding): `POST /api/users` rejects `role: 'Administrator'` from a Staff caller (403); `PATCH /api/users/:id` rejects any edit to a target whose *current* role is Administrator, and rejects setting `role: 'Administrator'` on anyone, from a Staff caller (403); `DELETE /api/users/:id` rejects deleting a target whose role is Administrator, from a Staff caller (403). `users.ejs` renamed its `IS_ADMIN` flag to `CAN_MANAGE_USERS` (now true for Staff too), added a `CURRENT_ROLE` flag, hid the "Administrator" option from the role `<select>` for a Staff actor, and renders "Protected" instead of Edit/Deactivate buttons on any row whose role is Administrator when the viewer is Staff.
- **Admin Settings**: unchanged. Staff still uses the separate, pre-existing `staff_settings.ejs` (Personal Details + Change Password only) — the Integrations tab (org-wide Google Calendar OAuth connection) remains exclusively on `admin_settings.ejs`, reachable only via the `requireAdmin`-gated `/admin/settings` and `/api/google-calendar/*` routes. This is the one module where a separate Staff page was deliberately kept, because the excluded content (system-level OAuth configuration) has no per-user equivalent to fall back to — hiding a tab would still leave the underlying routes reachable by URL, so a distinct restricted page was the correct choice here, not an oversight.
- **Calendar**: unchanged — explicitly out of scope for this revision. Staff remains view-only; `canManageCalendar` in `calendar.ejs` and the `requireAdmin` gate on `POST/PATCH/DELETE /api/calendarevents` were not touched.

### Security decisions and preserved restrictions

- **Privilege escalation into Administrator territory is blocked server-side**, not just hidden in the UI: Staff cannot create, edit, or delete an Administrator account, and cannot grant the Administrator role to any account (including its own). Verified by direct API calls bypassing the UI entirely (`test/auth.test.js`), not just by the button being absent.
- **Staff never submits a request** (Partnership or Document) — this was a separate, explicit decision made earlier the same day and is unaffected by this revision. Staff's authority is entirely on the reviewer side.
- **Audit Trail remains strictly own-scoped for Staff** at the database query level (`activityLogFilterFor`), across the UI, the API, search/filters, pagination, and both PDF and Excel export — re-verified end-to-end in this revision (see Testing below), not just carried over as an assumption from the original migration.
- **Google Calendar integration configuration remains Administrator-only** — connecting/disconnecting the org's single Google account is system-level configuration, not delegated to Staff.
- **Calendar event creation/editing/deletion remains Administrator-only** — explicitly out of scope for this revision per the request.

### Files changed (in addition to the original migration's files)

- `cirl.js` — `REQUEST_REVIEWER_ROLES` constant added; `GET/PATCH /api/requests`, `POST /api/requests/:id/documents`, `GET/PATCH /api/document-requests`, `POST /api/document-requests/:id/documents`, `/document-requests/:id/print`, `/api/document-requests/:id/pdf`, `POST/PATCH/DELETE /api/partnerships`, `GET/POST/PATCH/DELETE /api/users`, `GET /api/notifications` all re-gated; `canAccessDocumentRequest()`, `SELF_UPLOAD_ONLY_ROLES`, `CALENDAR_LINK_BY_ROLE` updated; three new privilege-escalation checks added inside the Users routes; `GET /staff/dashboard` now renders `administrator/admin_dashboard`.
- `views/administrator/admin_dashboard.ejs` — `sidebarPartial` support, `isStaffRole`-aware links.
- `views/staff/staff_dashboard.ejs` — **deleted** (duplicate of admin_dashboard.ejs).
- `views/administrator/partnership_requests.ejs` — `IS_ADMIN` now true for Staff; "Supporting Documents (Administrator)" label generalized to "(Reviewer)".
- `views/administrator/registry.ejs`, `views/administrator/lifecycle.ejs` — `CAN_MANAGE_REGISTRY`/`CAN_MANAGE_LIFECYCLE` now true for Staff.
- `views/users.ejs` — `IS_ADMIN` → `CAN_MANAGE_USERS` (+ new `CURRENT_ROLE`), role dropdown hides "Administrator" for Staff, "Protected" row state for Administrator-role targets, RBAC matrix table and its audit comment fully rewritten to the new matrix above.
- `test/auth.test.js` — rewrote the three Staff-restriction tests into full-parity tests; added three new privilege-escalation tests (cannot create/edit-into/grant Administrator).
- `test/partnerships.test.js` — rewrote "Staff cannot create a partnership" into "Staff CAN create, edit, and delete" (full CRUD parity).
- `test/notifications.test.js` — rewrote the Staff-blocked assertion on `GET /api/notifications` into an own-scoped-200 assertion.
- `test/requests.test.js` — added a `describe` block proving Staff sees and can approve a request submitted by a third party it never interacted with (real reviewer access, not an ownership coincidence).
- `test/document-request-print.test.js` — added Staff print/PDF/Under-Review-transition parity tests.

### Testing

- **Full Jest suite: 187/187 passing** (up from 183 immediately prior — 4 rewritten + 8 net-new tests), zero regressions across Administrator, Auth. Personnel, potential_partner, and Staff's already-existing behavior.
- Every touched EJS template was rendered standalone (`ejs.renderFile`) for both an Administrator and a Staff `user` object before any server was involved, catching template errors cheaply.
- Direct API-bypass verification (not just UI): every mutation, export, and filtering endpoint listed above was hit directly with `curl`/Playwright's `APIRequestContext`, independent of any button being visible.

### Playwright verification (real Chromium, installed this session)

`@playwright/test` and Chromium were not previously installed in this project; both were installed specifically to satisfy this task's requirement to use real browser automation. Five real-browser/API-hybrid tests were run against a live local server (disposable `zztest_pw_*` accounts, `NODE_ENV=test` only to bypass the login rate limiter during the test run — reverted to normal afterward):

1. **Administrator full checklist** — logs in, confirms landing on `/dashboard`, and visits/verifies Monitoring, Requests, Registry (Add Partnership visible), Document Library, Reports, User Management (Add User visible), and Admin Settings (Integrations tab present).
2. **Staff full checklist** — logs in, confirms landing on `/staff/dashboard`, and confirms: the Dashboard renders the same template (shared `#top-countries-list` element present); Monitoring, Requests (submission button confirmed absent), Registry (Add Partnership now visible — full parity), Document Library, and Reports all load; User Management shows Add User; the restricted Settings page has no Integrations tab; `GET /api/notifications` returns 200 (the latent bug fixed above).
3. **Privilege-escalation UI guard** — logs in as Staff, confirms the Administrator account's row in User Management renders "Protected" with no edit action wired to it, while other rows remain editable.
4. **Audit Trail three-account test** — Administrator, Staff A, and Staff B each perform one real, distinguishable action (a Registry "Add Partnership," doubling as a live Registry-parity check) via authenticated API calls sharing the real browser's session cookies; `GET /api/activitylogs` is then checked for all three roles: Administrator sees all three records, Staff A sees only its own (not Staff B's, not Administrator's), Staff B sees only its own — confirmed both via direct API call and via the same call made from Staff A's live browser session on the actual Reports page.
5. **Notification reviewer test** — a disposable potential_partner account submits a real Partnership Request; Staff A's `GET /api/notifications` is confirmed to contain the resulting "new submission" alert, proving the reviewer-broadcast widening actually reaches Staff end-to-end, not just in the route's source code.

All 5 Playwright tests passed. All disposable accounts and every `pwtest`-tagged partnership/request/notification/activity-log record were deleted immediately after the run; a residue check against the `zztest_pw_*`/`pwtest` prefixes confirmed zero remaining rows.

### Known limitations / deliberate non-goals

- Calendar and Admin Settings' Integrations tab remain Administrator-only, unchanged — explicitly out of scope for this revision.
- Staff still cannot submit a new Partnership or Document Request — a separate, explicit decision from earlier the same day, not reversed here.
- The Dashboard's "Top Partner Countries" and DSS insight cards still reflect whatever live-data behavior `admin_dashboard.ejs` already had (see the original migration's Known Limitations above) — unrelated to and unaffected by this revision.

## Partnership Request Draft / Document Collaboration Workflow — 2026-08-27

Replaces the old single-shot "Supporting Document (Submitter/Reviewer)" + "Review Decision Notes" + "Request More Info" review UI with a real multi-version draft/document collaboration timeline that both sides of a Partnership Request — the requester (Auth. Personnel/potential_partner) and the reviewers (Administrator/Staff, full parity as of the earlier 2026-08-27 revision) — can add to, indefinitely, with a note attached to every version.

### What was removed, and why

- **"Supporting Document (from submitter)"** and **"Supporting Documents (Reviewer)"** — two separate, disconnected sections in the review modal, one showing the original `attachmentLink` with no history, one showing a flat undecorated list of admin-uploaded files with no notes or timestamps. Replaced by a single **"View Draft"** button opening one unified, chronological timeline that shows both.
- **"Review Decision Notes"** textarea and the **"Request More Info"** button — the free-text "ask for more info" workflow is superseded by the concrete draft-upload-with-note mechanism: a reviewer who wants something changed now uploads a marked-up/annotated version with an explanatory note instead of writing a detached comment and bouncing the status. Approve/Reject remain exactly as before; PATCH `/api/requests/:id` still accepts an optional `notes` field server-side (nothing depended on removing it), but the UI no longer sends one.

### Data model (reused, not replaced)

No new collection and no new top-level field. The existing `requests.supportingDocuments` array — already a `$push`-only, never-replaced history — was enriched per entry from `{ documentId, fileLink, originalFilename, uploadedAt, uploadedBy }` to also include `uploadedByEmail`, `uploaderRole`, `note`, `fileType` (mimetype), and `fileSize`. The requester's original `attachmentLink` (already a request field) is treated as the timeline's first, synthetic "Requester — Initial submission" entry client-side; it was never given its own metadata object and still isn't — its existing `date`/`requestedBy` fields are reused for display, avoiding a schema migration for historical requests. Old `supportingDocuments` entries predating this change (missing the new fields) render safely with sensible fallbacks (`uploaderRole` defaults to "Reviewer").

### Backend changes

- **`POST /api/requests/:id/documents`** (`cirl.js`) — the single route both sides now use:
  - Gate widened from `requireStaffAccess` to `requireAuth`, with an explicit in-handler authorization check: allowed if the caller's role is in `REQUEST_REVIEWER_ROLES` (Administrator/Staff) **or** the caller is the request's own `submittedByEmail`. Anyone else gets 403 — enforced server-side, not just a hidden button.
  - New guard: uploads are rejected (400) once a request's status is `Approved`, `Rejected`, or `Withdrawn` — a decided request is read-only history from then on.
  - Accepts an optional `note` field (multipart form field alongside the file), trimmed and capped at 1000 chars.
  - Stores the richer `docRecord` described above; still `$push`s (never `$set`s over) `supportingDocuments`.
  - If the request was `Pending`, a new upload from either side bumps it to `Under Review` — reusing the existing status rather than inventing a new one (per the explicit "don't invent unnecessary statuses" instruction); `Under Review` already meant "still undecided" for the Approve/Reject guard, so this required no other code changes.
  - Response now includes the full updated request document (`{ success, documentId, fileLink, request }`), so the frontend can refresh its whole timeline from one round-trip instead of guessing the shape of what just got appended.
  - Notification direction now depends on who uploaded: a reviewer's upload notifies the requester (unchanged); the requester's own upload now notifies every reviewer (`REQUEST_REVIEWER_ROLES`), the same broadcast set used for a brand-new submission — previously this route couldn't be reached by a requester at all.
  - `logActivity` now includes the note text in the audit description when present.
- No other backend route changed. `GET /api/requests` and `GET /api/requests/mine` already returned `supportingDocuments`/`attachmentLink` in full — no new read endpoint was needed. File serving (`GET /uploads/documents/:filename`) already exempted both reviewer roles and enforced true-owner-only access for everyone else (from the earlier full-parity revision) — zero changes needed there either, since every version, regardless of who uploaded it, is archived under the requester's own email.

### RBAC

| Action | Administrator | Staff | Auth. Personnel / Partner |
|---|---|---|---|
| View any request's draft history | ✅ | ✅ | Own requests only |
| Upload a new version | ✅ (any request) | ✅ (any request) | ✅ (own request only) |
| Upload once Approved/Rejected/Withdrawn | ❌ (blocked for everyone) | ❌ | ❌ |
| Upload to another user's request | — | — | ❌ (403, server-enforced) |

Enforced entirely inside the route handler (ownership/role check + terminal-status check), not via the route's outer middleware alone and not via frontend hiding — verified by direct API calls bypassing the UI (see Testing below).

### UI changes

- **`views/administrator/partnership_requests.ejs`** (shared Administrator/Staff page): removed the two old Supporting Document sections, the Review Decision Notes textarea, and the Request More Info button; added a "View Draft" summary card + button in the request detail view, and a new `#pr-draft-modal` (same-page Bootstrap modal, never navigates away) rendering the full chronological timeline with per-version uploader name + role badge, timestamp, note, an inline Preview toggle (renders an `<iframe>` for PDFs, an `<img>` for images, directly inside the timeline card — no new tab), a Download link, and an upload form (file + note) at the bottom that's hidden with an explanatory message once the request is decided.
- **`views/auth. personnel/personnel_monitoring.ejs`** and **`views/potential_partner/partner_monitoring.ejs`**: added a "Draft" button (per row, and in Personnel's existing request-details modal footer) opening the identical same-page `#pr-draft-modal` timeline, scoped to `myRequests` (already own-scoped by `GET /api/requests/mine`), with the same upload-with-note capability. No new page, no navigation away from Monitoring.
- All new UI reuses the existing Bootstrap modal, badge, and button conventions already on each page; no new CSS framework or component library was introduced. Dark mode is inherited automatically (no hardcoded light-only colors were introduced — borders/text use existing utility classes and CSS variables).

### Notifications

Both directions now flow through the existing `notifyUsers()` helper — no new notification architecture:
- Reviewer uploads a version → requester is notified ("New draft uploaded…") — pre-existing behavior, unchanged.
- Requester uploads a revision → every reviewer (Administrator + Staff) is notified ("Revised draft uploaded…") — new, since a requester previously couldn't reach this route at all.
- Existing Partnership Request and Document Request notifications (submission, Approve/Reject/Fulfill) are untouched.

### Audit Trail

Every upload — from either side — is still logged via the existing `logActivity()` call on this route, now with the note text folded into the description, so Staff's own-scoped Audit Trail (from the earlier full-parity revision) correctly shows their own draft uploads and nothing else's.

### Testing

- **New Jest suite**: `test/request-draft-collaboration.test.js` (6 tests) — reviewer upload with full metadata + status transition to Under Review; requester (owner) upload appended (not replacing) the reviewer's version; a different user blocked from uploading to someone else's request (403); reviewer-upload → requester notification and requester-upload → reviewer notification both actually land in the `notifications` collection; the note text appears in `activitylogs`; uploads rejected once the request is Approved.
- **Full Jest suite: 193/193 passing** (up from 187 immediately prior), zero regressions.
- **Playwright** (5 new tests, real Chromium): Administrator's full View Draft + upload + inline image-preview workflow, with the old Review Decision Notes/Request More Info text confirmed absent from the rendered modal HTML (not just hidden); Staff's identical workflow with a PDF upload; Auth. Personnel opening the Draft modal from Monitoring, seeing the Administrator's earlier upload in the same timeline, uploading their own revision, and being rejected (403) when attempting a direct API upload to the Partner's request; the mirrored Partner test; a dedicated security test confirming two unrelated Personnel/Partner accounts are both blocked (403) from either request via direct API calls. All 5 passed; all disposable accounts, requests, documents, notifications, and activity-log entries were deleted afterward with a residue check confirming zero remaining rows.
- **Regression re-confirmed** for Partnership Registry, Document Library, Notifications, Activity Logs, Monitoring, Reports, Calendar, Google Calendar integration, and existing authentication/RBAC — all covered by the same 193-test full suite run, no separate re-testing needed since nothing outside the routes/views listed above was touched.

### Files changed

- `cirl.js` — `POST /api/requests/:id/documents` rewritten (gate, ownership/status guards, note field, richer `docRecord`, bidirectional notifications, response shape).
- `views/administrator/partnership_requests.ejs` — old review-UI sections removed; View Draft button, `#pr-draft-modal`, and its rendering/upload JS added; `resolvePR` simplified (no longer reads a removed textarea).
- `views/auth. personnel/personnel_monitoring.ejs`, `views/potential_partner/partner_monitoring.ejs` — Draft button, `#pr-draft-modal`, and matching JS added.
- `test/request-draft-collaboration.test.js` — new.

### Known limitations / deliberate non-goals

- Document Requests (the separate CSPC-F-CIRL-04 form workflow) were explicitly out of scope — the spec named Partnership Requests only; their existing single-file-per-decision upload widget is untouched.
- The synthetic "Requester — Initial submission" timeline entry has no independently stored timestamp (it reuses the request's existing `date` field, a day-level string, not a full timestamp) — a pre-existing limitation of the `attachmentLink` field this feature didn't need to change, since introducing one would have required a data migration for no functional benefit (the field already had no time-of-day precision before today).
- Preview for "other" file types (anything outside PDF/JPG/JPEG/PNG — none of which are actually uploadable here, since `verifyMagicBytes` already restricts to those three) shows a "Preview not available" message with a Download action, per spec; the upload input itself has always been restricted to PDF/JPG/JPEG/PNG.

## Document Request Draft / Document Collaboration Workflow — 2026-08-29

The Document Request analog of the Partnership Request draft/document collaboration workflow above (2026-08-27) — same architecture, same shared frontend helpers, ported to `documentrequests` with the one structural difference Document Requests actually have: no `attachmentLink`-style file at submission time, so the timeline's first entry is a text-only "Request submitted" event rather than a file version.

### What was removed, and why

- **"Supporting Documents (Administrator)"** list + its single-file upload widget (`dr-doc-file`, Administrator-only) in the Document Request review modal — replaced by the same **"View Draft"** button/modal pattern used for Partnership Requests, now open to both reviewer roles and the requester.
- **"Admin Remark (Optional)"** textarea — the free-text decision note is superseded by the per-upload note in the new draft timeline, exactly like Partnership Requests' "Review Decision Notes" removal.
- **Not removed, deliberately**: the **Released By / Received By** fields. These are unrelated to the "ask for more info" pattern — they exist solely to populate the official CSPC-F-CIRL-04 printable form's signature lines when a request is marked Fulfilled (confirmed by reading `views/print/document_request_print.ejs`, which renders `decidedBy`/`releasedBy`/`receivedBy` but never `remark`). Removing them would have broken a real compliance document for no reason the spec asked for, so they stay exactly where they were, gated by the same `canAct` visibility they already had.
- Document Requests never had a "Request More Info" button in the first place (confirmed by inspecting `openDRDetail()`'s footer before making any change) — nothing to remove there.

### Data model (reused, not replaced)

Same treatment as Partnership Requests: `documentrequests.supportingDocuments` — already a `$push`-only history — enriched per entry with `uploadedByEmail`, `uploaderRole`, `note`, `fileType`, `fileSize`, alongside the existing `documentId`, `fileLink`, `originalFilename`, `uploadedAt`, `uploadedBy`. No new collection, no new top-level field. The timeline's synthetic first entry ("Request submitted") is not stored anywhere new — it's derived client-side from the request's own existing `submittedAt`/`date`/`requestedBy` fields.

### Backend changes

- **`POST /api/document-requests/:id/documents`** (`cirl.js`) — rewritten to mirror `POST /api/requests/:id/documents` exactly:
  - Gate widened from `requireStaffAccess` to `requireAuth`, with an in-handler check: allowed if the caller's role is in `REQUEST_REVIEWER_ROLES` (Administrator/Staff) **or** the caller is the request's own `requestedByEmail`. Anyone else gets 403.
  - New guard: uploads rejected (400) once status is `Fulfilled` or `Rejected` (Document Requests have no `Withdrawn`/cancelled-but-kept state — a self-cancel while Pending deletes the record outright via the existing `DELETE /api/document-requests/:id`, unaffected by this change).
  - Accepts an optional `note` field (multipart, trimmed, capped at 1000 chars), stored on the docRecord.
  - If the request was `Pending`, a new upload from either side bumps it to `Under Review` — the existing status, not a new one.
  - Response now includes the full updated request (`{ success, documentId, fileLink, request }`).
  - Notification direction depends on uploader: a reviewer's upload still notifies the requester (unchanged); the requester's own upload now notifies every reviewer (`REQUEST_REVIEWER_ROLES`) — new, since a requester previously couldn't reach this route.
  - `logActivity` includes the note text when present.
- No other Document Request route changed. `GET /api/document-requests` / `GET /api/document-requests/mine` already returned `supportingDocuments` in full. File serving (`GET /uploads/documents/:filename`) already exempted both reviewer roles and enforced true-owner-only access for everyone else — zero changes needed, since every version is archived under the requester's own email regardless of who uploaded it.

### RBAC

Identical shape to the Partnership Request table: Administrator/Staff can view and upload to any Document Request; Auth. Personnel/potential_partner can view and upload only to their own; uploads are blocked for everyone once `Fulfilled`/`Rejected`; a different user's request is unreachable (403), enforced inside the route handler, not just the outer middleware.

### UI changes
